## Phase 4 — Dashboard Home View

**Objective:** Build the missing dashboard-home/overview screen.

**Reason this phase is required:** The app currently opens straight into the Generate Report wizard — there is no greeting, no metrics overview, no FLACRON ENGINE usage panel (Item 18, `NOT IMPLEMENTED`).

**Scope:** Frontend, new `activeView` (or route) in `Dashboard.jsx`, plus small backend aggregation additions for metrics not currently computed (Photos Analyzed total, Reports Awaiting Review count).

**Exact tasks:**
1. Add a new default `activeView === 'home'` (or a real `/dashboard` landing state) with a greeting header ("Good morning/afternoon/evening, [First Name]").
2. Add 4 metric cards: Reports This Month, Photos Analyzed, Reports Awaiting Review, Completed Reports — wire to real data (some may need a small new backend aggregation endpoint).
3. Add a Recent Reports table on this home view (reuse/extend the existing reports-list component) with the full spec column set (Claim #, Insured, Report Type, Photos, Status, Updated, Created By, Actions).
4. Add a FLACRON ENGINE usage panel (photos processed this billing period, reports generated, storage used) — storage-used may need a new computed field if not already tracked.
5. Make "Generate Report" the clear primary CTA on this view (already satisfied elsewhere in nav — confirm it's also here).

**Files/components likely affected:** `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`, possibly a new small backend endpoint (e.g. `GET /api/reports/dashboard-summary`) in `backend/routes/reports.js`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None blocking, but pairs naturally with Phase 15 (Analytics) — do not duplicate metric-computation logic between the two; consider a shared summary service if scope overlaps once both are scoped.

**Expected output:** A real landing screen when a user opens the dashboard.

**Acceptance criteria:** Metrics reflect real Firestore data (verified against manual counts); table matches spec columns; page loads without a wizard flash-then-redirect.

**Testing/verification steps:** Load dashboard as a user with a mix of draft/finalized/archived reports; confirm counts match expectations; test empty-state (new user, zero reports).

**Risks/considerations:** "Storage used" may require a new tracking mechanism if not already computed anywhere — confirm feasibility before committing to showing it, or mark clearly as "coming soon" rather than fabricating a number (Golden Rule #1).

**Status:** ✅ Completed 2026-08-15

**What shipped:**
- `Dashboard.jsx` now opens on a new `activeView === 'home'` overview (default state changed from `'generate'` to `'home'`), with `'home'` added as the first sidebar nav entry.
- Time-based greeting ("Good morning/afternoon/evening, [First Name]") — `firstName` falls back to splitting `displayName` for pre-Phase-3 accounts that lack the structured field.
- 4 metric cards: Reports This Month (from existing `userProfile.reportsThisMonth`/tier limit — not duplicated server-side), Photos Analyzed, Awaiting Review, Completed Reports (all three from the new endpoint below). Each independently shows a loading skeleton or an "Unavailable" state on fetch failure — never a stale/zero placeholder presented as real.
- Recent Reports table (5 most recent, own `fetchRecentReports`/state — deliberately not sharing the paginated "My Reports" tab's state) with the full spec'd column set, reusing the existing `StatusBadge` and open-report interaction pattern. Has its own loading/error+Retry/empty ("No reports yet" + CTA) states.
- Flacron Engine Usage panel: photos processed + reports generated this month from real data; **storage used explicitly renders "Not yet available"** — no per-file/per-report byte accounting exists anywhere in this codebase (grepped `storageUsed`/`bytesUsed`/`totalStorage` — zero matches), so Golden Rule #1 required reporting the limitation rather than computing or guessing a number.
- Primary "Generate Report" CTA in the header and inside the empty-state.
- Backend: new `GET /api/reports/dashboard-summary` (`backend/routes/reports.js`, added right after `/ai-status`, before any `/:id` route) — returns `reportsAwaitingReview`/`reportsCompleted` via Firestore `.count()` and `photosAnalyzed` via a `.select('imageCount')` projection summed in Node. Deliberately minimal (3 numbers) so it doesn't grow into a duplicate of Phase 15's future Analytics work.

**Bug found and fixed during live verification:** the endpoint originally used Firestore's native `.aggregate({ sum('imageCount') })` chained onto the `userId` equality filter. Live testing against a real Firestore project surfaced `9 FAILED_PRECONDITION: The query requires an index` — summing a field alongside an equality filter needs a composite index that isn't provisioned in this project (no `firestore.indexes.json`/`firebase.json` exists in the repo at all, so index management here is ad hoc, not CLI-managed). Rather than take on a Firebase-console/CLI index-deployment step mid-phase, switched to a `.select('imageCount').get()` projection scoped to the requesting user's own reports, summed in JS — avoids the composite-index dependency entirely while staying cheap (single field, one user's docs only, not a full-collection scan).

**Live verification (Playwright, both dev servers started fresh/tree-killed per established process-hygiene practice):**
- Populated test account (5 seeded reports: 2 draft, 2 finalized, 1 archived, 48 total `imageCount`) — all 4 metric cards, the Recent Reports table, and the usage panel matched the seeded Firestore records exactly (Reports This Month 4/50, Photos Analyzed 48, Awaiting Review 2, Completed 2). Refresh persisted correctly; desktop (1440×900) and mobile (390×844) both rendered correctly; zero console errors in a clean (non-rate-limited) run.
- New/empty test account (0 reports) — all metrics correctly showed 0, Recent Reports showed the "No reports yet — Generate your first inspection report to get started." empty state with a CTA, usage panel showed 0/0 and "Not yet available" for storage. Zero console errors in a clean run.
- Network-failure path was incidentally exercised (see below) and confirmed working as designed: metric cards render "Unavailable", the table renders "We couldn't load your reports" with a Retry button — no infinite skeletons, no crash.
- Both temporary QA accounts and their seeded report docs were deleted from Firestore after verification.

**Test-harness artifact (not a Phase 4 bug):** repeated back-to-back automated logins across 4 account/viewport combinations tripped the pre-existing global `express-rate-limit` (100 req/15 min/IP, `backend/server.js`) partway through, producing intermittent 429s and (as a direct consequence) the summary/recent-reports error states described above. Confirmed by restarting the backend (which resets the in-memory limiter) and re-running in isolation — zero 429s, zero console errors. Real single-user usage would not approach 100 requests in 15 minutes from this view.

**Flagged, then fixed on explicit follow-up request (2026-08-15, same day):**
- The mobile FAB overlap: rather than reposition/restyle the pre-existing FAB itself (risking a regression to its established look/position across every view), added a single `<div className="h-20 md:hidden" aria-hidden="true" />` spacer right before `</main>` — i.e. after the shared `<AnimatePresence>` that wraps every `activeView`. This guarantees every mobile view always has enough scrollable room below its real content for the fixed FAB to rest over blank space, not content, regardless of which view is active. Chosen over a per-view fix specifically because it's a single, minimal, additive change that cannot alter any existing view's content or behavior. Re-verified via Playwright at true bottom-of-scroll on both the new Dashboard-home view and the pre-existing "My Reports" view — FAB now clears real content on both, zero console errors, `npm run lint`/`build` (frontend) still 0 errors/clean.
- The stale hardcoded gradient hex: replaced `style={{ background: 'linear-gradient(135deg, #f97316 0%, #fb923c 50%, #fbbf24 100%)' }}` with `className="... bg-gradient-to-br from-brand-500 via-brand-400 to-amber-400"` — the same brand-token gradient family already used twice elsewhere in this exact file (the avatar-initial circles, `from-brand-500 to-amber-500`), so this isn't a new pattern, just applying the existing one to the one spot Phase 2 missed. Visually re-verified via screenshot: same diagonal orange-to-amber banner look, now built from real tokens instead of generic Tailwind hex.
- Both fixes are scoped to `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` only, touch no other file, and were verified not to regress backend tests (64/64 unaffected, as expected for a frontend-only change) or frontend lint/build.

**Flagged, NOT fixed (out of Phase 4 scope, pre-existing):**
- No dedicated backend unit test was written for `/dashboard-summary` — it's near-pure Firestore I/O, so live verification against real seeded data (done above) was used as the primary validation instead of a mocked test.

**Re-audit (2026-08-16) — triggered specifically by the client's reported missing dashboard greeting; CONFIRMED, zero bugs found, no code changes needed:**

- **Code audit:** re-read `Dashboard.jsx` directly — `activeView` defaults to `'home'` (`useState('home')`), the greeting (`greetingWord`/`firstName` computed from `userProfile.firstName` with a `displayName`-split fallback), all 4 metric cards, the Recent Reports table (full spec column set), and the Flacron Engine usage panel (photos processed, reports generated, storage honestly "Not yet available") are all present exactly as logged. Backend's `GET /dashboard-summary` (`reports.js`) confirmed present with the same `.count()`/`.select('imageCount')` projection approach documented (avoiding the composite-index dependency).
- **A real environment issue was hit and resolved during this pass (not a Phase 4 bug):** the backend crashed with a Node `FATAL ERROR: Zone Allocation failed - process out of memory`. Root-caused to **this session's own accumulated process leak**: repeated `taskkill` calls against only the port-listening `node server.js` child (across the Phase 1-4 re-audits) left the wrapping `nodemon` + `npm run dev` parent processes running as orphans every time, plus every Playwright run that hit a script error exited before its `browser.close()`, leaking Chromium instances — 27 orphaned `node.exe` + 11 orphaned `chrome.exe` processes had accumulated by this point on a machine with only ~8GB RAM. Fixed for the remainder of this session by killing full process trees (matched by command line, not just the listening PID) and wrapping the QA script's browser lifecycle in `try/finally`. Not a code defect; purely a test-session hygiene issue, now corrected.
- **Live-verified in a real browser** (fresh dev servers, real Firebase project, Playwright): seeded a populated test account directly via the Firebase Admin SDK (5 reports: 2 draft, 2 finalized, 1 archived, 48 total `imageCount`) and a genuinely empty account.
  - **Populated account, desktop:** greeting shows the correct time-of-day word and first name ("QA"); all 4 metric cards match seeded data exactly (Reports This Month 5, Photos Analyzed 48, Awaiting Review 2, Completed 2); Recent Reports correctly shows 4 rows with real claim numbers (the 5th, archived, report is correctly hidden — confirmed as pre-existing, intentional `GET /reports` behavior shared with "My Reports", not a bug, after an initial false alarm from my own test reading the loading-skeleton's 5 placeholder rows before the real fetch resolved); usage panel shows 48 photos processed and "Not yet available" for storage; Generate Report CTA present. 13/14 assertions passed cleanly; the one apparent "failure" (a refresh briefly showing the table's built-in error+Retry state) was confirmed to be the same already-documented, out-of-scope `aiLimiter`-on-the-whole-router rate-limit artifact (Phase 5 finding) reacting to this session's rapid repeated automated logins, not a Phase 4 regression — verified by re-running in isolation with a fresh backend restart.
  - **Populated account, mobile (390×844):** all 15/15 assertions passed cleanly, including refresh persistence and zero horizontal overflow.
  - **Empty account, desktop:** all 4 metrics correctly show 0, Recent Reports shows the "No reports yet" empty state with a working Generate Report CTA, usage panel shows 0 photos processed. **4/4 passed, zero console errors** — the cleanest possible confirmation.
  - Both throwaway accounts (and the 5 seeded report documents) deleted from the real Firebase project afterward.
- **Automated tests:** backend `npm test` 72/72, `npm run lint` 0 errors/38 warnings; frontend `npm run lint` 0 errors/15 warnings, `npm test` 4/4, `npm run build` succeeds — all identical to the original Phase 4 sign-off, confirming zero regression from Phase 5's later changes.
- **No code changes were made** — every piece of Phase 4 functionality, including the specifically-reported missing greeting, is present, correct, and working in this local dev environment.
- **Reiterating the cross-cutting finding from the Phase 3 re-audit:** none of this has ever been committed or deployed (see `CONTEXT.md`'s top-of-file callout). If the client's original "greeting is missing" report came from testing anywhere other than this exact local working tree, that remains the most likely explanation — this session could not reproduce the missing greeting anywhere in this codebase.

**Status:** ✅ **Re-confirmed Completed (2026-08-16)** — the specifically-reported missing greeting, and every other Phase 4 requirement, verified present and working; zero bugs found, zero code changes needed.

## Phase 5 — Generate Report Wizard Completion

**Objective:** Bring the 5-step wizard up to the spec's intended field/step coverage (without necessarily forcing the exact 7-step visual structure, if that's a deliberate simplification — flag as open question).

**Reason this phase is required:** Steps 2 (Inspection Information) and 3 (Documentation upload) don't exist at all (Items 20, 21 — `NOT IMPLEMENTED`); Step 1 is missing Policy Number, Insurance Company, Insured First/Last split, real Claim Type (Items 19 — `PARTIALLY COMPLETE`).

**Scope:** Frontend wizard (`Dashboard.jsx` steps), backend report schema to persist new fields, no AI-prompt change in this phase (Phase 1/7/8 handle photo-analysis-side changes).

**Exact tasks:**
1. **OPEN QUESTION:** Confirm with the client whether the wizard should be restructured into the spec's literal 7 stages (Claim/Inspection/Documentation/Photos/Analysis/Report/Review) or whether the current 5-stage UX should be kept and just have the *missing content* folded in (e.g., inspection fields added to an existing step). Recommend the latter for lower risk/effort unless the client wants the exact spec'd step count.
2. Add missing Claim Info fields: Policy Number, Insurance Company, Insured First Name/Last Name (split from the current single field), a real Claim Type enum (Property/Auto/Commercial/Liability/Other) distinct from Loss Type, Property Type.
3. Add an Inspection Information section/step: Inspection Date/Time, Inspector Name/ID, Inspection Type, Weather Conditions, Occupancy Status, Contact Present, and a proper Property Address split (Street/City/State/ZIP) replacing the current single free-text field.
4. Add a Documentation upload step/section: PDF/DOCX/TXT upload with filename/size/status/remove UI, backed by a new backend upload+storage path and (at minimum) file storage — text extraction for report-generation input is explicitly out of scope for this phase and deferred to Phase 7/8 integration follow-up, to avoid scope creep; note this explicitly if deferred.
5. Add an explicit "Save Draft" button (in addition to the existing silent autosave) to satisfy the spec's expected control, if the client wants it visible rather than implicit.

**Files/components likely affected:** `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (wizard step components, `ClaimIdentityFields`), `backend/routes/reports.js` (report schema/persist logic), `backend/config/storage.js` (new document storage path).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None, but this phase's output (documentation + inspection fields) is a soft prerequisite for Phases 6, 10, 12, 13, 15, 21 — implement early.

**Expected output:** A wizard that captures the full claim/inspection/documentation data set the rest of the spec assumes exists.

**Acceptance criteria:** All listed fields present, validated, and persisted on the report document; existing reports created before this change still open/edit without error (migration/back-compat check).

**Testing/verification steps:** Create a new report through the full wizard, verify every new field is saved and appears in the generated report's raw claim data; open a pre-existing (old-shape) report to confirm no crash from missing new fields.

**Risks/considerations:** Backward compatibility with existing report documents that lack the new fields; scope discipline — this phase should NOT include document *text extraction* into the AI prompt (that's report-generation logic, a separate follow-up once this phase's storage plumbing exists).

**Status:** ✅ Completed 2026-08-15

**Open question resolved:** client explicitly instructed keeping the current 5-step flow and folding the missing content in — no step-count restructuring.

**What shipped:**
- **Step 1 (Claim Information)** — `ClaimIdentityFields` extended with Policy Number, Insurance Company, Insured First Name/Last Name, Claim Type (`Property/Auto/Commercial/Liability/Other`), Property Type (`Single-Family Home/Multi-Family/Condo-Townhouse/Commercial/Other`). Insured Name stays the single canonical field the backend/AI-prompt/exports already depend on — First/Last are additive structured capture that live-compose into it (see bug fix below), never overwriting a Quick-Demo-filled or manually-typed value.
- **Step 2 (Property Details)** — structured Street/City/State/ZIP added alongside the existing single Property Address field, same live-compose-without-overwrite behavior; a new **Inspection Details** subsection added below Property Description: Inspection Date/Time, Inspector Name/ID, Inspection Type, Weather Conditions, Occupancy Status, Contact Present (+ conditional Contact Name).
- **Step 4 (renamed "Upload Photos & Documents")** — a new Supporting Documents subsection below the existing photo dropzone: drag-and-drop or browse, PDF/DOC/DOCX/TXT up to 10MB/10 files, each shown with filename/formatted size/"Ready" status/remove button; invalid type or oversize is rejected client-side with a toast before ever being staged.
- **Step 5 (Review & Generate)** — summary grid extended with Policy Number, Insurance Company, Claim Type, Property Type, Inspector, Inspection Date, and a Documents count row.
- **Explicit "Save Draft" button** — added to the wizard footer (visible on every step) alongside the pre-existing silent 30s autosave; both write to the same `localStorage` key, the button additionally shows a toast and a live "Saved HH:MM" label. Photos and documents are `File` objects that cannot be persisted to `localStorage` — this is called out in the UI copy, and was already true for photos before this phase.
- **Backend (`backend/routes/reports.js`)** — `POST /generate`'s multer usage changed from a single `images`-only field to `.fields([{images},{documents}])` (a new `generateUpload` instance, scoped to this one route only — the other two image-only routes are untouched). All 19 new fields are optional, validated when present (character limits, enum allowlists, date/time format), and persisted onto the report document; **not** added to `reportData`/`generateReport()`'s input, so the AI prompt is provably unchanged (Golden Rule: no AI-prompt change this phase). Documents are magic-byte validated (new `backend/utils/documentValidation.js`, mirroring the existing image validator: PDF/DOCX-zip/DOC-OLE signatures + a NUL-byte heuristic for `.txt`) and stored privately under `users/{uid}/reports/{reportId}/documents/{file}` (new `reportDocumentObject()` in `config/storage.js`), with a metadata array (`fileName/size/mimeType/objectPath/uploadedAt`) on the report doc. A new `GET /:id/documents/download` route proxies them back out, mirroring the existing export-download pattern.
- **`ReportDetailModal`** extended to show the new fields and a "Supporting Documents" list with working download links — every new field/row is conditionally rendered (`report.policyNumber && [...]`), so a pre-existing report simply omits those rows instead of showing blank/undefined.
- **Text extraction / AI-prompt integration for uploaded documents is explicitly out of scope for this phase**, per the task and the original PHASES.md risk note — documents are stored and displayed only, not read into the report-generation prompt. Deferred to a future Phase 7/8-style follow-up.

**Real bug found and fixed during live verification:** the First/Last Name → Insured Name (and Street/City/State/Zip → Property Address) "auto-compose while empty" logic had a live bug — typing into the *first* field (e.g. First Name) made Insured Name non-empty, which then blocked the *second* field (Last Name) from ever contributing, permanently freezing Insured Name at just "Jane" instead of "Jane Verifier". Fixed by comparing against the *previous* composed value instead of only checking emptiness: the sync now keeps applying as long as Insured Name still exactly equals what First+Last would already produce, and only stops the moment a user types something else directly into it (a business/trust name, a Quick Demo fill, a linked CRM claim). Re-verified end-to-end: "Jane" + "Verifier" → `insuredName: "Jane Verifier"` persisted correctly on the generated report; same fix applied and re-verified for the address fields (`"42 Verify Lane, Springfield, IL 62704"`).

**Backward compatibility — explicitly verified:** seeded a pre-Phase-5 "old-shape" report (all 19 new fields entirely absent, not even empty strings) directly in Firestore. It opened in the report list and detail modal with zero crashes/console errors — the conditional-row rendering simply omitted every new field, showing only the fields that existed before this phase.

**Live verification (Playwright, real Firebase project, fresh dev servers per established process-hygiene practice):**
- Full wizard walkthrough: all new Step 1/2 fields filled and correctly auto-composed; Inspection Details filled; 1 photo + 2 documents uploaded, one document removed (2→1), confirmed via the visible "Ready" status badges and list count; Save Draft clicked and confirmed via toast + "Saved HH:MM" label; full page refresh mid-wizard confirmed all text fields restored from `localStorage` (`claimNumber` round-tripped exactly) while photos/documents correctly reset to 0 (the documented, pre-existing files-can't-survive-localStorage limitation); Review step showed all new fields correctly; **Generate Report submitted successfully against the real Anthropic API** (`modelUsed: "anthropic/claude-opus-4-8"`) and the resulting Firestore document was inspected directly, confirming every new field (`policyNumber`, `insuredFirstName/LastName`, `claimType`, `propertyType`, `propertyStreet/City/State/Zip`, and a fully-formed `documents` array with correct `fileName/size/mimeType/objectPath/uploadedAt`) persisted exactly as entered.
- Required-field validation confirmed: clearing Claim Number/Insured Name correctly disabled the Generate button and showed the existing warning text.
- Invalid-document-type rejection confirmed: uploading a `.jpg` through the document input was rejected client-side (0 documents added, toast shown) before ever reaching the server.
- Document download confirmed: clicking a document's filename in `ReportDetailModal` triggered a real file download of the correct file via the new `/documents/download` route.
- Desktop (1440×900) and mobile (390×844) both rendered every new section correctly, including the new Inspection Details and Supporting Documents subsections on mobile with no layout breakage.
- Both the QA test account and its uploaded Storage objects (photos + documents) were deleted from the real Firebase project after verification.

**Significant pre-existing finding, NOT introduced by or fixed in this phase:** live testing repeatedly surfaced `429 Too Many Requests` on ordinary GET traffic (`/reports`, `/reports/templates`, `/reports/dashboard-summary`) as well as on `POST /generate` itself. Root cause: `backend/server.js`'s router-mounting table applies the AI-specific `aiLimiter` (**10 requests per 60 seconds**, intended for expensive AI-generation calls) as a `pre` middleware to the **entire** `/reports` router — every route under `/api/reports/*`, not just AI/generation endpoints. `CLAUDE.md`'s existing fragility note ("`GET /api/reports/ai-status` is public and shares the 10/min AI rate-limit bucket") already flagged part of this, but the actual blast radius is broader than that note suggests: a single Dashboard-home page load already fires ~4 requests into this same 10/60s bucket, so a real user doing completely ordinary usage (open dashboard, browse My Reports, generate one report) can plausibly exhaust this budget within a minute, not just under automated test load. This is pre-existing (the router-mounting table and the reports router itself predate this phase; Phase 5 only added routes to the same table), out of this phase's scope to fix, and is flagged here for a future dedicated pass — likely scoping `aiLimiter` down to just `/generate`, `/analyze-images`, `/:id/images`, and `/:id/sections/suggest` instead of the whole router.

**Deliberately not done:** no dedicated integration test was added for the `/generate` route's new validation branches (would require mocking Firestore/Storage/Anthropic end-to-end); instead a focused unit-test file (`backend/test/document-validation.test.js`, 8 tests) covers the new magic-byte document validator in isolation, and the validation branches themselves were live-verified through the browser (required-field blocking, invalid-document-type rejection) as described above.

---

## Phase 6 — Photo Upload & Per-Photo UX Hardening

**Objective:** Bring the photo-upload step's UX and per-photo data model in line with spec, without yet requiring a full async job queue.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 22 (`PARTIALLY COMPLETE`) and Item 43 (`NOT IMPLEMENTED`) — no list-view toggle, no per-photo size/status display, no rotate/preview, no bulk actions, no thumbnail generation, no duplicate-upload prevention, and a single bad file currently rejects the entire batch.

**Scope:** Frontend upload step UI + backend per-photo data model foundation (this is the prerequisite data layer Phase 7's async pipeline will build on).

**Exact tasks:**
1. Introduce a per-photo record (not just a flat `imagePaths` array) — minimally: `{id, fileName, size, mimeType, status, thumbnailUrl, uploadedAt}` — stored as a subcollection or embedded array-of-objects on the report doc (architectural choice to make now since Phase 7 depends on it).
2. Add server-side thumbnail generation using the already-installed `sharp` library (currently only used for logos) for report photos.
3. Add EXIF-orientation normalization on upload.
4. Add a simple duplicate-upload guard (e.g., hash-based check within a single upload batch).
5. Change validation so a single invalid/corrupt photo is rejected individually (flagged `FAILED`) rather than failing the entire request.
6. Add list-view toggle alongside the existing grid view.
7. Add per-photo card fields: file size, upload status, remove, rotate, click-to-preview (full-size modal).
8. Add bulk actions: Select All, Remove Selected, Retry Failed Uploads.
9. Update the max-limit UI copy to the spec's exact message: "Maximum of 100 photos reached. Remove a photo to upload another."

**Files/components likely affected:** `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (photo step UI), `backend/routes/reports.js` (upload handling), `backend/utils/imageValidation.js`, `backend/config/storage.js`, new thumbnail-generation utility.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Should follow Phase 5 (same wizard area) to avoid conflicting rework, though not a hard technical blocker.

**Expected output:** A photo-upload experience matching spec UX, and a per-photo data model ready for Phase 7 to attach async job status to.

**Acceptance criteria:** Uploading a mixed batch (including one deliberately corrupt file) isolates the failure to that one photo; thumbnails render from server-generated images, not client-side blob URLs alone; list/grid toggle works; bulk actions function correctly.

**Testing/verification steps:** Upload 100 photos including 1-2 corrupt/invalid files; confirm 98 succeed and 2 are flagged with a retry option; verify thumbnails load faster than full images on slow connections.

**Risks/considerations:** Changing the report's image storage shape (flat array → per-photo records) is a data-model migration — plan for existing reports with the old shape (read-compatibility, not necessarily write-migration).

**Status:** ✅ Completed 2026-08-16

**Architectural decision (how this stays synchronous, not Phase 7's async pipeline):** photos are still uploaded atomically with the report at `POST /generate` time (no pre-generate upload endpoint, no job queue) — explicitly per instruction not to build Phase 7's async pipeline in this phase. Per-photo hardening therefore splits across two layers: (1) the WIZARD step (pre-generate) does everything client-side, since the files aren't on the server yet — grid/list toggle, per-photo status/size/remove/rotate/preview, bulk actions, client-side duplicate (SHA-256) and corrupt-file (`createImageBitmap` decode) detection; (2) the BACKEND (at `/generate` and `/:id/images`, the two real "upload" moments) does the authoritative, server-side version of the same checks — per-photo records, magic-byte validation, hash-based dedup, EXIF normalization, real `sharp` thumbnails — as defense-in-depth against any caller (including a direct API integration) that bypasses the wizard's client-side filtering.

**What shipped:**
- **Per-photo data model** — new `photos` array on the report doc: `{id, fileName, size, mimeType, status, objectPath, thumbnailPath, error, uploadedAt}`, `status` one of `uploaded | failed | duplicate`. `imagePaths`/`imageCount` are kept in lockstep (derived from only the `uploaded` records), so every existing consumer (AI analysis, PDF/DOCX export image embedding, the Phase 4 dashboard-summary photo count) is completely unaffected.
- **Individual failure isolation** — new `backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js` (`processPhotoBatch()`) replaces the old `firstInvalidImage()` early-reject-the-whole-request check. One corrupt/spoofed file is now marked `failed` on its own record; every other photo in the same batch still uploads and gets AI-analyzed normally. The multer `fileFilter` for the `images` field was also relaxed (accepts everything, defers to per-file magic-byte validation) — previously an unlisted-but-real mimetype (e.g. a scanner's `image/tiff`) would 500 the *entire* request at the multer layer before the route handler ever ran; that failure mode is gone too.
- **Duplicate-upload guard** — SHA-256 content hash, scoped to one upload batch (not against a report's previously-uploaded photos). Server-side in `processPhotoBatch`; client-side (Web Crypto `crypto.subtle.digest`) in the wizard for instant feedback before the file is ever sent.
- **Server-generated thumbnails** — new `backend/utils/thumbnailService.js`, using the already-installed `sharp` (previously only used for white-label logos). Thumbnails are capped at 320px on the long edge, JPEG, stored under a new `users/{uid}/reports/{reportId}/thumbnails/` Storage path (`reportThumbnailObject()` in `config/storage.js`). Thumbnail generation is best-effort (e.g. a HEIC variant this libvips build can't decode) — a failure there never fails the photo itself, just leaves it without a thumbnail.
- **EXIF-orientation normalization** — `sharp(...).rotate()` (reads the EXIF Orientation tag, physically rotates pixels to match, strips the tag) applied to the *full* stored image, not just the thumbnail. This matters because the PDF/DOCX export generators embed raw image bytes and don't read EXIF themselves — without this fix, a portrait/upside-down phone photo would render sideways in an exported report. Falls back to the original bytes on any failure (never blocks the upload).
- **Wizard UX (client-side, `Dashboard.jsx` Step 4)**:
  - Grid/list view toggle.
  - Per-photo card/row: file size, live status (`Checking` / `Ready` / `Failed` / `Duplicate` — compact dot badge in grid, labeled pill in list), remove, rotate (real rotation — redraws onto a canvas and re-encodes, so the actual uploaded bytes are rotated, not just a CSS preview transform), click-to-preview (full-size modal).
  - Bulk actions: Select All / Deselect All, Remove Selected, Retry Failed Uploads (re-runs the client-side decode check on `corrupt`-status photos only — a legitimate retry, since `createImageBitmap` can occasionally fail transiently, e.g. under memory pressure, not just on genuinely corrupt files).
  - Exact spec'd 100-photo limit message — `"Maximum of 100 photos reached. Remove a photo to upload another."` — shown both as a toast on a blocked overflow attempt and as a persistent notice once the cap is reached (dropzone disabled).
  - Only `status === 'ready'` photos are ever included in the multipart submission; corrupt/duplicate ones stay visible (for removal/retry) but are never sent.
- **Read-only per-photo gallery for a generated report** (new, not explicitly spec'd but needed to make "server-generated thumbnails" actually observable/testable post-generation, since the wizard's own preview is necessarily client-side blob URLs pre-upload) — new backend routes `GET /:id/photos` (normalized per-photo list) and `GET /:id/photos/:photoId/image?variant=thumbnail|full` (authenticated proxy, private objects — same pattern as the existing document-download route), plus a new `ReportPhotoGallery` component wired into `ReportDetailModal`, shown whenever `report.imageCount > 0`.
- **Backward compatibility** — reports created before this phase have no `photos` field at all, only the old flat `imagePaths`/`imageCount`. `GET /:id/photos` synthesizes an equivalent list for those (synthetic `legacy-{index}` ids, `fileName: "Photo N"`, `status: 'uploaded'`, no thumbnail), so the gallery UI never has to special-case the old shape — verified against a real seeded pre-Phase-6 report (see Testing below).
- **Storage cleanup** — permanent report deletion (`DELETE /:id?permanent=true`) now also deletes thumbnail objects, not just full images.

**Files created:** `backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js`, `backend/utils/thumbnailService.js`, `backend/test/photo-batch-processor.test.js` (10 tests).
**Files modified:** `backend/routes/reports.js` (multer fileFilter relaxed for images; `/generate` and `/:id/images` rewritten around `processPhotoBatch`; new `/:id/photos` + `/:id/photos/:photoId/image` routes; delete-cleanup extended to thumbnails; removed the now-dead `persistReportImages` helper), `backend/config/storage.js` (new `reportThumbnailObject()` path builder), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (Step 4 UI rewrite, new photo-management state/handlers, new `PhotoStatusBadge`/`ReportPhotoGallery` components), `frontend/src/services/api.js` (new `getPhotos`/`getPhotoImageBlob` methods).

**Acceptance criteria — status:**
- ✅ Uploading a mixed batch (including a deliberately corrupt file) isolates the failure to that one photo — verified both via unit tests and a real, unmocked HTTP call directly to `POST /generate` with a corrupt file mixed among valid ones (bypassing the wizard's own client-side filtering entirely): the corrupt file came back `status: 'failed'` with the other 2 valid photos `status: 'uploaded'`, and a duplicate came back `status: 'duplicate'` — see Testing below.
- ✅ Thumbnails render from server-generated images, not client-side blob URLs alone — confirmed via direct HTTP checks (`GET /:id/photos/:photoId/image?variant=thumbnail` returns real JPEG bytes, `Content-Type: image/jpeg`) and via the browser (`ReportPhotoGallery` fetches and displays them as authenticated blobs).
- ✅ List/grid toggle works; bulk actions (Select All, Remove Selected, Retry Failed Uploads) function correctly — live-verified in a real browser (Playwright), see Testing below.

**Testing/verification performed:**
- **Automated:** backend `npm test` → **82/82 passing** (72 pre-existing + 10 new in `photo-batch-processor.test.js`, dependency-injected against fake Storage/sharp calls — no real network/Firebase in the unit tests, matching this codebase's existing DI test pattern from `aiService.analyzeImages`'s `callVisionApi`). Backend `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 40 warnings (baseline 38 + 2 new `catch(err)`-unused warnings matching the existing accepted pattern). Frontend `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 15 warnings (unchanged baseline — the two new unused-import warnings from an earlier draft were removed). Frontend `npm test` → 4/4 passing (unchanged). Frontend `npm run build` → succeeds.
- **Live, real Anthropic + real Firebase project** (throwaway QA account, Agency tier, created via the Firebase Admin SDK and fully deleted afterward; fixture images generated with `sharp` — 2 distinct valid JPEGs, 1 valid PNG, an exact byte-duplicate, a genuinely corrupt file with a spoofed `.jpg` name, a real JPEG with an EXIF `Orientation=6` tag, and a 12-image batch for bulk-action tests):
  - Full wizard walkthrough (Playwright, desktop 1440×900): staged 3 valid photos → all validated to `Ready`; added a corrupt file → flagged `Failed` individually without rejecting the other 3; added an exact duplicate → flagged `Duplicate`; added the EXIF-rotated photo → validated `Ready`; toggled grid↔list (list renders full rows); rotated a `Ready` photo (confirmed the blob URL actually changed, proving new bytes were produced, not just a CSS transform); opened and closed the full-size preview modal; ran "Retry Failed Uploads" (corrupt file correctly stays `Failed` — it's still genuinely not a valid image — no crash); Select All + Remove Selected cleared all staged photos; single-photo remove worked; re-staged a mixed batch and **generated a real report against the live Anthropic API** (79.9s, well within the request timeout). **18/18 scripted checks passed, zero console errors, zero failed/5xx HTTP requests.**
  - **Direct, unmocked API call bypassing all client-side filtering** (the strongest test of the backend's own isolation logic): signed in via the real Firebase Auth REST API, POSTed directly to `/api/v1/reports/generate` with 4 files (`valid-red.jpg`, `corrupt.jpg`, `valid-blue.jpg`, `valid-red-copy.jpg` — an exact duplicate of the first) with no wizard involved at all. Response: `valid-red.jpg → uploaded`, `corrupt.jpg → failed` ("File is not a valid image..."), `valid-blue.jpg → uploaded` (unaffected by the corrupt file before it), `valid-red-copy.jpg → duplicate` ("Duplicate of \"valid-red.jpg\"..."). Confirmed both successfully-uploaded photos AND their thumbnails exist in the real Firebase Storage bucket.
  - **Gallery endpoints, direct HTTP:** `GET /:id/photos` returns correct per-photo metadata (status, `hasThumbnail`, error messages); `GET /:id/photos/:photoId/image?variant=thumbnail|full` returns real `image/jpeg` bytes (200) for `uploaded` photos and a clean 404 for `failed`/`duplicate` ones (which were never stored, correctly).
  - **Backward compatibility, real report:** seeded a genuine pre-Phase-6 report directly in Firestore (`imagePaths`/`imageCount` only, no `photos` field at all, reusing 2 real already-uploaded images so `downloadBuffer` succeeds). `GET /:id/photos` correctly synthesized a 2-item legacy list; both `variant=thumbnail` and `variant=full` returned real 200 image bytes via direct HTTP. In the browser, the report opened in "My Reports" and its detail modal with **zero crashes**, showing "Photos (2)" and a gallery grid (thumbnails intermittently didn't load in the browser pass specifically — see the rate-limiter note below; the direct-HTTP check above proves the underlying mechanism is correct).
  - **Mobile (390×844):** opened the sidebar via the FAB toggle, reached Step 4, staged 2 photos — grid renders responsively, zero horizontal overflow.
  - **100-photo limit:** staged exactly 100 photos (repeating a 12-image batch); the dropzone disables, the persistent notice `"Maximum of 100 photos reached. Remove a photo to upload another."` renders and is visible; attempting one more file is blocked with the identical exact message; count stays at 100/100.
  - **Storage cleanup on delete:** verified with real Storage objects (2 images + 2 thumbnails) that permanent report deletion removes all 4 objects; also called the real `DELETE /:id?permanent=true` HTTP endpoint end-to-end and confirmed both the Firestore doc and Storage objects are gone afterward.
  - All test reports, the seeded legacy report, and the throwaway QA account (Auth user + Firestore profile + all Storage objects) were deleted afterward. All temporary QA scripts and fixture images were created outside the repo (session scratchpad) or deleted from the backend directory before finishing — confirmed via `git status`, zero residue.
- **Known, pre-existing, out-of-scope interference during live testing (not a Phase 6 bug):** the already-documented (Phase 5) `aiLimiter`-applied-to-the-whole-`/reports`-router issue (10 req/60s shared by every `/api/reports/*` route, not just AI/generation endpoints) was tripped repeatedly by this phase's own rapid, request-heavy Playwright test iterations, producing intermittent 429s — most visibly, it occasionally caused one of the 2 gallery-thumbnail fetches on the legacy-report browser check to silently fail (the `ReportPhotoGallery` component correctly falls back to a placeholder icon per-photo rather than crashing, exactly as designed for a genuine real-world thumbnail-fetch failure). This was confirmed to be the rate limiter, not a gallery defect, by re-running the identical check as a direct, unmocked HTTP call (outside the browser/rate-limit-heavy login flow) which returned clean 200s with correct image bytes every time. Restarting the backend between test passes (resetting the in-memory limiter) also produced clean browser runs. No code was changed for this — it's the same pre-existing, already-flagged production reliability risk noted in Phase 5, still not yet fixed, still out of this phase's scope.

**Status:** ✅ **Completed (2026-08-16) against its own original task list.** ⚠️ **Not yet complete against the full specification — see the addendum immediately below, added 2026-08-16 during a full-spec audit. Do not close Phase 6 out until the addendum's items are implemented and re-verified.**

---

### Phase 6 — Post-Completion Gap Addendum (2026-08-16, full-spec audit)

The original Phase 6 task list above was derived from a *summary spreadsheet* audit, not the complete 50-section specification. Comparing this phase's actual shipped code against the full spec (specifically §§16-22, the 100-photo technical requirement, image-limit UI, background processing, and mobile photo-capture sections) surfaced real, confirmed gaps. Each item below was verified directly against the current code (not assumed) before being listed.

**1. Mobile "Take Photo" / "Choose From Library" flow — NOT implemented.** Verified: the only photo-upload input in `Dashboard.jsx` is a single hidden `<input type="file" multiple accept="image/*">` with no `capture` attribute and no split UI. The spec (§22) wants an explicit choice ("Add Inspection Photos" → "Take Photo" / "Photo Library" as two distinct options) so a mobile user can deliberately choose the camera vs. an existing photo. **This is Phase-6-compatible** — it's a client-side input/UI change (e.g. two buttons, one with `capture="environment"`, one without), not an architecture change. Continuous multi-shot capture with each photo auto-appended to the staged list is also Phase-6-compatible (stage-as-captured, same local validation pipeline already built); a literal "each photo uploads to the server the instant it's taken" (not just staged client-side) would require Phase 7's real per-photo upload architecture — flagged so this distinction isn't lost during implementation.

**2. Real HEIC support — currently detection-only, never actually verified against a genuine HEIC file.** Verified: `imageValidation.js` detects the HEIC magic bytes purely to classify the type; `thumbnailService.js`/`photoBatchProcessor.js` attempt `sharp` normalization/thumbnailing and silently fall back (best-effort) if it throws — there is **no actual HEIC→JPEG conversion path**, and Phase 1's own test suite documents that HEIC images are "counted as skipped" for AI vision analysis, not converted so they *can* be analyzed. My Phase 6 QA session only used synthetic JPEG/PNG fixtures (`sharp`-generated) — a genuine `.heic` file (e.g. from an iPhone) was never actually run through the pipeline. **Open technical question:** does this deployment's `sharp`/libvips build have HEIF support compiled in at all? If not, HEIC photos will upload and store fine (original bytes preserved) but will have no thumbnail and be skipped from AI analysis and, per the spec's "export compatibility" requirement, may not embed correctly in PDF/DOCX exports either (the export generators haven't been tested with a HEIC source image). This needs a real-file verification pass before it can be called done; if libvips lacks HEIF, adding a dedicated conversion library (e.g. `heic-convert`) is a small, Phase-6-compatible addition, not a Phase 7 concern.

**3. Per-image upload progress and controlled concurrent uploads — architecturally limited by design, needs an explicit decision.** Photos still upload in one atomic multipart POST at Generate time (by design — this is what kept Phase 6 synchronous and out of Phase 7's territory). This means genuine *per-file network progress* and *controlled upload concurrency* (as literally described in spec §16 — "uploads should run concurrently in controlled batches") **cannot exist without either**: (a) a client-side simulated/estimated per-file progress bar during the single multipart upload (Phase-6-compatible, cosmetic only — doesn't reflect true per-file completion), or (b) Phase 7's real incremental per-photo upload endpoint (uploads happen individually, each with real progress, before Generate is even clicked). Recommend (a) for Phase 6 (a reasonable, honest UX improvement — an overall upload-progress bar with a "not per-file resumable yet" caveat) and explicitly deferring true per-file resumable concurrent uploads to Phase 7, where the architecture already supports it.

**4. Original image preservation — currently VIOLATED by the shipped code; this is a real defect against the full spec, not just a missing feature.** Verified: `photoBatchProcessor.js` uploads `storedBuffer` (the output of `normalizeOrientation()`, a re-encoded/re-rotated/re-compressed buffer), not `f.buffer` (the untouched original), as the primary "full" image object. The spec (§46) is explicit: "The original must not be permanently altered by AI processing" and wants three tiers — **original / optimized display / thumbnail** — as separate stored objects. The current code only has two tiers (a *modified* full image + a thumbnail), and the original bytes are discarded once EXIF-normalization succeeds (they're only preserved by accident, as a fallback, when normalization *fails*). **Fix:** store the original buffer unmodified at its own object path, generate the EXIF-normalized version as a *separate* "optimized display" object (used for the report photo-documentation layout, exports, and full-size preview), and keep the existing thumbnail as the third tier. This is a genuine, concrete code fix needed before Phase 6 can be called spec-complete, not just a nice-to-have.

**5. Low-quality image detection/warning — NOT implemented.** Verified via code search: no blur/resolution/exposure heuristic exists anywhere for uploaded photos. Spec §22 (Photo Analysis features list) explicitly wants this. **Assigned to new Phase 24** (below), not Phase 6, since it's closer to a photo-quality *analysis* feature (akin to Phase 8's per-photo review) than an upload-mechanics concern — but flagged here so it's not missed.

**6. Photo ordering, grouping, annotations — NOT implemented; assigned to new Phase 24.** No `position`/`order` field exists on photo records (order is implicit array-append order, no reorder capability); no room/area grouping UI exists; **no annotation/drawing tooling exists anywhere in the codebase** — this was *also* in the pre-`PHASES.md` backlog (`TASKS.md` T-2.4) and was dropped when this roadmap was created. See Phase 24.

**7. Duplicate detection scope — confirmed too narrow relative to the full spec.** Verified: `photoBatchProcessor.js`'s `seenHashes` Map is created fresh per call and is **never seeded from the report's existing `photos` array** — duplicate detection only ever catches duplicates *within the same upload batch*, exactly as the original (narrower) Phase 6 task said ("scoped to a single upload batch"), but the full spec (§16, "prevent duplicate accidental uploads where possible") does not carry that qualifier, and the client explicitly asked for detection against photos *already attached to the report*. **Fix needed:** when adding photos to an existing report (`POST /:id/images`) or across multiple upload interactions within one wizard session, hash-check new files against the existing `photos` array's already-stored images too (this requires downloading/hashing existing stored images once, or persisting each photo's hash on its record at upload time so later comparisons are cheap — recommend the latter: add a `contentHash` field to the per-photo record).

**8. Counting behavior — the current single "X / 100" count conflates uploaded, failed, and duplicate photos; needs an explicit breakdown.** The header currently shows one number (total staged, regardless of status). The client flagged this as imprecise. **Fix:** show a breakdown (e.g. "97 ready · 2 failed · 1 duplicate" alongside or instead of the bare total), and make sure the 100-photo cap counts against the right denominator — recommend capping the *total staged slots* at 100 (current behavior, keeps the UI simple) but always surfacing the ready/failed/duplicate split so a user isn't confused about why they're "at 100" when only e.g. 85 will actually be analyzed.

**9. The overly broad `/reports` `aiLimiter` — confirmed still present, now directly affects Phase 6's own new gallery endpoints.** Already documented as a pre-existing, out-of-scope Phase 5 finding, but Phase 6 added two more routes (`GET /:id/photos`, `GET /:id/photos/:photoId/image`) under the same affected router, and this session's own live QA repeatedly hit 429s on them. **Fix (small, Phase-6-appropriate, not a Phase 7 concern):** scope `aiLimiter` in `backend/server.js`'s router-mounting table down to only the genuinely AI-cost-bearing routes (`/generate`, `/analyze-images`, `/:id/images`, `/:id/sections/suggest`) instead of the entire `/reports` router; add a separate, much looser (or no) rate limit for read-only routes like `/:id/photos` and `/:id/photos/:photoId/image`.

**Status of this addendum:** ✅ **Reopened and implemented same day (2026-08-16 follow-up), per explicit client instruction.** Items 1, 3(a), 4, 7, 8, 9 are DONE (code + tests + live verification — see the full implementation/testing writeup and the "Client Resolutions" section above this phase for exact detail). Item 2 (HEIC) was rigorously VERIFIED against this real runtime but surfaced a genuine limitation, not a fix (needs a client call — supply a real `.heic` file, or approve a conversion-library dependency). Items 5 and 6 remain correctly assigned to Phase 24 (untouched, not in scope for this addendum). Item 3(b) (true per-file network progress/concurrency) remains correctly assigned to Phase 7.

**Implementation Update (2026-08-16 follow-up session):**
- **3-tier photo storage**: `photoBatchProcessor.js` now uploads the untouched original first (`originalPath`, via new `reportOriginalObject()` in `config/storage.js`), then the EXIF-normalized "display" copy (`objectPath`, unchanged name/role from the original ship — every existing reader, export, and AI-analysis call site is unaffected), then the thumbnail. A storage failure on any one tier isolates to that photo (existing per-photo isolation now applies per-tier too) without touching the others already saved. `GET /:id/photos/:photoId/image` gained `variant=original`.
- **Cross-existing-report duplicate detection**: `processPhotoBatch(uid, reportId, files, existingHashes, deps)` — note the signature gained a 4th positional parameter (`existingHashes`, before the test-only `deps`) — seeds its dedup map from the report's already-attached photos' `contentHash` values (new field, persisted per photo going forward) before processing new files. `POST /:id/images` passes the existing report's photo hashes; `POST /generate` passes `[]` (nothing exists yet for a brand-new report).
- **`aiLimiter` rescoped**: removed from `server.js`'s router-level `pre` middleware entirely; now imported from `backend/middleware/rateLimiters.js` (which already existed, unused, in the repo — likely a prior unfinished attempt at this exact fix) and applied directly to `POST /generate`, `POST /analyze-images`, `POST /:id/images`, `POST /:id/sections/suggest` only.
- **Mobile capture UI**: two explicit buttons ("Take Photo" using a `capture="environment"` input, "Choose From Library" using the existing plain input) added above the drag-and-drop dropzone in the wizard's Step 4, plus a one-time continuous-capture hint.
- **Per-image upload progress**: `reportsAPI.generate(formData, onUploadProgress)` in `frontend/src/services/api.js` now threads an `onUploadProgress` callback through to axios; the "Generating Your Report" overlay shows a live "Photo N of M — X%" readout and progress bar, computed from real bytes-sent/total, only during the genuine upload phase.
- **Counting breakdown**: the Step 4 header now shows "N ready · N failed · N duplicate" beneath the "X / 100" count whenever any photos aren't `ready`.
- **HEIC verification (real runtime, not assumed):** confirmed via direct execution against the exact installed `sharp@0.33.5`/libvips@8.15.3/libheif@1.18.2 that this build can encode real AV1-coded HEIF (`ftyp avif`, verified by inspecting the output bytes) but throws `Unsupported compression` for HEVC-coded HEIF — the codec real iPhone `.heic` files use. This is direct, current-runtime evidence (not documentation lookup or a guess) that classic HEIC will not decode with this exact build either, since libheif's codec plugins register encode+decode together and only the license-clean AV1 path is present. A literal real `.heic` file was not available to test decode directly in this sandboxed session (no camera, no HEVC-capable local encoder, no safe internet-fetch path) — flagged as the one item in this addendum needing either a real test file from the client or an explicit decision to add a conversion library.
- **Tests:** `backend/test/photo-batch-processor.test.js` expanded to 13 tests (was 10) covering the new 3-tier storage (original bytes vs. normalized display bytes differ; original-tier upload failure vs. display-tier upload failure both isolate correctly) and cross-existing-report duplicate detection. Full backend suite: **85/85 passing** (0 regressions). Frontend: 0 lint errors (15 warnings, unchanged baseline), 4/4 tests, build succeeds.

## Phase 7 — Async Photo Analysis Pipeline (Architectural)

**Objective:** Convert photo analysis from synchronous (blocking the `POST /generate` request) into a real background pipeline with progress tracking.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 23 (`NEEDS FIX`) and Item 41 (`NOT IMPLEMENTED`) — there is no queue/worker system at all; analysis is awaited inline in the request handler, meaning it cannot survive a closed browser tab and cannot report real incremental progress. This is the single largest architectural gap in the audit.

**Scope:** Backend infrastructure decision + implementation. This is the highest-risk, highest-effort phase in the roadmap and should be scoped carefully, likely broken into its own sub-tasks when implemented (job creation → queue → worker → status polling → UI wiring).

**Exact tasks:**
1. **OPEN QUESTION — must be answered before implementation:** What job-queue technology fits the current hosting (Render, single Node web service, no Redis/message broker currently provisioned)? Options to evaluate: (a) a lightweight in-process async queue with a persisted job-status doc per photo (simplest, no new infra, but doesn't survive a server restart mid-job), (b) Firestore-triggered Cloud Functions (requires GCP Functions setup, decouples from Render entirely), (c) a managed queue (e.g. Render background worker + Redis/BullMQ, adds infra cost/complexity). Recommend evaluating (a) as a first iteration given current infra, with (b)/(c) as a future scale-up — but this is a decision for the client/engineering lead, not to be assumed.
2. Define a report/photo status vocabulary matching (or deliberately adapting) spec's states: photo `UPLOADING/UPLOADED/QUEUED/ANALYZING/COMPLETED/FAILED/REVIEW_REQUIRED/EXCLUDED`; report `DRAFT/DOCUMENTATION/PHOTO_UPLOAD/ANALYZING/READY_TO_GENERATE/GENERATING/NEEDS_REVIEW/APPROVED/EXPORTED/ARCHIVED` — reconcile with the existing simpler `draft/finalized/archived` vocabulary (likely need a mapping layer, not a breaking rename).
3. Implement job creation on photo upload (using Phase 6's per-photo records) and a background worker that processes photos individually (batches respecting Phase 1's full-coverage fix).
4. Implement a status-polling endpoint (e.g. `GET /reports/:id/analysis-status`) returning per-photo and aggregate progress.
5. Build the `/reports/:id/analysis` page showing real progress ("63 of 87 photos analyzed", live-updating), replacing the current fake `setInterval` step list.
6. Ensure the page (and underlying job) survives navigation away and back, and survives the browser tab closing (job keeps running server-side).

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/routes/reports.js`, `backend/services/aiService.js`, new `backend/services/photoJobService.js` (or similar), new frontend route + page for `/reports/:id/analysis`, `frontend/src/App.jsx`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 1 (full coverage must exist before wiring progress around it) and Phase 6 (per-photo data model).

**Expected output:** Photo analysis that runs genuinely in the background with accurate, resumable progress reporting.

**Acceptance criteria:** Starting analysis, closing the browser, and returning later shows accurate resumed progress; a single photo's analysis failure doesn't block others; the report only proceeds to generation once all photos are in a terminal state (`COMPLETED`/`FAILED`/`EXCLUDED`).

**Testing/verification steps:** Start a 100-photo analysis, close the tab mid-way, reopen `/reports/:id/analysis`, confirm progress reflects true server-side state; kill/restart the backend process mid-job (if using in-process queue) and verify recovery behavior is at least understood/documented even if not fully resilient in v1.

**Risks/considerations:** This is a genuine architecture change with real infra/cost tradeoffs — do not start without the technology decision above being made explicitly. Consider doing a smaller spike/proof-of-concept before committing the full phase.

**Amendment (2026-08-16 full-spec audit) — 3 additional tasks:**
7. **Automatic retry with a maximum-attempt cap and a distinct "Needs Attention" terminal status** (spec §47) — if a photo's analysis job fails, retry automatically up to a defined max (e.g. 3 attempts) before landing it in `Needs Attention` (a status distinct from a plain `FAILED`, since `Needs Attention` implies "we already tried and gave up," inviting a manual "Retry Analysis" action, whereas `FAILED` in the current Phase 6 model is a same-request, immediate outcome). Track `attemptCount` on the `AnalysisJobs` record per spec §15's data model.
8. **`AnalysisJobs` collection field alignment** — when designing the job-record schema (task 3 above), match spec §15's exact field list (`id, reportId, photoId, type, status, attemptCount, startedAt, completedAt, error`) rather than inventing a divergent shape, since the spec's Database Structure section is the one part of the document that reads as a literal schema contract rather than descriptive prose.
9. **FLACRON ENGINE visual identity on the processing/analysis page** (spec §42) — the `/reports/:id/analysis` progress page (task 5 above) should use the distinctive orange lightning/processing-glow treatment described in the spec, not a generic progress bar, so the branding work already done elsewhere (2026-07-19 "Flacron Engine" rebrand) extends to this new page too.

**Status:** Completed (2026-08-16) — full implementation report below.

### Implementation summary (2026-08-16)

**Architecture decision (task 1, per client instruction):** in-process async queue — no new infra (no Redis/BullMQ/Cloud Functions). Firestore is the durable job-status store (`analysisJobs` collection, one doc per photo + one per report-generation stage, matching spec §15's exact field list per task 8). `POST /generate` now: uploads photos synchronously (fast), creates the job docs, responds to the client immediately, then continues running the actual AI pipeline (`photoJobService.runReportPipeline`) as an un-awaited, internally-caught async function in the same Node process. Accepted tradeoff (per client sign-off): in-flight work does not survive a Render restart mid-job — acceptable for a v1 in-process design.

**Status vocabulary (task 2):** reused the existing dead/unused `'processing'`/`'failed'` report-status values already present in the frontend's status filter and badge styling (built ahead of this phase, apparently anticipating it) rather than adopting the spec's full 10-state vocabulary — a mapping layer, not a breaking rename, per this phase's own task 2 guidance. Photo-level `analysisStatus`: `queued → analyzing → completed | needs_attention` (task 7's distinct terminal status, separate from upload-time `failed`).

**Task 3 (job creation + background worker):** `backend/services/photoJobService.js` (new). Creates one `analysisJobs` doc per analyzable photo + one `report_generation` doc; `runReportPipeline` analyzes photos via `aiService.analyzeImages`'s existing concurrent-batch worker pool, then generates the report content, updating both the per-photo `photos[]` array (via a Firestore transaction — needed because concurrent vision batches can otherwise race and overwrite each other's writes to the same array) and the job docs as each stage completes.

**Task 7 (retry with attempt cap):** `aiService.analyzeBatch` retries internally up to `MAX_BATCH_ATTEMPTS = 3` before a batch is marked `needs_attention`; `POST /:id/analysis/retry` re-downloads the stuck photos' already-uploaded bytes (nothing re-uploaded from the client) and re-fires the pipeline scoped to just those photos. A `mergeImageAnalysis()` helper combines the fresh partial result with whatever already succeeded on the first run (sums counts, concatenates damages, dedupes review items, escalates to the worse severity) — an early implementation bug where a partial retry would have silently discarded all prior findings was caught and fixed before shipping (see verification below).

**Task 4 (status endpoint):** `GET /:id/analysis-status` returns per-status counts (queued/analyzing/completed/needsAttention/failed) + the report-generation stage's own status, polled by the frontend every 3s.

**Tasks 5–6 (frontend progress page + resumability) + task 9 (FLACRON ENGINE branding):** implemented as a new `activeView === 'analysis'` view inside `Dashboard.jsx` (not a standalone route — real bookmarkable routes are Phase 30's job; this view is reachable via internal state, consistent with the rest of the pre-migration app) — FLACRON ENGINE branded (pulsing gradient circle + Zap icon per spec §42), live progress bar from the polled status, "you can safely leave this page" messaging, and a Retry Analysis button. Resumability: clicking a `status: 'processing'` report anywhere in My Reports (`openReport()`) routes back into this same analysis view instead of the normal detail modal, so navigating away and back resumes tracking the real server-side job rather than losing it.

**Performance fix found during this phase's own live verification (not a pre-existing task, but a real defect the phase's acceptance criteria exposed):** `processPhotoBatch` (`backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js`) processed uploaded files fully sequentially — each file needing 3 real Storage round-trips (original/display/thumbnail) — which measured at **28.7 seconds** for a 12-photo `POST /generate`, directly undermining this phase's "the client never waits for AI" goal (the AI stage was already async; the upload stage itself was the remaining synchronous bottleneck). Fixed by restructuring into (1) a fast, sequential, CPU-only validation + SHA-256 duplicate-hash pass over all files (kept sequential deliberately, so two copies of the same duplicate can never both slip past the "not yet seen" check at once), then (2) a concurrency-capped worker pool (`UPLOAD_CONCURRENCY = 8`, same wave-based pattern already used for `VISION_BATCH_CONCURRENCY`) that performs the actual Storage I/O only for files that passed validation. Response time for the same 12-photo request dropped to **~9-11 seconds** (measured across 3 separate runs: 11022ms, 9589ms, 9061ms) — a 61-68% reduction. This is a real, load-bearing fix, not a nice-to-have: without it, Phase 7's core promise (immediate response) was still measurably broken for the exact scenario the phase exists to solve.

**Verification (2026-08-16):**
- **Automated:** full backend suite **94/94 passing** (5 new pure-function tests for `mergeImageAnalysis`, plus the existing `image-analysis-batching.test.js` and new `photo-batch-processor.test.js` upload-failure tests rewritten to identify target files by content/objectPath rather than call-index, since concurrent uploads make raw call ordering non-deterministic). Frontend **4/4 passing**. Both packages lint clean (0 errors; pre-existing warning counts unchanged) and build clean.
- **Live, against the real backend/Firebase project/Anthropic API (not mocked), via a throwaway QA account (created and fully deleted after testing — reports, `analysisJobs`, Firestore user doc, and the Firebase Auth user itself; zero residue, confirmed via `git status`):**
  - 12-photo `POST /generate`: responds in ~9-11s (was 28.7s before the concurrency fix); pipeline progresses `processing → draft` correctly; real content (14-15k chars), real `imageAnalysis`, `qualityScore: 100`, all 12 photos reach `analysisStatus: 'completed'`; `reportsThisMonth` increments only on success.
  - 24-photo `POST /generate` (3 concurrent vision batches, not just 2): all 24 photos correctly reach `completed` with no lost updates and no duplicate/collided photo records — confirms the Firestore-transaction fix holds under 3+ simultaneously-completing batches, not just 2.
  - `POST /:id/analysis/retry`: injected a real `needs_attention` state on one photo of a completed report (genuine API failures are impractical to trigger on demand), called retry, confirmed the report correctly returned to `processing`, the targeted photo was re-analyzed and reached `completed`, and — critically — `imageAnalysis.totalImagesAnalyzed` **increased** (existing 4 + 1 fresh = 5) rather than being overwritten, proving the merge-not-replace fix works for real.
  - 409 `REPORT_PROCESSING` guards: confirmed `PUT /:id`, `POST /:id/approve`, and `POST /:id/export` all correctly reject with 409 while a report is still `processing`, and all three succeed normally once the pipeline reaches a terminal state.
  - `POST /:id/images` (adding photos to an existing, already-completed report): responds in ~3.1s (async, not blocking on vision analysis); the 3 newly added photos correctly reach `analysisStatus: 'completed'` in the background and merge onto the report (5 total photos, all completed).
- **Not verified this session:** live-browser confirmation of the new `activeView === 'analysis'` frontend view (rendering, polling, resume-on-navigate-away-and-back, mobile viewport) — **no browser-automation tool (e.g. Playwright MCP) was available in this session**, unlike prior phases in this project where one was used. The frontend code implementing this (described in tasks 5-6 above) was written and is included in this phase's diff, but has only been exercised indirectly (via the backend endpoints it calls, all of which were verified live above) — not visually confirmed in an actual browser. **This should be verified in a real browser before this phase is considered fully QA-complete**, either by the client directly or in a future session where a browser tool is available.

**Deferred to Phase 8 (correctly, not scope-crept into this phase):** per-photo structured review UI, per-photo Edit/Approve/Exclude controls, and feeding user-approved (not raw) observations into report generation — this phase only makes the *pipeline* async; it does not change what the AI analysis output looks like or how it's reviewed.
