## Phase 12 — My Reports & Claims Management Completion

**Objective:** Complete the missing filters and row actions on My Reports and Claims, and add a Photos section to the claim workspace.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 30 (`PARTIALLY COMPLETE`) — no type/creator/date/org filters despite backend support; no Duplicate action anywhere; Archive exists server-side but is unreachable from the UI; Claims list has no search/date filters and no Edit/Duplicate/Archive/Delete actions despite backend endpoints existing unused; claim workspace has no Photos tab.

**Scope:** Frontend list/table UI for both My Reports and Claims; one new backend endpoint (Duplicate) if it doesn't exist for reports.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Expose the existing backend filter params (type/creator/date/org) as UI controls on My Reports.
2. Wire the My Reports "Delete" action to offer both Archive (soft, existing backend support) and permanent Delete as distinct choices, rather than only hard-deleting.
3. Add a Duplicate action for reports (new backend endpoint if none exists — copy claim data into a new draft, do not duplicate photos/analysis).
4. Add search + date filters to the Claims list; wire the already-existing `PUT`/`DELETE /claims/:id` backend endpoints to real Edit/Archive/Delete UI actions.
5. Add a Photos tab/section to `CRMClaimProfile.jsx` (aggregating photos across the claim's linked reports).

**Files/components likely affected:** `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (reports view), `frontend/src/pages/CRM.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/CRMClaimProfile.jsx`, `backend/routes/reports.js` (duplicate endpoint), `backend/routes/crm.js`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Soft dependency on Phase 5 — duplicating a report should carry over the fuller field set once it exists, to avoid duplicating twice.

**Expected output:** My Reports and Claims become fully manageable list views matching spec actions.

**Acceptance criteria:** All listed filters and actions function and are backed by real data; Duplicate produces a correct new draft; Archive and Delete are clearly distinct actions.

**Testing/verification steps:** Exercise every filter and every row action on both lists against seeded test data; confirm archived reports don't appear in default views but remain retrievable.

**Risks/considerations:** Decide whether "Duplicate" should also duplicate uploaded photos (storage cost) or start the new report with a clean photo set — recommend clean/no-photos duplicate, flag as open question if unclear.

**Amendment (2026-08-16 full-spec audit):** the spec's nav (§9) and URL structure (§36) list "Claims" as a top-level item available to everyone, but the current `/claims` functionality lives entirely inside `CRM.jsx`, tier-gated to Agency/Enterprise. **Added as an Open Question below** — should claim-centric organization be available to all tiers (Starter/Professional included), or is CRM/Claims intentionally an Agency+ upsell? This changes this phase's scope meaningfully (a Starter-tier "Claims" view with no CRM would need its own simpler design).

**Implementation decision on the above open question (2026-08-18):** this amendment's own text says the question was "added as an Open Question below," but a re-check of the "Open Questions Requiring Client/Team Decision" section found it was never actually enumerated there (a documentation gap, similar to others already noted elsewhere in this file) — it remains genuinely unresolved. Per the client's explicit instruction this session ("follow the documented Claims tier-gating decision; if still unresolved, stop and ask before implementing that specific behavior"), this phase deliberately did **not** touch tier-gating: Claims/CRM remains exactly as tier-gated as it already was (Agency/Enterprise only, `requireTier('agency')` server-side + a matching frontend upsell gate) — every task below only completes the *existing* Agency+ Claims feature's filters/actions, none of which requires deciding whether to expose it more broadly. The broader "should Starter/Professional get a simpler Claims view" question remains open and is flagged again below for a future decision, not answered here.

## ✅ Completed (2026-08-18)

**Implementation summary:**
- **My Reports filters (`Dashboard.jsx`)** — a collapsible "Filters" panel (default closed, so the existing simple search+status bar is unchanged until opened) adds: Claim # (dedicated exact-ish substring match, separate from the general search box), Loss Type, Report Type, Creator (email substring), Created-date range, and Organization (a live CRM-client dropdown, shown only for Agency/Enterprise tiers that actually have CRM clients). All six are real, server-side filters — `GET /:id`'s list route (`backend/routes/reports.js`) gained `reportType`, `clientId`, `claimNumber`, and `creator` query params alongside the pre-existing `lossType`/`status`/`startDate`/`endDate`/`search`. **Creator is real but currently single-valued per account** — reports have no shared team-visibility pool yet (Phase 14 territory), so a new `createdByEmail` field is stored at generate time and is genuinely filterable, but every report in one account's own list necessarily shares the same creator today; this is honest, forward-compatible plumbing, not a fabricated feature.
- **My Reports row actions** — Open/View, Edit ("Review & edit"), and Full Preview stayed as direct icon buttons (unchanged, lowest regression risk); a new "More actions" kebab menu (`RowActionsMenu`, a new local component in `Dashboard.jsx`) adds Duplicate, Download PDF, Share (disabled with a tooltip unless the report is finalized), and Archive-or-Restore (whichever applies), with permanent Delete moved into the same menu. Bulk actions gained "Archive Selected" alongside the existing "Delete Selected."
- **Archive vs. permanent Delete, clearly separated** — Archive now has its own confirm dialog (non-danger style, "Archive this report? ... Nothing is deleted") distinct from permanent Delete's existing danger-styled dialog ("This permanently deletes... cannot be undone"). Archiving now also stores `preArchiveStatus` on the report so a new `POST /:id/restore` endpoint can put a restored report back to exactly the status it had before (draft or finalized) rather than always resetting to draft — protecting a finalized report's clean-export state (Golden Rule #3) across an archive/restore round trip.
- **Duplicate (new `POST /:id/duplicate`)** — copies the complete Phase 5 claim/inspection field set (all 19 fields plus the core claim/loss fields and `clientId`/`claimId`) into a brand-new draft; deliberately does **not** copy photos, supporting documents, AI-generated content, or review/approval state, per this phase's own risk note's recommendation (clean/no-photos duplicate). The new claim number gets a visible " (Copy)" suffix so the duplicate is never confused with the original in the list.
- **Claims list (`CRM.jsx`)** — added a real search box (claim #/loss type/address) and a loss-date range, both applied server-side via `crmService.getClaims`'s extended `startDate`/`endDate`/`archived` params (previously client-side status-only filtering over an unfiltered fetch). Added Edit (reusing `NewClaimModal` in a new edit mode — pre-fills the form and calls the already-existing `PUT /claims/:id` instead of `POST /claims`), Archive/Restore (a new `archived` boolean field, toggled via the same existing `PUT /claims/:id` — no new backend route needed, validated server-side via a new `express-validator` rule), and Delete (the existing, unmodified `DELETE /claims/:id`) row actions — all previously missing from the UI despite their backend endpoints already existing and going unused.
- **Claim workspace (`CRMClaimProfile.jsx`)** — converted from a single stacked-sections page into a real tabbed layout (Overview / Reports / Photos), with loading/error/retry states added throughout (previously just a bare `PageLoader` with no retry). The new **Photos tab** aggregates real photos across every report linked to the claim (via the existing `GET /reports/:id/photos` endpoint, the same one `ReportPhotoGallery` uses), with filters for Report, Category (dynamically populated from real `analysis.category` values), and Review Status — never fabricated data, and a report whose photos fail to load is skipped rather than failing the whole tab.
- **Real bug found and fixed:** duplicating a report set `editableContent` to the literal JavaScript `null` (the fresh draft's `content` field), which `SectionedReportEditor`'s `parseReportSections` (via `String(content)`) rendered as the literal text **"null"** in the editor — a real, visible data-integrity-looking bug, not a crash. Fixed by falling back to `''` at the one call site that didn't already guard against it (every other call site in `Dashboard.jsx` already did).

**Files touched:** `backend/routes/reports.js` (list-filter params, `/duplicate`, `/restore`, `createdByEmail`, `preArchiveStatus`), `backend/routes/crm.js` (claims list date/archived params, `archived` validator), `backend/services/crmService.js` (`getClaims` date-range + archived filtering), `frontend/src/services/api.js` (`reportsAPI.duplicate`/`.restore`), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (filters panel, `RowActionsMenu`, Archive/Restore/Duplicate handlers, the null-content fix), `frontend/src/pages/CRM.jsx` (Claims search/date/archived UI, Edit/Archive/Delete actions, `NewClaimModal` edit mode), `frontend/src/pages/CRMClaimProfile.jsx` (tabs, Photos aggregation panel, retry states).

**Testing/verification — automated:** Backend 135/135 tests (unchanged — new endpoints are straightforward Firestore CRUD in the same style as existing, already-tested routes; live verification below was judged more valuable than mocking Firestore for these), 0 lint errors, 44 warnings (+1 over the 43 baseline — one new `catch(err)` following the codebase's existing accepted pattern). Frontend 15/15 tests, 0 lint errors, 15 warnings (unchanged baseline), build succeeds.

**Testing/verification — live** (real Anthropic API + real Firebase project, Playwright/Chromium, 2 throwaway Agency-tier accounts + one seeded CRM client/claim, all fully deleted afterward): **My Reports filters — 13/13** (Loss Type, Report Type, Claim #, Status, Created-date range, Organization, and Creator each independently isolated the correct seeded report(s); Clear Filters correctly restored the full list). **My Reports actions — 15/15** across a single clean run (Duplicate → correct " (Copy)" claim number in the list and a clean empty editor after the null-content fix; Archive → non-danger dialog, disappears from default view, reappears under the Archived filter with a Restore action; Restore → returns to the All view as a draft; permanent Delete → gone entirely; Download PDF → a real file downloads; Share → enabled and copies a real working link for a finalized report, correctly disabled with a tooltip for an unfinalized one). **Claims tab — 10/10** (search finds/excludes correctly, an empty-result state renders, date range matches by loss date, Edit persists a changed field, Archive/Restore/the "Show Archived" toggle all work). **Claim workspace — 7/7** (loads directly by URL, all three tabs present, Reports tab shows the linked report(s), Photos tab correctly aggregated 2 real, AI-analyzed, reviewer-pending photos from a real end-to-end generated report linked to the seeded claim — with working Report/Category/Review-status filters — and the workspace survives a direct page refresh). **Cross-user authorization — 5/5** (a second account gets a clean "Claim unavailable" on the first account's claim URL with zero data leaked, and never sees the first account's reports in its own My Reports list). **Mobile (390×844) — 5/5** (My Reports and the claim workspace both render with zero horizontal overflow, zero console/network errors). Several 429s from this session's own rapid repeated test iterations were encountered and resolved each time via a backend restart (clears the in-memory rate-limiter store) — the same well-documented, self-inflicted testing artifact from Phases 9-11, confirmed not a regression by clean re-runs; the one pre-existing, unrelated stuck `processing` report from 2026-07-29 was reconfirmed present and left untouched throughout.

**Not covered live:** a genuinely pre-Phase-6 legacy-shaped report (no `photos[]` array) was not specifically re-tested against the new filters/Duplicate/Archive actions this session — these all operate on fields (`lossType`, `reportType`, `status`, `claimNumber`) that exist identically on legacy reports, and Duplicate explicitly never touches `photos`/`imagePaths` either way, so no legacy-specific behavior was introduced, but this wasn't independently re-confirmed with a real synthesized legacy record. Pagination beyond a single page of results was not exercised (the seeded test data fit on one page).

**Status:** ✅ **Completed (2026-08-18)**

## Phase 13 — Real Template Builder

**Objective:** Expand Templates from a flat per-user field-defaults bag into a real, spec-shaped template builder with Org/Flacron-provided tiers.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 32 (`PARTIALLY COMPLETE`) — current "templates" only save 7 wizard field values; no section/layout/required-field/org-logo/header-footer customization; no standalone `/templates` page; no shared organization or Flacron-provided templates.

**Scope:** Substantial new feature — new data model, new page, new builder UI.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Design a real Template data model: name, description, report sections (referencing Phase 9's section model), required fields, default wording, photo layout preference (from Phase 11), org logo/header/footer.
2. Add a `/templates` page listing My Templates / Organization Templates / Flacron Templates (seed a small set of real example templates: Residential Property, Water Loss, Roof, Wind/Hail, Fire, Commercial Property).
3. Add a `/templates/new` builder page for creating/editing a template's structure.
4. Add create/duplicate/edit/archive actions.
5. Wire the wizard/report-generation flow to optionally start from a selected template (structure + defaults), replacing or extending the current "My Templates" quick-load.

**Files/components likely affected:** New `frontend/src/pages/Templates.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/TemplateBuilder.jsx`, new backend `backend/routes/templates.js` (or expand `reports.js`), new Firestore collection design.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 9 (needs a real section model to templatize) and Phase 5 (fuller field set to templatize).

**Expected output:** Templates that actually define report structure, not just form defaults.

**Acceptance criteria:** Starting a new report from a template pre-populates both fields and section structure; org-level templates are visible to all org members; Flacron-provided example templates work out of the box.

**Testing/verification steps:** Create a custom template, use it to generate a report, confirm sections/structure match the template; confirm org-scoping (a template created by one org member is visible to teammates, not to other orgs).

**Risks/considerations:** This is one of the larger net-new features in the roadmap — consider whether the client wants full spec parity here or a reduced scope; recommend confirming appetite before starting.

**Scope decision made this session (2026-08-18):** implemented at full data-model breadth (task 1's complete list — sections/required fields/default wording/photo layout/org branding) but a reduced UI-polish scope consistent with this project's "ahead of Phase 30" precedent (Phases 9-12): a real, standalone `/templates` + `/templates/new` + `/templates/:id/edit` route set (not yet folded into `Dashboard.jsx`'s `activeView` architecture), plain-text section bodies in the builder (not the full TipTap rich-text editor from Phase 9 — a template section is a reusable *default*, not a specific report's final rich content with real photos), and no dedicated permissions-matrix UI beyond what Phase 14 will formalize (organization template edit rights reuse the existing `teamRole` value from `teams.js`, not a new roles system).

## ✅ Completed (2026-08-18)

**Implementation summary:**
- **New Firestore collection `templates`** (`backend/services/templateService.js`) — the real structural template data model: `name`/`description`, `scope` (`personal`/`organization`/`flacron`), `ownerId`/`organizationId`, `fields` (wizard defaults, extends the legacy 7-field set with `claimType`/`propertyType`/`inspectionType`/`weatherConditions`/`occupancyStatus`), `requiredFields` (a template-chosen subset of otherwise-optional wizard fields, enforced server-side on generate), `sections` (ordered `{title, body}` custom report sections, Phase 9-shaped), `photoLayout` (the exact Phase 11 `ExportOptionsModal` option set, reused as this template's export defaults), and `branding` (`companyName`/`footerText`/a Storage-backed `logoObjectPath`+`logoUrl`). This is entirely separate from, and does not touch, the pre-existing shallow `reportTemplates` collection/`/reports/templates` endpoints (backward compatibility preserved by construction, not by special-casing).
- **Organization model** — this codebase has no separate "organizations" collection; an enterprise team's owner IS the organization (`resolveOrganizationId(user) = user.teamOwnerId || user.uid`), and a team member's role comes from `teams.js`'s existing `teamRole` field (owner/admin/editor/viewer) captured at invite-accept. A solo (non-team) account's organization is just itself, so "Organization Templates" degrade gracefully to personal-only until that account actually has teammates — no new team infrastructure was needed.
- **Real Create/Edit/Duplicate/Archive/Restore/Delete** (`backend/routes/templates.js`, mounted at `/api/templates`, browser-only `authenticateToken` since templates aren't part of the public reports API) — `canView`/`canEdit` enforce scope rules server-side: personal templates are owner-only; organization templates are visible to every member sharing the same `organizationId` and editable by any role except `viewer`; Flacron templates are visible to everyone and never editable/archivable/deletable by anyone (a 403 `FORBIDDEN`, not a silent no-op). Duplicate always creates a **personal** copy owned by the duplicator regardless of the source's scope (including duplicating a Flacron template to get an editable starting point) and never inherits the source's Storage-backed logo (a different owner's/org's Storage path the duplicator has no rights to). Archive/Restore mirrors this codebase's existing Phase 12 convention (two explicit actions, not a toggle).
- **A real security fix baked into the design, not found after the fact:** `PUT /:id`'s branding update (`sanitizeBrandingText`) deliberately **never accepts a client-supplied `logoObjectPath`/`logoUrl`** — only the dedicated `POST /:id/logo` upload route (which controls the actual Storage write) can set them. Without this, a client could have pointed a template's "logo" at an arbitrary Storage object path it doesn't own (e.g. another user's private report photo) and had it silently downloaded into every export generated from that template. Covered by a regression test (`template-service.test.js`) and live-verified (see Testing below).
- **6 seeded Flacron example templates** (`FLACRON_TEMPLATE_DEFS` in `templateService.js`) — Residential Property, Water Loss, Roof, Wind/Hail, Fire, Commercial Property, exactly as named in this phase's task 2, each with real wizard-field defaults, one genuine custom section (e.g. Roof's "ROOF-SPECIFIC FINDINGS", written in the same cautious, non-conclusive language Golden Rule #2 already requires everywhere else), and at least one required field. Seeding is idempotent (a fixed-slug-id existence check per template, lazily run on first `/templates` list call and retried on failure) — safe on every server start without ever clobbering a future admin edit to one of these docs.
- **Wizard integration (`Dashboard.jsx`)** — a new "Start From a Report Template" section (distinct from, and additive to, the pre-existing "My Templates" quick-load) opens `TemplatePickerModal.jsx` (new shared component, tabs for My/Organization/Flacron), and selecting a template applies its `fields` to the wizard's existing `form` state plus a new `templateId` field already wired through the existing generic `Object.entries(form).forEach(...)` FormData submission — no new plumbing needed there. `POST /generate` (backend) resolves `templateId` server-side (never trusts a client-supplied structure), enforces the template's `requiredFields` (`400 TEMPLATE_REQUIRED_FIELD_MISSING` with the exact missing-field list, surfaced as a real toast in the wizard instead of the generic "Generation failed" fallback), and persists `templateId`/`templateName`/`templateGuidance`/`templateSections`/`exportDefaults`/`templateBranding` onto the new report.
- **Deterministic section-structure delivery, not left to prompt luck** — mirroring Phase 8's `insertPhotoObservations` precedent, a template's custom sections are **appended verbatim** to the AI-generated content after generation (`appendTemplateSections`, new in `richContent.js`), guaranteeing the acceptance criterion ("starting a new report from a template pre-populates... section structure") is exactly true rather than merely probable. A short `buildTemplateGuidance()` summary is *also* passed into `aiService.buildReportPrompt` as a style/consistency nudge (so the AI doesn't duplicate the upcoming sections), but that's a secondary nicety, never the sole mechanism relied on. Both `photoJobService.retryFailedAnalysis` and `regenerateFromPhotoReview` re-read the persisted `templateGuidance`/`templateSections` so a retry or a photo-review regenerate re-applies the same template structure instead of silently losing it.
- **Photo layout / export defaults** — a template's `photoLayout` is stored as `report.exportDefaults`; `ExportOptionsModal.jsx` now seeds its initial checkbox/layout state from it when present (falling back to the pre-existing hardcoded defaults otherwise) with zero call-site changes needed, since the modal already receives the full `report` object.
- **Branding fallback, made to actually work, not just stored** — `POST /:id/export`'s existing `companyName`/`reportFooter`/`logoObjectPath` resolution (`wlConfig` → `userData` → now **also** → `report.templateBranding`) only ever fills in when neither the org's white-label config nor the user's own profile branding is set, so a template default can never silently override an explicit choice. **A real bug found and fixed during live testing:** the export generators only ever *display* a custom `companyName`/footer when `hideFlacronBranding` is true (normally an enterprise white-label-only switch) — without also falling back `hideFlacronBranding` when a template's branding is the only branding source present, the template's companyName/footerText would resolve into the export options object but never actually render, making the feature silently inert. Fixed with a narrowly-scoped fallback (`!userData.company && !!templateBranding?.companyName`) that only ever engages for the template-branding case, leaving the pre-existing (and separately out-of-scope) `userData.company`-without-white-label inert-ness untouched — see Known Issues below.
- **New `backend/routes/templates.js` endpoints:** `GET /`, `GET /:id`, `POST /`, `PUT /:id`, `POST /:id/duplicate`, `POST /:id/archive`, `POST /:id/restore`, `DELETE /:id`, `POST /:id/logo`, `DELETE /:id/logo`.
- **New frontend:** `frontend/src/pages/Templates.jsx` (My/Organization/Flacron tabs, archived toggle, Create/Edit/Duplicate/Archive/Restore/Delete), `frontend/src/pages/TemplateBuilder.jsx` (Basics/Default Wording/Required Fields/Additional Report Sections with add/reorder/remove/Photo & Export Layout/Branding Defaults incl. logo upload), `frontend/src/components/TemplatePickerModal.jsx` (in-wizard picker).

**Files touched:** New — `backend/services/templateService.js`, `backend/routes/templates.js`, `frontend/src/pages/Templates.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/TemplateBuilder.jsx`, `frontend/src/components/TemplatePickerModal.jsx`, `backend/test/template-service.test.js`. Modified — `backend/config/storage.js` (`templateLogoObject`), `backend/utils/richContent.js` (`appendTemplateSections`), `backend/services/aiService.js` (`templateGuidance` in the prompt), `backend/services/photoJobService.js` (template propagation through generate/retry/regenerate), `backend/routes/reports.js` (`templateId` resolution + required-field validation in `POST /generate`, template fields persisted on the report doc, `templateBranding`/`hideFlacronBranding` fallback in `POST /:id/export`), `backend/server.js` (mount `/templates`), `frontend/src/services/api.js` (`templatesAPI`), `frontend/src/components/ExportOptionsModal.jsx` (`exportDefaults` seeding), `frontend/src/App.jsx` (3 new routes), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (wizard integration, nav link), `frontend/src/pages/EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` (nav link only).

**Testing/verification — automated:** Backend 159/159 tests pass (24 new, `template-service.test.js` — access-control matrix for all 3 scopes × owner/admin/viewer/outsider, sanitizers, the IDOR guard, `appendTemplateSections`, and Flacron seed-data coverage of all 6 named examples), 0 lint errors, 44 warnings (unchanged pre-existing baseline). Frontend 15/15 tests pass, 0 lint errors, 15 warnings (unchanged baseline), production build succeeds (`Templates`/`TemplateBuilder` chunk correctly).

**Testing/verification — live (real running dev server, real Firebase/Firestore project, real Anthropic API — no browser automation tool was available in this session, see "Not covered live" below):** two throwaway Node scripts drove the actual HTTP API end-to-end against 5 real throwaway Firestore accounts (an org owner + an org editor + an org viewer sharing one organization, plus two fully unrelated outsider accounts), authenticated via real signed JWTs through the same custom-JWT fallback path `authenticateToken` already supports, then fully deleted (Firestore docs + Storage objects) afterward:
- **49/49 checks passed** across two runs. Flacron seeding: all 6 named templates present and visible to a totally unrelated account. Personal template: create/visible-to-owner-only/invisible-to-outsider. Organization template: create/visible to every same-org member (owner+editor+viewer)/invisible to an outsider. Role enforcement: org viewer → `403` editing the org template; org editor → succeeds; an outsider → `403`/`404`. Flacron immutability: edit → `403`, delete → `403`, for every account including the template's own "creator" (none — `createdBy: null`). Duplicate: duplicating a Flacron template succeeds and the copy is `scope:'personal'`, owned by the duplicator. Archive/Restore: archived template hidden from the default list, reappears with `includeArchived=true`, restore succeeds. Required-field validation: generating from a template missing its declared required field → `400 TEMPLATE_REQUIRED_FIELD_MISSING` with the exact field name; providing it → `201`, and the created report correctly carries `templateId`/`exportDefaults` (matching the template's 4-per-page/no-cover-page layout)/`templateBranding`. Delete: succeeds, and the deleted template is genuinely gone (`404` on re-fetch).
- **A real end-to-end generation** (real Claude API call, ~90s pipeline) confirmed the template's custom section ("ORG CUSTOM SECTION") was deterministically appended to the generated `content`, verbatim body text and all — not merely requested via the prompt.
- **Logo upload/removal:** a real PNG buffer uploaded via `POST /:id/logo` produced a genuine Firebase Storage download-token URL that persisted across a re-fetch; `DELETE /:id/logo` cleared it; an outsider attempting to upload a logo onto another account's template got `403`.
- **Branding fallback, cross-format:** after finding and fixing the `hideFlacronBranding` gap above, a template's `companyName`/`footerText` were confirmed actually appearing in a real exported **HTML** file (and NOT the generic "FLACRONAI" label) and a real exported **DOCX**'s `word/document.xml` (inspected directly via `pizzip`, same technique Phase 9/11 used); a PDF export of the same report also succeeded (its `reportFooter` fallback logic is identical code, verified by inspection but not independently re-extracted from the PDF's compressed binary text this session).
- All test data (5 users, 4 templates, 3 reports, their Storage objects) was fully deleted afterward; `git status` confirms zero repo residue (all scratch verification scripts were run from outside the repo/backend directory specifically to avoid disturbing `nodemon`'s file watcher mid-background-job, after one earlier scratch file inside `backend/` was found to trigger a `nodemon` restart that killed an in-flight background report-generation job — a real, reproduced instance of Phase 7's own documented "no persistent job queue" tradeoff, not a Phase 13 defect, but a genuine hazard for testing methodology worth recording here).

**Not covered live:** no real-browser (Playwright/Chromium) click-through was performed this session — neither `chromium-cli` nor a Playwright installation was available in this environment, and installing one was judged disproportionate to do unprompted. This means the wizard's "Start From a Report Template" UI, the `/templates` and `/templates/new`/`/templates/:id/edit` pages' actual rendering, loading/empty/error states, and mobile responsiveness were verified by code review and a successful production build only, not by looking at a rendered page or checking the browser console — a real gap relative to Phases 8-12's Playwright-based sessions. The comprehensive live API-level testing above substitutes for correctness/security/integration verification but not for visual/rendering confirmation. **Recommend a follow-up session with browser automation available** (or the client's own manual click-through) before treating the frontend UI layer as fully QA'd, even though the underlying data/API layer it calls is now thoroughly verified. Also not covered: reusing a template that references a now-deleted organization (a member leaving/being removed from a team was out of this phase's scope, no such flow exists yet); pagination of the templates list (no account in this roadmap has enough templates to paginate).

**Known issue found, not fixed (pre-existing, out of Phase 13 scope):** a *non-enterprise* user's own `userData.company` (Settings → Profile → "Company Name," available to every tier) has the exact same latent inert-ness this phase found and fixed for template branding — it's stored and used for PDF `Author` metadata, but never actually shown in the visible header/footer text of any export unless the account also happens to be enterprise with white-label `hideFlacronBranding` set. This predates Phase 13 entirely (the `hideFlacronBranding` gate was already there) and was deliberately not touched, to avoid scope creep into an unrelated, already-shipped feature — flagged here for a future micro-task.

**Status:** ✅ **Completed (2026-08-18)**

## Phase 14 — Team Roles Expansion & Member Profiles

**Objective:** Expand the role model from 4 roles to the spec's 7, add a Suspend action, and add a member-profile view.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 33 (`PARTIALLY COMPLETE`) — only owner/admin/editor/viewer exist; spec wants Owner/Admin/Manager/Adjuster/Inspector/Reviewer/Viewer; no Suspend action; no member-profile page with reports/photos/activity stats.

**Scope:** Backend role enum/permissions + frontend team page and new member-profile page.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Extend the role enum in `backend/routes/teams.js` to the full 7-role set, and define what each additional role can/cannot do (this requires a permissions matrix decision — flag as open question if not already defined elsewhere in the product).
2. Add a Suspend action (distinct from Remove) — suspended members retain their record but lose access.
3. Build a standalone `/team` page (currently only inside `EnterpriseDashboard`'s tab) if the client wants team management available outside Enterprise tier — confirm tier-gating intent.
4. Add a member-profile view: name/email/role/reports-generated/photos-analyzed/recent-activity, with admin actions (change role, suspend, remove).

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/routes/teams.js`, `frontend/src/pages/EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`, new `frontend/src/pages/TeamMemberProfile.jsx`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None blocking.

**Expected output:** A complete role model and a real member-management experience.

**Acceptance criteria:** All 7 roles are assignable and enforced server-side; suspended members cannot log in/act but remain visible in the team list; member profile shows accurate activity stats.

**Testing/verification steps:** Assign each role and verify its actual permission boundaries server-side (not just UI hiding); suspend a member and confirm their access is blocked.

**Risks/considerations:** Defining what Manager/Adjuster/Inspector/Reviewer can each actually do differently is a product decision, not just a UI label change — do not implement without a clear permissions matrix.

**Permissions-matrix decision (2026-08-18, client-approved before implementation):** a least-privilege proposal was presented and explicitly approved before any code was written, per this phase's own risk note. Capability keys (`canGenerate`/`canEditReports`/`canApprove`/`canExport`/`canManageTemplates`/`canWhiteLabel`/`canBilling`/`canViewAllProfiles`/`canManageTeam`) live in one new shared module (`backend/utils/orgRoles.js`), consumed by `teams.js`, `reports.js`, and `templateService.js` alike:

| Capability | Owner | Admin | Manager | Adjuster | Inspector | Reviewer | Viewer | Editor (legacy) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Generate reports | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Edit report content | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (pre-approval only) | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Approve/finalize | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Export/download | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Manage org templates | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
| White-label/branding | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
| Billing | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
| View other members' profiles | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
| Manage team (invite/role/suspend/reactivate/remove) | ✅ any member | ✅ anyone except Owner/other Admins | ✅ only Adjuster/Inspector/Reviewer/Viewer/legacy-Editor | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |

Anti-escalation/anti-lockout rules approved alongside the matrix (all server-enforced, not just UI-hidden): no one can change their own role, suspend themselves, or remove themselves; only Owner can assign the Admin role; Admin cannot modify Owner or other Admins; Manager can only manage Adjuster/Inspector/Reviewer/Viewer/legacy-Editor and cannot assign Manager/Admin/Owner; ownership transfer is explicitly out of scope and was not simulated. `editor` is kept as a permanent legacy alias (adjuster-equivalent capabilities, labelled "Editor (legacy)") for members who predate this phase — never silently migrated, never assignable to a new invite or role-change, only ever changed by an explicit admin/manager/owner action.

**Status:** ✅ **Completed (2026-08-18)**

## ✅ Completed (2026-08-18)

**Implementation summary:**
- **Shared role/capability module (`backend/utils/orgRoles.js`, new)** — the single source of truth for the 7-role (+ legacy `editor`) capability matrix above, `resolveOrganizationId`/`resolveRole` (moved here from `templateService.js`, which now imports them instead of keeping its own copy), and the hierarchy helpers (`getAssignableRoles`/`getManageableTargetRoles`/`canAssignRole`/`canManageTargetRole`/`hasCapability`). `templateService.js`'s `canManageOrganizationTemplates` now delegates to `hasCapability(user, 'canManageTemplates')` — an explicit Owner/Admin/Manager allow-list, replacing the old "anything that isn't a viewer" rule that would have silently let every new role manage templates by default.
- **`backend/routes/teams.js` — rewritten role/member management.** `GET /teams/roles` now returns the caller's own `myRole`, `assignableRoles`, and `manageableTargetRoles` alongside the full matrix, so neither frontend nor a future integration ever has to duplicate the hierarchy. `GET /teams/members` (roster) is now gated to `canManageTeam || canViewAllProfiles` (Owner/Admin/Manager) instead of being silently empty for anyone but the literal account owner (a pre-existing bug: the old query filtered by `ownerId === req.user.uid`, which only the owner's own uid could ever match — no role, including the old Admin, could actually see the roster before this phase). New `GET /teams/members/:memberId` (accepting the literal `"me"` for self-lookup, including a synthesized record for the Owner, who has no member row) backs the new member-profile page, gated to self-view-always / other-view-requires-`canViewAllProfiles`, and returns real `stats` (reports generated, photos analyzed, recent activity — see below) plus a per-target `canManage` flag that already reflects the hierarchy for that specific pair of viewer/target roles. `POST /invite` and `PUT /members/:id/role` now check `canAssignRole`/`canManageTargetRole` (previously: any accepted "admin" role checked nothing beyond tier, and only the literal owner's uid could act on any member at all — no role-based team management existed prior to this phase, despite the 4-role model implying it did). New `POST /members/:id/suspend` and `POST /members/:id/reactivate`. `DELETE /members/:id` (remove) now actually reverts the removed member's own `users/{uid}` doc (`teamOwnerId`/`teamRole`/`teamPermissions`/`teamMemberId`/`teamMembershipStatus` cleared, `tier` reset to `starter`) — previously it only deleted the `enterpriseTeams` row and left the removed member's own account permanently retaining enterprise-tier access forever, a real, unrelated access-control bug this phase's own "remove must revoke access" requirement forced fixing. A role change now also writes the new role onto the target's own `users/{uid}` doc (`teamRole`/`teamPermissions`) — previously that only ever got written once, at invite-accept time, so changing a member's role via the API had literally never taken effect for that member's own live permission checks before this phase.
- **Suspension — all four requested safeguards, all server-enforced.** (1) `status: 'suspended'` on the `enterpriseTeams` doc (member stays visible to admins, per the acceptance criteria). (2) `tokenVersion` incremented on the member's own `users/{uid}` doc (kills any outstanding custom JWT immediately — they're otherwise stateless for 7 days). (3) A mirrored `teamMembershipStatus` field on that same user doc, checked by a new `isTeamMembershipSuspended()` in `middleware/auth.js` inside every authenticated-request code path (`authenticateToken`'s Firebase-ID-token branch, its custom-JWT branch, `authenticateApiKey`, and `optionalAuth`) — this is the actual "live per-request check": it costs zero extra Firestore reads, since it just reads a field off the user doc every request already fetches. (4) `getAuth().revokeRefreshTokens(uid)` (best-effort, never blocks the suspend call if it fails) — confirmed live via `tokensValidAfterTime` genuinely advancing on the real Firebase Auth user record. Reactivation clears the suspended flag/mirror field and deliberately does **not** re-issue or revoke anything further — suspend already forced a fresh login, so reactivation's very next login already reads the correct restored state; nothing "stale" can be resurrected.
- **Report-action capability gating (`backend/routes/reports.js`, new `requireTeamCapability` middleware from `middleware/auth.js`).** `POST /generate`, `POST /:id/analysis/retry`, `POST /:id/photos/regenerate`, and `POST /:id/images` now require `canGenerate`; `POST /:id/approve` requires `canApprove`; `POST /:id/export` and `GET /:id/download` require `canExport`; `PUT /:id`, `POST /:id/sections/suggest`, `POST /:id/sections/assist`, and `PUT /:id/photos/:photoId/review` require `canEditReports`. A solo (non-team) account is completely unaffected — `resolveRole()` always resolves them to `owner` (every capability true), so this only ever restricts genuine invited team members whose role lacks the capability. The one extra rule the approved matrix specifically called for — "Inspector can edit a report, but only before it's approved" — is implemented as a narrow addition inside `PUT /:id`'s existing reopen-on-edit logic: editing an already-reviewed report's content is blocked for any role that lacks `canApprove` (today, only Inspector), since re-opening an approved report is reserved for a role that could also re-approve it.
- **A real, flagged architectural limitation surfaced by this exact combination:** reports in this codebase have always been strictly `userId`-scoped (no shared team report pool exists yet — that's Phase 19 territory). Combined with Inspector's `canApprove: false`, this means a report an Inspector generates can **never** actually be approved by anyone — the Inspector themself is blocked by their own role, and nobody else can touch a report they don't own. This is flagged, not silently accepted: it's a direct, honest consequence of the approved least-privilege matrix layered onto the existing single-owner report model, and is exactly the kind of workflow Phase 19's "Supervisor review-request workflow" (assigning a report to a specific reviewer across accounts) is meant to eventually resolve. Not fixed here — fixing it would mean building cross-member report access, which is explicitly out of this phase's scope.
- **Frontend — `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s Team tab, made role-aware instead of owner-only.** The role guide, invite-role dropdown, and role-change dropdown are now all driven by `GET /teams/roles`'s live `assignableRoles`/full capability matrix (previously 3 hardcoded roles with a hand-maintained permissions list that would have silently gone stale the moment new roles existed). Members lacking `canManageTeam`/`canViewAllProfiles` (Adjuster/Inspector/Reviewer/Viewer/legacy-Editor) now see a plain "Team management is limited to Owners, Admins, and Managers" card instead of a broken/empty roster (this page was previously reachable — and silently broken — for any enterprise-tier login, not just the owner, since `/enterprise-dashboard` is gated only by tier). The hardcoded "Owner row" now renders the real org owner's info (`teamOwnerInfo`, newly returned by `GET /members`) rather than assuming the logged-in viewer always IS the owner. Row actions (Change Role/Suspend/Reactivate/Remove) are shown per-row only when the server-provided `manageableTargetRoles` actually allows the viewer to act on that specific member's role — the hierarchy is never re-implemented client-side, only consumed. A "View My Profile" link is always shown regardless of role.
- **Frontend — new standalone `frontend/src/pages/TeamMemberProfile.jsx`** at `/team/members/:memberId` (`:memberId` accepts the literal `me`), added to `App.jsx` ahead of the Phase 30 routing migration (same precedent as `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`/`Templates.jsx`). Shows name/email/role (with a "(legacy)" note when applicable)/status, two real stat tiles (Reports Generated, Photos Analyzed — sourced the same way `dashboard-summary` already does, a `.select()` projection summed in Node rather than a Firestore `.aggregate()`, to avoid needing a composite index this project has never provisioned), a real recent-activity list sourced from the existing `auditLogs` collection filtered by `actorUid` (fetched as a bounded window and sorted in Node rather than combining an equality filter with `.orderBy()`, for the identical index-avoidance reason), and — only when the server says `canManage: true` for this specific viewer/target pair — admin actions (change role, suspend/reactivate, remove, the last behind the existing shared `ConfirmDialog`). Loading/error (404 not-found vs. 403 access-denied get distinct messages)/empty (no recorded activity yet) states all present.
- **Legacy `editor` role, handled explicitly, never silently migrated.** `ROLES.editor` carries the exact same capabilities as `adjuster` (matching the old 4-role model's "editor could generate + export") and a `legacy: true` flag; it's excluded from `ASSIGNABLE_ROLES` everywhere (nobody, including Owner, can newly assign "editor" — confirmed live, `400 INVALID_ROLE`), but included in `MANAGEABLE_TARGET_ROLES` (an admin/manager/owner can still suspend/reactivate/remove/role-change an existing legacy-editor member) and in `ROLES` itself (so its capabilities/label resolve correctly everywhere, including the brand-new suspend/reactivate self-healing path for a member whose `users/{uid}` doc predates `teamMemberId`/`teamMembershipStatus` entirely).

**Files touched:** `backend/utils/orgRoles.js` (new), `backend/routes/teams.js` (rewritten), `backend/middleware/auth.js` (`isTeamMembershipSuspended`, `requireTeamCapability`, applied at every `req.user`-setting call site), `backend/routes/reports.js` (capability gates on 8 routes, the Inspector pre-approval-edit rule), `backend/services/templateService.js` (delegates role/org resolution + `canManageOrganizationTemplates` to the shared module), `frontend/src/services/api.js` (`getMember`/`suspendMember`/`reactivateMember`), `frontend/src/pages/EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` (role-aware Team tab), `frontend/src/pages/TeamMemberProfile.jsx` (new), `frontend/src/App.jsx` (new `/team/members/:memberId` route).

**Testing/verification — automated:** Backend `npm test` → **159/159 passing** (unchanged — the existing `teams.test.js`/`template-service.test.js` suites already covered the pure-logic helpers this phase relocated/extended, and continued passing against the refactored code with zero test changes needed), `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 44 warnings (unchanged baseline). Frontend `npm test` → **15/15 passing** (unchanged), `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 15 warnings (unchanged baseline — two warnings introduced mid-session by this phase's own new code were found and fixed before the final count), `npm run build` → succeeds (new `TeamMemberProfile` chunk builds correctly).

**Testing/verification — live, real API/real Firebase project, no browser-automation tool available this session either (same documented gap as Phases 7 and 13 — no `chromium-cli`/Playwright in this environment):** two throwaway Node scripts drove the real running HTTP API directly (never inside `backend/`, to avoid the same nodemon-restart-mid-job hazard Phase 13 first documented), against **10 real throwaway accounts** — an organization (`owner1`) with a real invited Admin, two Managers, an Adjuster, an Inspector, a Reviewer, a Viewer, a second Admin (`admin2`, to test admin-vs-admin hierarchy), a manufactured pre-Phase-14-shaped legacy Editor (deliberately created without the new `teamMemberId`/`teamMembershipStatus` fields, to prove the self-healing suspend path), plus a fully separate second organization (`owner2`) and one entirely unrelated starter-tier outsider — authenticated via real signed custom JWTs through `authenticateToken`'s existing fallback path (real Firebase Auth users were still created for every account, specifically so `revokeRefreshTokens()` had a real user record to act on). **87/87 checks passed** across two runs (a self-inflicted 100-req/15-min local rate-limiter trip split the run in two — see below, the same well-documented testing artifact every prior phase's live session has hit): every one of the 8 roles' capability flags matched the approved matrix exactly via `GET /teams/roles`; roster visibility correctly split Owner/Admin/Manager (200) from Adjuster/Inspector/Reviewer/Viewer (403 `TEAM_LIST_DENIED`); every role's own `GET /members/me` returned its own real record; profile-view authorization correctly distinguished self-view/`canViewAllProfiles`-view/denied, with `canManage` correctly `false` for a Manager viewing an Admin's profile and `true` for a Manager viewing an Adjuster's; the full anti-escalation suite passed — Admin blocked from assigning/modifying Admin, Manager blocked from assigning/modifying Manager or Admin, nobody can invite/assign `owner` or `editor`, nobody can change/suspend/remove themselves; a **live "no re-login needed" proof** specifically confirmed a never-reissued JWT loses `canManageTeam` the instant the owner changed that account's role, with zero token invalidation involved; suspension was proven immediate through **three independent mechanisms** — the original (now-stale-tokenVersion) JWT rejected with `401 TOKEN_REVOKED`, a freshly-minted JWT with the correct tokenVersion still rejected with `403 TEAM_ACCESS_SUSPENDED` (proving the live status-check specifically, independent of the tokenVersion bump), and Firebase's own `tokensValidAfterTime` genuinely advancing on the real Auth user record; the suspended member's record was confirmed still visible/queryable in Firestore throughout; reactivation restored access cleanly; removal was confirmed to genuinely revert the member's own account to a plain starter-tier solo account (tier-gated back out of every team route) rather than leaving them in a broken suspended-forever state; a second organization's owner got a clean `404` on every attempt to touch org1's members and saw a genuinely empty roster of their own (zero cross-org leakage); report-action gating was confirmed for Inspector (generate ✅, edit-draft ✅, approve/export ❌) including the specific pre-approval-edit rule against a manually-finalized report, for Reviewer (generate ❌), and for a full real Adjuster generate→wait-for-pipeline→approve→export lifecycle (a real PDF was genuinely exported to Storage and then deleted during cleanup); the legacy Editor generated/approved a real report with Adjuster-equivalent capabilities, was suspended/reactivated through the self-healing mirrored-field path (proving a record that predates this phase's own new fields still gets protected correctly), and was explicitly migrated to `adjuster` by an admin (never silently); and real `auditLogs` entries were confirmed for every invite/role-change/suspend/reactivate/remove action, with a member's own profile `recentActivity`/`reportsGenerated` stats confirmed to reflect genuinely real, freshly-generated data (not fabricated). All 10 accounts, their reports (4), one organization template, and one exported PDF Storage object were fully deleted afterward via a dedicated cleanup script (re-run once more at the end, confirming 0 residual QA records) plus a separate Storage-specific cleanup pass; `git status` confirms zero repo residue (only the two new files — `backend/utils/orgRoles.js`, `frontend/src/pages/TeamMemberProfile.jsx` — plus the expected modified files appear).

**A real, reproduced testing-methodology hazard, not a Phase 14 defect (same category Phase 13 first documented):** the full live-verification run's own request volume (invite/accept for 8 accounts, then ~85 further checks) exceeded this project's own `server.js` global rate limiter (100 requests/15 minutes per IP, a pre-existing Golden-Rule-#6 security feature, not something this phase touched) partway through, producing a burst of self-inflicted `429`s on the later template/legacy-editor checks. Unlike every prior phase's session, this session's environment did not permit killing/restarting the local dev server process to clear the limiter's in-memory store (blocked by the harness's own safety classifier, correctly, since that process wasn't started by this session) — so instead of a restart, the run was simply split into two passes (sections A–I, then J–M, skip-flagged via a `--from-j` CLI arg) separated by waiting out the limiter's 15-minute window twice. Both passes finished 100% green; this is a documented testing-infrastructure fact, not a product bug, and required no code change.

**Not covered live:** as with Phases 7 and 13, no real browser was available this session (no `chromium-cli`/Playwright), so `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s Team tab UI and the new `TeamMemberProfile.jsx` page's actual rendering, loading/empty/error states, and mobile responsiveness were verified by code review + a successful production build only, not by looking at a rendered page or a browser console — every underlying API call and permission boundary those pages exercise was independently verified live and directly, at the HTTP level, as detailed above. A genuine cross-account "Manager suspends an Adjuster who is mid-report-generation" race and true concurrent-request races were not tested (consistent with every prior phase's stated scope for concurrency testing). Ownership transfer was, per explicit client instruction, neither implemented nor simulated/tested.

## Phase 15 — General Analytics Page

**Objective:** Build a real `/analytics` page with the spec's metrics and charts, distinct from the existing CRM-scoped analytics.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 34 (`NOT IMPLEMENTED`) — no general analytics page exists; CRM analytics is scoped to clients/claims/appointments only.

**Scope:** New frontend page + backend aggregation endpoints.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Add backend aggregation for: Reports Generated, Photos Analyzed, Avg Report Completion Time, Reports Per User, Reports By Type, Reports By Status, Monthly Usage.
2. Build charts: Reports Over Time, Photos Processed Over Time, Team Usage (enterprise: team comparison).
3. Add the `/analytics` page and nav entry.
4. Consider sharing computation logic with Phase 4's dashboard metrics and the existing CRM analytics to avoid three parallel, drifting implementations of "reports over time."

**Files/components likely affected:** New `frontend/src/pages/Analytics.jsx`, new/expanded backend aggregation service, `frontend/src/App.jsx`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Photos Analyzed and completion-time metrics benefit from Phase 7/8 (accurate per-photo data); Reports By Type benefits from Phase 5 (real report-type field).

**Expected output:** A genuine cross-account analytics page.

**Acceptance criteria:** All listed metrics are computed from real data (no hardcoded/fabricated numbers per Golden Rule #1); charts render correctly with real time-series data.

**Testing/verification steps:** Cross-check displayed numbers against manual Firestore queries for a test account.

**Risks/considerations:** Avoid duplicating aggregation logic across Dashboard-home (Phase 4), CRM analytics (existing), and this page — consolidate into one shared metrics service if feasible.

**Status:** ✅ **Completed (2026-08-18)**

## ✅ Completed (2026-08-18)

**Scope decision (server-side, using Phase 14's role model directly, no new open question needed):** an enterprise-tier caller with `canViewAllProfiles` (Owner/Admin/Manager) sees organization-wide analytics aggregated across the whole team roster, plus a per-member "Reports Per User"/team-comparison breakdown; every other caller — restricted team roles (Adjuster/Inspector/Reviewer/Viewer/legacy Editor) and the large majority of accounts (every solo starter/professional/agency user, who can never have a team at all) — sees personal-scope analytics limited to their own `uid`. The `tier === 'enterprise'` check is a free, I/O-free short-circuit specifically so the extra `enterpriseTeams` roster read never runs for the common case.

**Implementation summary:**
- **New shared `backend/services/analyticsService.js`** — pure, dependency-free aggregation functions (`normalizeStatus`, `groupCount`, `computeAverageCompletionHours`, `buildMonthlyUsage`, `pickBucketGranularity`, `buildTimeSeries`, `buildReportsPerUser`, `resolveRange`) plus a thin Firestore-fetching orchestrator (`getAnalytics`), unit-tested exactly the way `crmService.buildDashboardAnalytics` already is (see `backend/test/analytics-service.test.js`, 12 tests). New `backend/routes/analytics.js` exposes it as `GET /api/analytics` (browser-only `authenticateToken`, matching the `teams.js`/`templates.js` precedent for dashboard-only features), mounted under both `/api` and `/api/v1` in `server.js`.
- **Every spec'd metric delivered from real data, nothing fabricated:** Reports Generated, Photos Analyzed, Average Report Completion Time (createdAt→reviewedAt for finalized reports, with legacy `completed`/`approved` folded into "finalized" — matching `reports.js`'s own `isReviewed()` definition; returns `null`/"not enough data" rather than a fake `0` when no report has actually completed review yet, per Golden Rule #1), Reports Per User (organization scope only, zero-filled for a roster member who hasn't generated anything, never mis-attributing a since-removed member's historical reports to anyone), Reports By Type, Reports By Status, Monthly Usage (a fixed rolling 12-calendar-month view, **deliberately independent of the page's own date-range filter** — a multi-month trend is inherently a different kind of view than the range-filtered headline metrics, not an inconsistency), Reports Over Time and Photos Processed Over Time (gap-free daily/weekly/monthly buckets, granularity auto-scaled to the selected range's width so a chart never tries to render hundreds of daily bars for an "All Time" view), and Team Usage/team-comparison (the org-wide Reports-Over-Time series plus the Reports-Per-User breakdown — deliberately reused rather than computed as a redundant 5th dataset).
- **Date-range filtering:** 7/30/90/365-day presets, "All Time," and an explicit custom start/end date picker.
- **Timezone/date-boundary accuracy, without any IANA-timezone-library dependency:** the frontend computes real local-midnight-to-local-midnight boundaries using plain `Date` local-time semantics (the browser's own timezone does the conversion correctly for free) and sends them as UTC ISO instants, plus a `tzOffset` minutes value (`-new Date().getTimezoneOffset()`) on every request; the backend shifts each report's timestamp by that offset before reading calendar day/week/month boundaries for chart bucketing. **A real bug caught by this module's own unit test before shipping:** the shift helper's sign convention was initially inverted (`date - tzOffsetMinutes*60000` instead of the correct `date + tzOffsetMinutes*60000`), which would have bucketed every report into the wrong local day for any non-UTC caller — a dedicated test asserting the correct bucket for a timestamp deliberately chosen to sit right at a UTC-day boundary caught it immediately; fixed, and the test's own fixture (which had also encoded the same wrong assumption) was corrected alongside it.
- **Consolidation decision, made deliberately and documented, not skipped:** did **not** refactor `crmService.js`'s already-shipped, already-tested `buildDashboardAnalytics` or `reports.js`'s intentionally-minimal `dashboard-summary` endpoint to call into the new service. `crmService.js`'s existing turnaround-hours calculation only counts `finalized`/`approved` statuses (not `completed`), while the new, more-complete definition (matching `isReviewed()`) also counts `completed` — silently merging them would have changed already-shipped, live-QA'd production behavior for any account with legacy `completed`-status reports, which is outside this phase's scope. `analyticsService.js`'s own header comment documents this reasoning so a genuine future consolidation (if ever justified) is a straightforward refactor, not a guess.
- **Query-performance/scale approach:** `.select()` field-masked projections (never full report documents, so a large `content` field is never read/deserialized for a page that only ever needs counts and dates), a Firestore `userId in [...]` query chunked at 30 per the documented Firestore limit for organization-wide fetches, and the tier-gated short-circuit described above so the common case (a solo account) never triggers the extra roster read at all.
- **Frontend — new standalone `frontend/src/pages/Analytics.jsx`** at `/analytics`, added to `App.jsx` ahead of the Phase 30 routing migration (same precedent as `Templates.jsx`/`TeamMemberProfile.jsx`). Range-filter chips + custom date pickers, 3 headline stat cards (with an honest "Not enough data yet" state for completion time), 2 breakdown bar-charts (By Type/By Status), a 12-month usage bar chart, 2 horizontally-scrollable time-series bar charts (Reports/Photos), and a conditional Team Comparison section (organization scope only) — all built in the same plain-Tailwind-bars charting style `CRM.jsx`'s existing dashboard already uses, so **no new chart-library dependency was added**; `chart.js`/`react-chartjs-2` remain the same pre-existing, zero-import dead dependencies documented in `CLAUDE.md` before this phase. An amber banner distinguishes a genuine empty state ("no reports in this range yet," with a link to widen the range or generate a first report) from the honest zero values shown elsewhere.
- **Navigation entry:** a new `href`-based "Analytics" sidebar item added to both `Dashboard.jsx` and `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s existing sidebars (matching the already-established Templates-link pattern exactly), available to every tier — this is the *general* analytics page, not an enterprise-only feature, so it was deliberately not gated by `requiredTier` in `ProtectedRoute`.
- **A pre-existing, unrelated documentation-accuracy fix made in passing:** the Phase Summary Table's Phase 13 row still read "Not Started" despite Phase 13 having been completed in session 18 (per `PROGRESS.md`) — corrected while editing this same table for Phase 15, rather than left stale.

**Files touched:** `backend/services/analyticsService.js` (new), `backend/routes/analytics.js` (new), `backend/server.js` (mounted the new route), `backend/test/analytics-service.test.js` (new, 12 tests), `frontend/src/pages/Analytics.jsx` (new), `frontend/src/services/api.js` (`analyticsAPI.get`), `frontend/src/App.jsx` (new `/analytics` route), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (sidebar nav entry), `frontend/src/pages/EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` (sidebar nav entry).

**Testing/verification — automated:** Backend `npm test` → **171/171 passing** (12 new, `analytics-service.test.js` — one of which caught the real timezone-sign bug described above before it ever reached live testing), `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 44 warnings (unchanged baseline). Frontend `npm test` → **15/15 passing** (unchanged), `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 14 warnings (no new warnings introduced by this phase's own code), `npm run build` → succeeds (new `Analytics` chunk, 10.57kB gzip 3.32kB).

**Testing/verification — live, real Firebase project, no browser-automation tool available this session either (same documented gap as Phases 7/13/14 — no `chromium-cli`/Playwright in this environment):** a throwaway Node script (run from outside `backend/`, avoiding the established nodemon-restart-mid-job hazard) seeded **7 real throwaway accounts** directly via the Firebase Admin SDK — an organization (`owner1`) with a real Admin, Adjuster, and Viewer team member; a fully separate second organization (`owner2`, specifically to test cross-organization isolation); a solo professional-tier account with no team at all; and a starter-tier account with zero reports ever — plus **9 real report documents** with hand-chosen `createdAt`/`reviewedAt`/`status`/`reportType`/`imageCount` values, so every expected number could be calculated by hand ahead of time rather than inferred from the code under test. Authenticated via real signed custom JWTs through `authenticateToken`'s existing fallback path, driving **55/55 checks, all passing**, against the live HTTP API (confirmed working under both the legacy `/api` and versioned `/api/v1` prefixes):
- Every headline number (`reportsGenerated`/`photosAnalyzed`/`avgCompletionHours`, including the exact averaged-hours arithmetic) matched hand-calculated expected values exactly across a 30-day default range, a 90-day range (correctly picking up an older report the 30-day range excluded), an all-time range (correctly folding a legacy `completed`-status report into the "finalized" bucket and into the completion-time average), and a custom range deliberately narrow enough to isolate exactly one specific report.
- Organization-wide scope correctly activated only for the privileged Owner role, with the correct team size and a correctly zero-filled Reports-Per-User row for a roster member (Viewer) who had never generated anything — and the sum of every roster member's `reportsGenerated` in that breakdown exactly equalled the headline total, proving the unrelated second organization's report was never mixed in.
- The restricted Adjuster role — a member of `owner1`'s own organization — correctly saw only their own personal-scope data, never the Owner's or Admin's reports, despite being on the same team; `reportsPerUser` was correctly `null` for them (no org-wide visibility).
- The solo professional-tier account and the enterprise-tier-but-restricted Viewer role both correctly took the personal-scope path — proving the tier/capability short-circuit works from both directions (no team ever existed vs. a team exists but this role can't see it).
- The zero-report starter-tier account got an honest all-zero/all-null response with zero crashes, including a 12-entry Monthly Usage array of genuine zeroes (not an empty/missing array).
- **Cross-organization isolation was proven in both directions:** `owner2`'s own report never appeared anywhere in `owner1`'s org-wide totals, and `owner1`'s organization's 6 reports never appeared in `owner2`'s own (correctly personal-scoped, since `owner2` never invited a team) analytics.
- Unauthenticated and garbage-token requests correctly received `401` (`NO_TOKEN`/`INVALID_TOKEN`).
- A live suspend of the Adjuster team member, performed mid-test via a direct Firestore write mirroring `teams.js`'s own suspend logic, proved the exact same Phase 14 defense-in-depth mechanics apply to this brand-new route: a token minted **after** suspension (with the fresh, post-suspend `tokenVersion`) still correctly received `403 TEAM_ACCESS_SUSPENDED` from the live per-request status check, while the original pre-suspension (now stale-`tokenVersion`) token correctly received `401 TOKEN_REVOKED` — both mechanisms independently confirmed working, not just one masking the other.

All 7 accounts, their 9 reports, and their 3 `enterpriseTeams` membership docs were fully deleted afterward via a dedicated cleanup script, re-verified immediately afterward with a zero-residue query pass (0 leftover reports/user docs/membership docs, 0 remaining Firebase Auth users); `git status` shows only the expected new/modified files with zero stray scratch files in the repo.

**Not covered live:** as with every prior phase in this environment, `Analytics.jsx`'s actual rendering, chart horizontal-scroll behavior, tooltip display, and mobile responsiveness were verified by code review + a successful production build only, since no browser automation tool was available — every underlying number and permission boundary the page displays was independently verified live and directly at the HTTP level, as detailed above.
