## Phase 24 — Photo Quality Warnings, Ordering, Grouping & Annotations

**Objective:** Add the remaining per-photo enrichment features the full spec calls for that aren't upload-mechanics (Phase 6) or AI-classification (Phase 8): low-quality detection, persisted ordering, area/room grouping, and drawing-based annotations.

**Reason this phase is required (2026-08-16 full-spec audit):** spec §22 (Photo Analysis feature list) wants "duplicate detection, low-quality image warning... photo ordering... captions, photo annotations"; spec Page 32 (§ Photo Documentation Layout) assumes each photo has an area/location and observation for layout purposes (partly Phase 8, partly this). **Verified via direct code search: none of low-quality detection, persisted photo ordering, room/area grouping UI, or any annotation/drawing tooling exist anywhere in the codebase.** Annotation was also a requirement in the pre-`PHASES.md` backlog (`TASKS.md` T-2.4) that was silently dropped when this roadmap was created from a summary spreadsheet audit — this phase restores it.

**Scope:** Backend: a quality-heuristic check on upload, a `position` field on photo records, a `roomOrArea` tag field. Frontend: a low-quality warning UI, drag-to-reorder in the wizard/gallery, a simple grouping/filter view, and a canvas-based annotation tool (arrows/circles/highlight/measurements/timestamps) on the full-size photo preview.

**Exact tasks:**
1. **OPEN QUESTION:** choose a low-quality heuristic approach — a cheap client/server-side check (resolution below a threshold, or a fast blur-detection algorithm like Laplacian variance via `sharp`) vs. asking the AI vision model itself to flag poor image quality as part of its analysis (only available once Phase 8's per-photo structured output exists). Recommend the cheap heuristic now (works independently of Phase 7/8), with an AI-based refinement later if wanted.
2. Add the chosen quality check to `photoBatchProcessor.js`, producing a `qualityWarning: true/false` (+ reason) field on the per-photo record; surface it as a visible warning badge in the wizard and gallery (distinct from the existing `failed`/`duplicate` statuses — a low-quality photo still uploads and analyzes, it's just flagged).
3. Add a persisted `position` (integer) field to the per-photo record; add drag-to-reorder in the wizard's grid/list view and the read-only gallery, persisting the new order via a small `PATCH /:id/photos/reorder` endpoint (or similar).
4. Add a `roomOrArea` tag (free text or, once Phase 8's taxonomy exists, a dropdown from that same list) with a simple grouping/filter view in the gallery ("show only Kitchen photos").
5. Build an annotation tool: a canvas overlay on the full-size preview (already exists as a modal in Phase 6) supporting arrows, circles/rectangles, freehand highlight, straight-line measurements, and an auto-applied capture timestamp; persist annotations as a lightweight vector/shape-list JSON alongside the photo record (never bake annotations into the stored image bytes, so they stay editable and don't corrupt the original/optimized tiers from Phase 6's addendum).

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js`, `backend/routes/reports.js` (new reorder endpoint, annotation persistence), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (wizard grid, gallery), new `frontend/src/components/PhotoAnnotator.jsx`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Soft dependency on Phase 6 (per-photo record must exist — it does) and Phase 8 (a real classification taxonomy makes grouping more useful, but a free-text tag works without it).

**Expected output:** Photos can be flagged for quality, manually reordered, grouped by area, and annotated — all persisting correctly.

**Acceptance criteria:** A deliberately blurry/low-resolution test photo shows a quality warning without being rejected; drag-reordering persists across a page reload; grouping by area correctly filters the gallery; an annotation (e.g. a circle + measurement) saves, reloads correctly, and does not alter the underlying image file.

**Testing/verification steps:** Upload a genuinely low-quality photo and confirm the warning; reorder photos, refresh, confirm order persisted; tag several photos by area and confirm the group filter; annotate a photo, reload the report, confirm the annotation is still there and the original/optimized/thumbnail image files are still byte-identical to before annotating.

**Risks/considerations:** Keep annotations as a separate data layer (never rasterized into the image) so Phase 6's original-preservation fix isn't undone by this phase. Canvas-based drawing tools are a real, non-trivial frontend feature — consider a lightweight existing library (e.g. `fabric.js`, `react-konva`) rather than hand-rolling canvas math, similar to Phase 9's rich-text-editor library decision.

**Status:** Completed (2026-08-19, session 27)

**Implementation summary:**
- **Open Question #1 resolved per explicit client instruction this session:** a deterministic server-side heuristic, computed on upload with `sharp` (already a dependency, no new one added) — resolution below 800×600, OR a Laplacian-variance blur score below 50 (empirically calibrated: a synthetic sharp fixture scores ~5461, the same fixture blurred scores ~32; both documented in `photoQuality.js`'s header and covered by dedicated unit tests). Never rejects a photo — `qualityWarning`/`qualityReasons`/`qualityMetrics` are purely informational, surfaced everywhere a photo appears (wizard, gallery, Photo Library). The wizard additionally gets an immediate CLIENT-side low-resolution check (from the same decode pass already used for corrupt-file detection) — blur has no cheap client equivalent, so that half only appears after the real upload/analysis pass, honestly documented as such.
- **Library decision (documented, not guessed):** `react-konva`@18.2.16 + `konva`@9.3.22 for the annotation canvas — the only React-idiomatic option compatible with this project's React 18.2 (the latest `react-konva` line requires React 19); `fabric.js` was considered and passed over for its more imperative API. `exif-reader`@2.0.3 (tiny, zero-dependency, sharp's own documented companion) was added for EXIF capture-time parsing — the only other new dependency.
- **Backend:** `photoQuality.js` (heuristic), `photoCaptureTime.js` (EXIF `DateTimeOriginal` extraction from the ORIGINAL bytes only — the display/thumbnail tiers have EXIF stripped by Phase 6's own re-encode — with a trustworthiness check rejecting clock-reset defaults like 1970/2000-01-01 and implausible future timestamps). `photoBatchProcessor.js` now stamps every uploaded photo with `position` (sequential from a caller-supplied `startPosition`, continuing correctly across `POST /:id/images` add-on batches), `qualityWarning`/`qualityReasons`/`qualityMetrics`, `capturedAt`, and initializes `roomOrArea`/`annotations` to `null`. `photoJobService.js` gained `reorderPhotos` (exact-permutation validation, no stale-check — a lost reorder is cheap to redo, unlike a lost annotation), `updatePhotoAnnotations` (full-list replace, bounded to 60 shapes/200 points-per-shape, numeric/type/color/label validation, `expectedUpdatedAt` optimistic-concurrency guarding against silently clobbering a concurrent editor), and a `set_area` branch on the existing `updatePhotoReview` (reused rather than a new route — works even when AI analysis is queued/unavailable). Two new routes: `PATCH /:id/photos/reorder`, `PUT /:id/photos/:photoId/annotations`, both on the exact dual-path authorization already established by the sibling review route (owner+`canEditReports`, or a Phase 19 `review`-tier grantee) plus the same legacy-id guard. `GET /:id/photos` and the export appendix builder (`buildAppendixPhotoList`) both now sort by `position` (falling back to array index for pre-Phase-24 photos), so the reviewer's saved order is what review, export, and the Photo Library actually see. `photoLibrary.js`/`routes/photos.js` expose the new fields and a `roomOrArea` filter (with an `__unassigned__` sentinel).
- **Frontend:** a dependency-free `useDragReorder` hook (Pointer Events, not the HTML5 Drag-and-Drop API, specifically because Pointer Events unify mouse and touch in one code path) wired into both the wizard's staged (pre-upload, client-only) grid/list and the persisted post-generation gallery; a `QualityWarningBadge` component; `PhotoAnalysisPanel` (PhotoReview.jsx) extended with a room/area select (Phase 8 taxonomy + custom free text) and an Annotate/Edit Annotations entry point, working independently of whether AI analysis exists yet; a new `PhotoAnnotator.jsx` (toolbar: Select/Move, Arrow, Rectangle, Circle, Highlight/freehand, Measurement, 5-color picker, Undo, Delete, Clear all, Save/Cancel) rendering shapes as normalized (0..1) coordinates over the real full-size image so they stay correctly positioned regardless of display size, with measurement labels always phrased as "~N px (image pixels, not a physical measurement)" — never a physical unit, since no calibration scale exists anywhere in this product — and a capture-time badge that plainly distinguishes a real EXIF timestamp from the honest upload-time fallback. Photo Library gained an area filter (taxonomy + real custom values actually present in the loaded window + an "Unassigned" option) and a "Group by Area" toggle, explicitly labeled as applying to the currently loaded page, not the whole library at once.
- **Verified end-to-end against the real Firebase/Anthropic-backed local dev stack** (both dev servers, two throwaway Firebase Auth+Firestore accounts, real Claude vision analysis, real Playwright + this machine's cached Chromium — no dedicated browser tool was available in-session, so Playwright was installed globally and driven directly via Node scripts): wizard upload with a normal/blurry/low-resolution/rotated-normal photo set, client-side low-res flagging, a real mouse-dragged wizard reorder that landed correctly at `position:0` after generation, real Claude vision analysis (which independently flagged the same synthetic images as "may be out of focus" in its own prose — an incidental cross-check that the test fixtures were realistic), server-side quality flags matching expectations exactly (blurry→`blurry`, low-res→both `low_resolution` and `blurry`, normal→neither), area tagging (taxonomy value, custom value, clearing, over-length rejection) via both direct API and the real gallery UI, persisted reorder via the API with exact-permutation and legacy-id rejection, a full annotation lifecycle (5 shapes of every type saved/persisted, a stale `expectedUpdatedAt` correctly rejected with 409, a valid one allowing an undo/delete-shrink, oversized/malformed payloads rejected with 400) with a REAL mouse-drawn arrow through the actual UI (draw → undo → redraw → save, zero console errors, zero failed requests), byte-for-byte SHA-256-verified original/display/thumbnail image bytes unchanged after annotating, Photo Library's area filter/grouping against real tagged data, full cross-account authorization isolation (a second, unrelated account got 404 on every new endpoint and never saw the first account's photos in its own library, plus a bare 401 with no token at all), and a real mobile viewport (iPhone 13 profile) pass — no horizontal overflow on the dashboard or Photo Library, the Annotate button and toolbar reachable via genuine CDP touch taps, and a drag gesture at mobile viewport size correctly drawing a shape. One test-script-only bug was found and fixed before it produced a false failure (a QA script forcibly removed the cookie-consent banner via raw DOM `.remove()`, which desynced it from React and crashed the app on next render — switched to only ever dismissing it through its own real button). A pre-existing, already-documented UX defect (the cookie-consent banner can block real clicks, including login, until dismissed) was reproduced again on the mobile login form specifically and worked around in the test script, not fixed here (unrelated to this phase). All QA accounts, the generated report, its Storage objects, and the throwaway scripts were deleted afterward; a temporary local-only rate-limit increase (100→5000 req/15min, needed only because repeated full-flow browser test runs exhausted the real limiter) was reverted to its original value before finishing — confirmed via `git diff` showing no residual change.
- **Tests:** 279/279 backend (`node --test`, 21 new: `photo-quality.test.js`, `photo-capture-time.test.js`, `photo-annotations-validation.test.js`, plus new/extended cases in `photo-batch-processor.test.js`), 0 backend lint errors (61 pre-existing warnings, unchanged). 27/27 frontend (Vitest, 10 new in `photoAnnotations.test.js`), 0 frontend lint errors (14 pre-existing warnings, unchanged), a clean production build (Konva/react-konva land in their own lazy chunk, `PhotoAnnotator-*.js` ~317KB/97KB gzip, loaded only when Dashboard or Photo Library is actually visited — no cost to the public/marketing bundle).
- **Files created:** `backend/utils/photoQuality.js`, `backend/utils/photoCaptureTime.js`, `backend/test/photo-quality.test.js`, `backend/test/photo-capture-time.test.js`, `backend/test/photo-annotations-validation.test.js`, `frontend/src/hooks/useDragReorder.js`, `frontend/src/utils/photoAnnotations.js`, `frontend/src/components/PhotoAnnotator.jsx`, `frontend/src/__tests__/photoAnnotations.test.js`.
- **Files modified:** `backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js`, `backend/services/photoJobService.js`, `backend/routes/reports.js`, `backend/routes/photos.js`, `backend/utils/photoLibrary.js`, `backend/test/photo-batch-processor.test.js`, `backend/package.json`/`package-lock.json` (`exif-reader`), `frontend/src/components/PhotoReview.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/PhotoLibrary.jsx`, `frontend/src/services/api.js`, `frontend/package.json`/`package-lock.json` (`konva`, `react-konva`).
- **Not done, by design:** shape resize handles after creation (move/delete/redraw only — a deliberate, disclosed scoping choice, not an oversight); a genuine on-device multi-touch drag-gesture simulation (Playwright has no high-level touch-drag primitive, and hand-constructed synthetic touch/pointer events don't trigger a real browser's touch-to-pointer synthesis the way hardware/CDP input does — real taps WERE verified via CDP, and the drag gesture itself was verified at mobile viewport size via mouse, which exercises the identical Pointer-Events-based handler code with no separate touch-only branch to miss; this is a real, disclosed tooling gap, not a product one).

## Phase 25 — Supporting Document Text Extraction & FLACRON ENGINE Integration

**Objective:** Extract text from uploaded supporting documents (PDF/DOC/DOCX/TXT) and feed it into the report-generation AI prompt, closing the gap Phase 5 deliberately left open.

**Reason this phase is required:** Phase 5's own completion notes are explicit: "Text extraction / AI-prompt integration for uploaded documents deliberately NOT implemented... Deferred to a future Phase 7/8-style follow-up." Spec §11 ("FLACRON ENGINE... Text Processing... Supporting documents") and the wizard's Step 6 ("3 Documents Processed") both assume document content actually reaches the report draft — today, documents are stored and downloadable only; their content is invisible to `generateReport()`.

**Scope:** Backend text-extraction utilities per format + AI-prompt integration; no frontend change expected beyond possibly showing "N documents processed" more accurately in the generation-progress UI.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Add text extraction per document type: PDF (a library such as `pdf-parse`), DOCX (a library such as `mammoth` or `docx` reading), TXT (already plain text — no library needed). Store extracted text on the document record (or re-extract on demand — decide based on cost/size).
2. Cap extracted text length fed into the prompt (documents can be large; avoid blowing the AI context budget) — truncate with a clear "[additional content truncated]" marker if needed.
3. Wire extracted text into `buildReportPrompt()`/`generateReport()`'s input alongside claim/inspection/photo-analysis data, clearly labeled as "from supporting document: {filename}" so the AI (and a human reviewer reading the prompt/output) can trace claims back to their source.
4. **(Optional, from the old pre-`PHASES.md` backlog, T-2.11)** Voice-to-text: allow recording a voice note during the wizard's Documentation step, transcribe it, and treat the transcript the same as a text document above — keep the original audio/transcript available for the human reviewer, never silently replace it with an AI "cleaned up" version without keeping the original.

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/utils/documentValidation.js` (or a new sibling `documentTextExtraction.js`), `backend/services/aiService.js` (`buildReportPrompt`), `backend/routes/reports.js` (`/generate`).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 5 (document upload/storage already exists — this phase only adds the missing text-extraction/prompt-integration step).

**Expected output:** Uploaded documents' actual content measurably influences the generated report draft.

**Acceptance criteria:** Uploading a PDF containing a specific detail (e.g. a policy number, a specific damage note) and generating a report produces a draft that reflects that detail; a document that fails to extract (corrupt/password-protected/scanned-image-only PDF) degrades gracefully (report still generates, without that document's content) rather than failing the whole request.

**Testing/verification steps:** Generate a report with a real PDF/DOCX containing distinctive text, confirm it appears in the AI-generated draft; test a corrupt/password-protected document and confirm graceful degradation, not a hard failure.

**Risks/considerations:** Golden Rule #2 still applies — extracted document text is *input* to the AI, not itself a verdict; make sure the prompt framing doesn't let document content smuggle in unverified claims as if the AI generated/endorsed them. Scanned-image PDFs (no embedded text layer) will extract empty/garbage text — decide whether OCR is in scope (likely not for v1 — flag as a future enhancement if the client wants it).

**Status:** Not Started

---

## Phase 26 — Help Center & Contact Support

**Objective:** Build the `/help` Help Center and `/support` Contact Support pages.

**Reason this phase is required:** Confirmed via direct search — neither page nor route exists anywhere in the codebase, and no other phase in this roadmap mentions either one.

**Scope:** Two new frontend pages; a light backend endpoint for the Contact Support form (can likely reuse the existing sales/contact-form infrastructure pattern, e.g. `salesAPI.contact`, rather than building a parallel system).

**Exact tasks:**
1. Build `/help` with the spec's category list (Getting Started, Creating Reports, Uploading Photos, FLACRON ENGINE, Templates, Team Management, Billing, Security, API) — a simple categorized article/FAQ layout; content can start minimal (a handful of real articles per category) rather than exhaustive, to avoid this becoming a content-writing project disguised as an engineering one.
2. Build `/support` with the spec's form (Name, Email, Category, Subject, Message, Attachments) submitting to a backend endpoint that stores the request and notifies the team (reuse `emailService.js`'s existing branded-email pattern) — attachments need size/type validation matching the existing document-upload pattern (`documentValidation.js`).
3. Add both to the top bar / sidebar Help entry (spec §35) and to the public/authenticated navigation as appropriate.

**Files/components likely affected:** New `frontend/src/pages/HelpCenter.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/ContactSupport.jsx`, a new or extended backend route (e.g. `backend/routes/support.js`, or extending `backend/routes/sales.js`'s contact pattern), `frontend/src/App.jsx` (routes), `frontend/src/components/Navbar.jsx`/`Dashboard.jsx` (nav entries).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None blocking — this is a self-contained, additive feature.

**Expected output:** Users have a real place to find help and contact support instead of neither existing.

**Acceptance criteria:** Both pages render, are reachable from navigation, and the support form successfully creates a real, notified support request when submitted.

**Testing/verification steps:** Submit the support form with and without an attachment, confirm the request is stored/delivered; click through every Help Center category.

**Risks/considerations:** Decide the Help Center's content scope with the client up front (a handful of real articles vs. a fuller knowledge base) — this is as much a content-authoring task as an engineering one, and could balloon if not scoped.

## Phase 27 — Application-Wide Mobile, Empty/Loading/Error States & Performance Completion

**Objective:** A systematic completeness pass across every existing page for mobile responsiveness, empty/loading/error states, and performance, rather than relying on each feature phase to remember these individually.

**Reason this phase is required:** Spec §§21, 23, 24, 25, 45 describe requirements that cut across the *entire* application rather than belonging to one feature — general mobile responsiveness (with priority on claim/photo/review flows), consistent empty-state messaging, FLACRON-branded loading states (not generic spinners for long AI operations), user-friendly error states with Retry/Continue actions, and performance practices (lazy loading, real pagination, virtualized grids, avoiding loading 100 full-resolution photos at once). Individual phases each get their own page/feature right, but nothing currently re-verifies the *whole app* against this list at once, and some of these are already-known, longstanding tech debt (`CLAUDE.md`'s fragility notes: in-memory pagination over full collections in several routes) that no phase currently owns fixing.

**Scope:** Cross-cutting audit + fixes; touches many files, but each fix is typically small and mechanical (add a missing empty state, swap in‑memory pagination for cursor-based, add a skeleton loader) rather than new features.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Convert the known in-memory-pagination routes (`reports.js:171`-area, `sales.js` admin/leads listings, `crmService.js` client/appointment listings — see `CLAUDE.md`'s fragility notes) to real Firestore cursor-based pagination.
2. Add virtualization (e.g. `react-window`) to any photo grid that can reach 100 items (Phase 6's wizard grid, Phase 22's photo library) if not already lazy/paginated enough to avoid rendering all 100 full images at once (Phase 6 already thumbnails everything server-side, which helps significantly — confirm this alone is sufficient before adding virtualization, to avoid over-engineering).
3. Sweep every major screen (this roadmap's own new pages as they ship, plus existing ones) for a consistent three-state pattern: skeleton-loading, a real empty state with a clear next action, and a friendly error state with Retry (already done well in Phase 4's dashboard — use it as the reference pattern).
4. Ensure long-running AI operations (report generation, photo analysis once Phase 7 exists) use the FLACRON-branded processing treatment (spec §42) consistently, not a generic spinner.
5. A dedicated mobile pass on any authenticated screen not already covered by an earlier phase's own mobile testing (the old `TASKS.md` backlog's T-6.24 flagged this exact gap for the report editor/approval/calendar screens, predating this roadmap).

**Files/components likely affected:** Broad — `backend/routes/reports.js`, `sales.js`, `crmService.js` (pagination), various frontend pages for state-pattern consistency.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Soft — most valuable done *after* most other phases ship (so it's auditing final pages, not pages that'll change again soon), but the pagination fixes are independent and can happen anytime.

**Expected output:** A consistently polished, performant application, not one where quality depends on which phase happened to touch a given page.

**Acceptance criteria:** No route reads a full collection into memory just to paginate client-side; no photo grid up to 100 items causes a visible performance problem; every major screen has a real empty/loading/error state matching the established reference pattern.

**Testing/verification steps:** Load-test a paginated list with a large seeded dataset and confirm cursor pagination (not a full scan); load a 100-photo report and confirm smooth scrolling/rendering; disconnect network mid-load on several screens and confirm a friendly error+Retry state, not a blank page or crash.

**Risks/considerations:** This phase can expand indefinitely if not scoped — recommend treating it as a checklist run at the *end* of the roadmap (or periodically), not a single one-time phase, and being explicit each time about which specific pages were covered in that pass.

**Status:** Not Started

**Reason this phase is required:** Promoted from Open Question #9 per explicit client request this session ("SSO, White Label... completion" named directly as a gap). The existing codebase has a `WhiteLabelPortal.jsx` and backend `whitelabel.js` route (confirmed to exist from earlier phases' work, e.g. Phase 2's fix to its default brand colors) but its completeness against the full spec (custom domain support, custom email templates/sender, client-portal branding) has never been systematically checked. SSO (SAML/OIDC for Enterprise) has no confirmed implementation anywhere in prior audits.

**Scope:** Phase 1 (this phase): a verification/audit pass, no assumptions. Phase 2 (a likely follow-on, possibly its own future phase once scoped): whatever completion work the audit finds necessary.

**Exact tasks:**
1. **Verification pass:** systematically check `WhiteLabelPortal.jsx`/`backend/routes/whitelabel.js` against every White-Label item in the spec (logo/name/colors/header/footer/contact/disclaimer/domain/email-sender/email-templates/client-portal/custom-templates) and record exactly what's real vs. stubbed vs. missing (the existing "Custom Domain" feature was previously found to be a "Coming Soon" placeholder — confirm whether that's still true and what else shares that status).
2. **Verification pass:** search for any existing SSO/SAML/OIDC code at all (likely none) and confirm this is a fully net-new Enterprise feature, not a partial one.
3. Based on the verification findings, produce a scoped completion task list (this becomes the real "Phase 28b" once the audit above is done) — do not guess at SSO/White-Label implementation work before the verification pass reports back.

**Files/components likely affected:** `frontend/src/pages/WhiteLabelPortal.jsx`, `backend/routes/whitelabel.js`, new SSO-related backend auth code (likely `backend/middleware/auth.js` extension or a dedicated `backend/routes/sso.js`) once scoped.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None for the verification pass; the completion work's scope depends entirely on that pass's findings, and may also depend on the Organization-entity-model Open Question (#12) if SSO needs a formal multi-tenant org concept.

**Expected output:** An accurate, evidence-based understanding of White-Label/SSO completeness, followed by a real, scoped completion plan (not a guess).

**Acceptance criteria (for the verification pass specifically):** every White-Label spec item is marked Working/Partial/Missing with file:line evidence; SSO is confirmed present or absent with the same rigor.

**Testing/verification steps:** Exercise every White-Label config field as an Enterprise test account; attempt to find any SSO login path in the running app.

**Risks/considerations:** Do not skip straight to "completion work" without the verification pass — this phase exists specifically because assuming completeness (or incompleteness) without checking was already flagged as a risk in the original spreadsheet audit.

## Phase 29 — Usage & Billing Completion & Storage-Usage Tracking

**Objective:** Verify the existing Usage & Billing page against the spec's exact field list, and build the one piece of real infrastructure it (and two other phases) depend on but which doesn't exist yet: per-user storage-usage tracking.

**Reason this phase is required:** Billing itself is functionally solid (Stripe checkout/webhooks/invoices all confirmed working in earlier phases) but has never been checked field-by-field against spec §26, and its "Storage: 2.8 GB"-style figure cannot be shown honestly today — **confirmed via code search: no `storageUsed`/`bytesUsed`/`totalStorage` field or computation exists anywhere in the codebase.** Phase 4's dashboard already correctly handles this gap (shows "Not yet available" rather than fabricate a number, per Golden Rule #1) — this phase builds the real thing so that placeholder can finally be replaced everywhere it appears (Dashboard usage panel, this Billing page, Settings' Data tab in Phase 18).

**Scope:** A backend storage-accounting mechanism (likely: sum object sizes under `users/{uid}/` from Storage metadata, either on-demand or via a periodic/cached computation to avoid an expensive live bucket-listing call on every page load) + frontend field-completeness pass on the existing Billing page.

**Exact tasks:**
1. **OPEN QUESTION:** choose a storage-accounting approach — (a) live-list the user's Storage prefix and sum object sizes on request (simple, but potentially slow/expensive at scale for a page load), (b) maintain a running byte-counter field on the user doc, incremented/decremented at every upload/delete call site (more moving parts to keep correct, but cheap to read), or (c) a scheduled/periodic recomputation job. Recommend (b) for accuracy-per-cost, incrementing at the same call sites Phase 6 already touches (photo/thumbnail/document uploads) and decrementing on delete — but this is a real design decision, not a given.
2. Implement the chosen mechanism; backfill existing accounts' current usage once (a one-time script, not an automatic migration, per this project's established convention for backfills).
3. Wire the real number into Phase 4's dashboard usage panel (replacing "Not yet available"), this Billing page, and Phase 18's Settings Data tab.
4. Field-by-field audit of the existing Billing page against spec §26 (Current Plan, Reports X/Y, Photos Processed, Storage, billing period, Upgrade/Manage buttons, invoices table) — fill in whatever's missing.

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/config/storage.js` (or a new `backend/services/storageUsageService.js`), every upload/delete call site in `backend/routes/reports.js`/`users.js`/`whitelabel.js` (if approach (b) is chosen), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (usage panel), `frontend/src/pages/Subscriptions.jsx` (Billing page).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None blocking for the verification half; the storage-tracking half is a real, standalone technical task.

**Expected output:** A real, accurate storage-usage figure available everywhere the product currently either fabricates or honestly withholds one, and a Billing page confirmed complete against spec.

**Acceptance criteria:** Storage usage shown anywhere in the app reflects real object sizes (verified against actual Firebase Storage bucket contents for a test account); Billing page shows every spec'd field.

**Testing/verification steps:** Upload/delete photos and documents on a test account, confirm the tracked storage figure moves correctly and matches a manual bucket-size check; verify every Billing page field against a real subscription.

**Risks/considerations:** Approach (b)'s running counter can drift from reality if any upload/delete path is missed or a request fails partway through — plan a periodic reconciliation job (or at minimum admin tooling to recompute a specific user's true usage) rather than trusting the counter blindly forever.

**Status:** Not Started

## Phase 30 — Routing Migration: Real, Bookmarkable URLs

**Objective:** Replace `Dashboard.jsx`'s internal `activeView` state-switching with real React Router routes matching the spec's URL structure (§36), without breaking any current navigation, saved link, or the several query-param-driven flows that already depend on `/dashboard` behaving exactly as it does today.

**Reason this phase is required (client decision, 2026-08-16):** the spec's entire URL Structure section assumes every major feature is its own bookmarkable, shareable, browser-history-aware URL (`/reports`, `/reports/new`, `/reports/:id`, `/claims`, `/photos`, `/templates`, `/team`, `/analytics`, `/integrations`, `/organization`, `/audit-logs`, etc.). Verified directly: none of these exist as real routes today — `App.jsx` registers only `/dashboard` (plus the already-real `/settings` and `/subscriptions`), and every other "page" is an `activeView` string switched inside `Dashboard.jsx` (`useState('home')`, conditionals like `activeView === 'reports'`). The client has now explicitly decided: **build real routes matching the specification**, migrated incrementally so nothing currently working breaks.

**Scope:** Frontend routing architecture only — no new features, no visual redesign. This phase's entire job is moving existing view content behind real URLs, one view at a time, while keeping every current behavior intact.

**Why this must land before Phases 9, 10, 12, 13, 14, 15, 16, 17, 22 (and lightly touches 11, 18, 26):** those phases' own task lists describe building on top of `Dashboard.jsx`'s current view-switching (e.g. "add a Duplicate action to the reports view", "build a standalone `/team` page") — if they're implemented against the *old* architecture first, that work gets redone or awkwardly retrofitted once routing lands. Doing the migration first means every subsequent phase's new UI is written directly against real routes from the start.

**Exact tasks (staged migration — each stage is independently shippable and safe to stop after):**

1. **Introduce a shared authenticated layout + nested routing shell, with zero visible change.** Extract `Dashboard.jsx`'s sidebar/topbar/profile-menu chrome into a `DashboardLayout` component rendering `<Outlet/>` for the main content area. Add it to `App.jsx` as a layout route wrapping a new nested route tree. At this stage, the *only* child route is `index` (rendering exactly what `Dashboard.jsx` renders today, `activeView` state and all) — this stage should be invisible to a user and is purely a scaffolding/regression-safety step, verified by confirming every existing behavior (wizard, My Reports, Billing view, sidebar nav) still works identically.
2. **Migrate view-by-view, lowest-risk first.** Recommended order (simplest/most isolated state first, most complex last):
   - `activeView === 'reports'` → `/reports` (list) and `/reports/:id` (detail, if a dedicated detail route is wanted beyond the existing modal — decide per Phase 12's needs).
   - `activeView === 'billing'` → reconcile with the *already-existing* `/subscriptions` route (likely: make `/billing` the real spec-matching URL, `/subscriptions` a redirect to it, or vice versa — pick ONE canonical URL and redirect the other, never keep both live with duplicated logic).
   - `activeView === 'home'` → `/dashboard` itself (already the URL users land on; no visible change, just formalizing that this *is* the home route rather than a coincidental default state).
   - `activeView === 'generate'` → `/reports/new` (the wizard) — do this later in the sequence since it has the most internal step-state (`step`, `form`, `photos`, `documents`, etc.) to carry across the routing change safely; consider whether wizard step itself becomes part of the URL (e.g. `/reports/new?step=4`) for refresh-safety, matching the existing localStorage-based autosave.
   - Each of Phases 12 (Claims/My Reports), 13 (Templates), 14 (Team), 15 (Analytics), 16 (Integrations), 17 (Organization/Audit Logs), 22 (Photo Library) gets its real top-level route created *as part of that phase's own implementation*, once this migration phase has proven the pattern (layout + nested routes + old-view-preserved-behavior) on the views above — this phase does not need to pre-build empty routes for features that don't exist yet.
3. **Preserve every current query-param-driven flow.** `Dashboard.jsx` currently reads `?upgrade=success&tier=...&session_id=...` and `?billing=updated` from the URL on mount to drive post-Stripe-checkout confirmation logic — these must keep working unchanged regardless of which route they redirect through after migration (Stripe's configured success/cancel URLs point at specific paths; verify those still resolve correctly post-migration, updating Stripe's configured redirect URL if the canonical billing route's path changes).
4. **Add a redirect for anything that might be bookmarked today.** The only genuinely bookmarkable URL before this phase is `/dashboard` itself (everything else was `activeView` state, never in the address bar) — so there is no legacy `/dashboard?view=reports`-style URL to preserve. The one thing to verify: any in-app `<a href>`/`navigate()` calls or emails that link to `/dashboard` continue landing somewhere sensible (the new home route) after migration.
5. **Decide encoding for deep state** (e.g. `/reports?status=draft&search=...` for My Reports' existing filter/search/pagination state) so filtered views are themselves shareable/bookmarkable, matching the spirit of "real URLs" rather than just moving the view boundary without carrying its state into the URL too.

**Files/components likely affected:** `frontend/src/App.jsx` (new nested route tree, `DashboardLayout`), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (split into a layout + per-view components over the course of the migration — this is the biggest, most central file in the codebase, so expect this to be genuinely the largest single-file refactor in the whole roadmap), `frontend/src/pages/Subscriptions.jsx` (billing URL reconciliation).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None technical — this is foundational. Coordinate timing with whichever of Phases 9/10/12/13/14/15/16/17/22 the client wants to prioritize next, since this should land immediately before them, not necessarily as a single big-bang effort disconnected from real feature work.

**Expected output:** Every major authenticated feature has its own real, bookmarkable, browser-history-aware URL, with zero regression to any currently-working flow (wizard, billing, checkout redirects, sidebar nav).

**Acceptance criteria:** Direct navigation, browser refresh, and back/forward all work correctly on every migrated route; the post-Stripe-checkout confirmation flow still works end-to-end; no existing test (backend or frontend) regresses; a user mid-wizard who refreshes doesn't silently lose more state than they do today.

**Testing/verification steps:** For each migrated view: direct URL entry, refresh, browser back/forward, and a full click-through of that view's existing functionality (no behavior change, only URL change) — live-verified in a real browser, not just unit-tested, since this is fundamentally a navigation/UX-continuity concern. Full regression pass on the Stripe checkout-redirect flow specifically, since it's the one flow with real external-service coordination (Stripe's configured redirect URLs).

**Risks/considerations:** This is the single largest refactor in the entire roadmap by file-churn (touches the app's biggest file extensively) — do it incrementally, one view per commit per Golden Rule #7, and re-run the full test suite + a live-browser smoke pass after each view migrates, not just once at the end. Resist the temptation to "just do it all at once" even though `Dashboard.jsx` is already large and tangled — a partial, working migration that stops safely between phases is far preferable to a half-broken big-bang rewrite.

**Status:** Not Started

---

## Phase 31 — Liability Investigation Report (Document Type)

**Objective:** Add a Liability Investigation Report as a distinct document type, generated when a report's `claimType === 'Liability'`, matching the client's sample document's structure reliably rather than hoping a single freeform AI prompt reproduces it.

**Scope:** Backend section-assembly + prompt architecture + PDF/DOCX cover text only. No wizard document-type picker (claimType already selects it), no changes to the generic Property path or to any other Phase 32-39 document type.

**Workflow location:** Same wizard entry point as today's report generation (`Dashboard.jsx` 5-step wizard) — auto-selected when `claimType='Liability'`; no new screen or step.

**Required fields:**
- Reused as-is (already exist and are validated in `reports.js`): `claimNumber`, `insuredName` (premises owner), `insuredEmail`, `propertyAddress`, `lossDate`, `lossType`, `policyNumber`, `reportType`, `lossDescription`, `damagesObserved`, `recommendations`, `additionalNotes`.
- New: `claimantName`, `claimantContact` (both optional, ~150-char limit, validated the same way as the existing `optionalFieldLimits` pattern).

**Architecture (single structured AI call, not per-section calls):**
- Define a code-level section manifest: `static` sections (Parties table, Incident Data table, Adjuster Review Checklist) built directly from report fields — zero AI involvement, so they render exactly right every time.
- All `narrative` sections (Incident Summary, Scene Observations, Investigation Checklist suggestions, Recommendations, Conclusion) are requested in **one** AI call that returns a structured object with one key per narrative slot (reuse `aiService.js`'s `generateWithFallback` plumbing; request/parse a single JSON object; on a missing/malformed key, do one repair retry scoped to just that key, mirroring the existing `ensureLossSummary`/`ensureConclusion` partial-failure repair pattern — never silently ship a blank section).
- A deterministic assembler stitches static + parsed narrative sections into final markdown in manifest order, then hands off to the existing generic PDF/DOCX renderers unchanged.
- Net effect: one AI call per report (same cost/latency as today's generic report), no partial-failure risk from N independent calls, and the tabular/static parts of the layout never depend on the model getting formatting right.

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/services/aiService.js` (Liability manifest, single structured narrative prompt + parser/validator, assembler); `backend/routes/reports.js` (`claimantName`/`claimantContact` fields + validation, claimType→manifest wiring); `backend/utils/properPdfGenerator.js` and `documentGenerator.js` (`reportTitle`/`reportSubtitle` options, defaulting to today's generic text when unset); `backend/test/` (new test file).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None — `claimType='Liability'` already exists and is validated server-side, currently unused for anything but the generic template.

**Client decisions:** None blocking. Confirmed 2026-08-24: no new tier restriction (same access as generic reports); selection is automatic via `claimType`, not a manual document-type picker.

**Risks/considerations:** Structured-output parsing must handle a malformed/partial model response gracefully (one repair retry, then a clear generation-failure — never ship a section with missing narrative). No content-based filter against sample/fixture names (a real user's data can legitimately match a sample name) — the only safeguard against sample-data leakage is that no new code may hardcode a sample name/claim number/address as a default anywhere, checked in code review.

**Acceptance criteria:** A Liability-claimType report generates end-to-end (PDF+DOCX) with the sample's section structure; static sections render exactly from input data; narrative sections contain the existing cautious-language markers and no hard liability/fault verdict phrases; the generic Property-report path is functionally unaffected (same sections/title/validation as before — a structural regression check, not a literal byte diff, since AI narrative is inherently non-deterministic).

**Testing/verification steps:** Backend test asserting static-section markdown exactly matches expected output for given input fields; a generation test with realistic non-fixture data checking narrative markers/absence of verdict phrases and that exactly one AI call is made per report (not one per section); manual PDF+DOCX generation to visually confirm cover title/subtitle and layout; full existing report-generation test suite re-run to confirm no regression to the generic path.

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-08-24), per explicit instruction ("start Phase 31 only"). Full detail in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 31 entry; summary: implemented exactly as specced above (static Parties/Incident Data/Adjuster Review Checklist + one structured AI call for the 5 narrative slots + deterministic assembler), plus a `claimantName`/`claimantContact` conditional UI in the wizard and a Table-of-Contents fix (found live) so the PDF's ToC matches the Liability manifest instead of the generic one. Backend 292/292 tests pass (10 new). Live-verified end-to-end against the real Anthropic API + real Firebase project via a throwaway QA account (created and fully deleted afterward): a real browser wizard run produced a Liability report with all 9 sections correctly populated, cautious language throughout, no hard liability/fault verdict, and correct PDF + DOCX exports (including the fixed cover title/ToC).

## Phase 32 — Commercial Property Inspection Report (Document Type)

**Required fields:** Reused baseline as Phase 31. New: `propertyManagerName`/`propertyManagerContact`, `roofType`, `roofAge`, `tenantSuiteCount` (all optional, length/enum-limited like the existing `optionalFieldLimits` pattern).

**Files/components likely affected:** Same set as Phase 31 (`aiService.js`, `reports.js`, `properPdfGenerator.js`, `documentGenerator.js`, `backend/test/`), plus `Dashboard.jsx`/`EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` to conditionally render the new commercial fields.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 31 must land first — this phase reuses its manifest + single-structured-call pattern rather than inventing a new one.

**Client decisions:** None identified as blocking, but confirm the "no new tier restriction" default explicitly applies here too before starting — do not assume it carries over automatically from Phase 31.

**Risks/considerations:** Business-interruption/tenant-specific claims are explicitly out of scope per the sample ("outside the scope of this structural report") — the narrative rules must keep the model from drifting into BI coverage territory.

**Acceptance criteria:** Commercial-claimType report generates end-to-end with correct static/narrative sections; new fields validated and rendered; generic/Liability paths unaffected.

**Testing/verification steps:** Same pattern as Phase 31 — static-section exact-match test, narrative marker/verdict-phrase test, single-AI-call assertion, full regression pass, manual PDF/DOCX visual check.

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-08-24), per explicit instruction ("implement Phase 32 only"). Full detail in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 32 entry; summary: reused Phase 31's static+single-structured-call architecture exactly — one "Insured & Property Information" static table + Adjuster Review Checklist, plus one structured AI call for the 6 narrative slots (Loss Description, Damage Assessment, Roof Moisture Scan, Scope of Work, Recommendations, Conclusion) — with the 5 new commercial-specific fields (`propertyManagerName`/`propertyManagerContact`/`roofType`/`roofAge`/`tenantSuiteCount`) and a `Dashboard.jsx` conditional-fields block (`EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` intentionally left untouched — it has no `claimType` selector at all, same reasoning Phase 31 already applied). Per PHASES.md's own exact task list, the manifest has no cost/estimate section (deferred to the future Phase 37). Backend 303/303 tests pass (11 new). Live-verified end-to-end against the real Anthropic API + real Firebase project via a throwaway QA account (created and fully deleted afterward): a real browser wizard run produced a Commercial report with all 9 sections correctly populated from real (non-fixture) input data, cautious language throughout, business-interruption explicitly out of scope, zero dollar figures, and both PDF + DOCX exports generated without error. A direct regression call confirmed the generic Property and Liability paths still generate correctly, unaffected by the new Commercial dispatch branch.

## Phase 33 — Flood (NFIP) Inspection Report (Document Type)

**Objective:** Add a Flood/NFIP-specific Inspection Report, generated when `lossType === 'Flood'` (a new enum value — the first document type in this set keyed off `lossType` rather than `claimType`).

**Scope:** Add `'Flood'` to `LOSS_TYPES`; new NFIP-specific fields; same static+single-structured-call architecture as Phase 31.

**Workflow location:** Wizard loss-type dropdown gains "Flood"; auto-detected by `lossType='Flood'`.

**Required fields:** New — `floodZone`, `lowestFloorElevation`, `baseFloodElevation`, `nfipPolicyNumber` (or reuse `policyNumber` with a contextual label), `floodEventSource`/`reportedCrest` (all optional).

**Files/components likely affected:** `LOSS_TYPES` in `Dashboard.jsx` + `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`; `reports.js` (new fields + validation); `aiService.js` (Flood manifest); `properPdfGenerator.js`/`documentGenerator.js` (title/subtitle); `backend/test/`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 31's manifest/single-call pattern. Also depends on resolving the `claimType`-vs-`lossType` document-type precedence rule below — this is the first phase where both signals could theoretically apply to the same report (e.g. a Commercial claim with a Flood loss type).

**Client decisions (blocking, resolved 2026-08-24):** (1) Precedence rule when both a `claimType`-keyed and `lossType`-keyed template could apply to the same report: **`lossType === 'Flood'` takes precedence over any `claimType` template** — a Commercial claim with a Flood loss still gets the Flood manifest, with the applicable commercial-property fields (Property Manager Contact, Roof Type, Roof Age, Number of Tenant Suites) folded into its Property & Flood Zone Data table rather than a separate manifest. (2) Genuine NFIP regulatory/legal accuracy is NOT required — cautious/generic framing (as in the client's sample) is sufficient, backstopped by a **fixed, deterministic (non-AI) disclaimer** stating the document is an inspection draft, does not fully represent federal NFIP requirements, and is not an official coverage or claim determination.

**Risks/considerations:** Adding a `LOSS_TYPES` value is backward-compatible in principle but touches existing filter/search/analytics code keyed off that enum — verify nothing assumes a fixed, closed set. Confirmed 2026-08-24: `reports.js`'s list/search/filter endpoints and `crmService.js`'s analytics tally already treat `lossType` as an open string (exact-match filter + free-text search + a dynamic `claimsByLossType[lossType]++` tally), so no closed-enum assumption existed to fix.

**Acceptance criteria:** "Flood" selectable in the wizard; Flood-lossType report generates the NFIP-specific structure end-to-end; existing `lossType` filter/search/analytics behavior unaffected by the new enum value.

**Testing/verification steps:** Same pattern as Phase 31, plus a test that `lossType='Flood'` selects the Flood manifest under the decided precedence rule even when `claimType` is something else; a regression check on existing `lossType` filter UI/queries.

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-08-24), per explicit instruction ("implement Phase 33 only"). Full detail in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 33 entry; summary: reused Phase 31/32's static+single-structured-call architecture — 3 static sections (Insured & Policy Information, Property & Flood Zone Data, Flood Event Data) + Adjuster Review Checklist, all zero-AI, plus one structured AI call for 5 narrative slots (Property Description, Damage Assessment, Scope of Work, Recommendations, Conclusion). `lossType === 'Flood'` is checked FIRST in `generateReport`'s dispatcher, before the existing `claimType` checks, implementing the approved precedence rule; when `claimType === 'Commercial'`, the applicable commercial-property fields are folded into the Property & Flood Zone Data table. A fixed, deterministic NFIP disclaimer (not AI-generated) is always included verbatim. New optional fields: `floodZone`, `lowestFloorElevation`, `baseFloodElevation`, `floodEventSource`, `reportedCrest` (NFIP policy number reuses the existing `policyNumber` field with a contextual label). Backend 14/14 new tests pass (317/317 across the three document-type suites combined, 0 regressions). Live-verified end-to-end against the real Anthropic API + real Firebase project via a throwaway QA account (created and fully deleted afterward, along with all its test reports/photos): a real browser wizard run produced a correct Flood report (all 10 sections, fixed disclaimer verbatim, cautious language, zero dollar figures, correct PDF+DOCX export), a Commercial-claimType + Flood-lossType report correctly won on the Flood template with commercial fields folded in, and direct regression checks confirmed Property (generic), Liability, and Commercial (non-Flood) reports all still generate their own correct, unaffected structures.

## Phase 34 — Theft/Burglary Inspection Report (Document Type)

**Objective:** Add a Theft/Burglary Inspection Report, generated when `lossType === 'Theft'` (a new enum value), scoped to structural entry-point damage only — contents/valuation are explicitly out of scope per the sample, tracked via a separate insured-provided inventory and police report.

**Scope/workflow:** Same `lossType`-driven pattern and precedence rule established in Phase 33.

**Required fields:** New — `policeIncidentNumber`, `pointsOfEntry` (both optional). Reuses `damagesObserved`/`lossDescription` narratively for the rest — the smallest new-field surface of the property-side additions.

**Files/components likely affected:** Same set as Phase 33 (`LOSS_TYPES`, `reports.js`, `aiService.js`, PDF/DOCX title options, `backend/test/`).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 31's manifest pattern; reuses the `claimType`-vs-`lossType` precedence rule decided in Phase 33 (no new decision needed here).

**Client decisions:** Confirm no new tier restriction, consistent with Phase 31 — do not assume it carries over automatically; confirm per phase per Golden Rule #4.

**Risks/considerations:** The report must never imply a determination of which items were present or missing before the loss — that is explicitly deferred to the insured's contents inventory and the police report per the sample. Keep this boundary explicit in the narrative-generation rules.

**Acceptance criteria / testing:** Same pattern as Phases 31/33 (static exact-match, narrative marker/verdict checks, single-call assertion, regression pass, visual PDF/DOCX check).

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-08-24), per explicit instruction ("implement Phase 34 only"). Full detail in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 34 entry; summary: reused Phase 33's `lossType`-keyed pattern and precedence rule exactly — `lossType === 'Theft'` checked immediately after Flood in `generateReport`'s dispatcher, before any `claimType` check — with 3 static sections (Insured & Policy Information, Property & Loss Information, Incident Data — Police Report) + Adjuster Review Checklist, all zero-AI, plus one structured AI call for 5 narrative slots (Incident Summary, Damage Assessment, Scope of Work, Recommendations, Conclusion). A fixed, deterministic (non-AI) disclaimer states that whether items existed, were stolen, or their value is established solely by the insured's contents inventory and the police report. New optional fields: `policeIncidentNumber`, `pointsOfEntry`. Backend 12/12 new tests pass (329/329 total, 0 regressions). Live-verified end-to-end against the real Anthropic API + real Firebase project via a throwaway QA account (created and fully deleted afterward, along with all its test reports/photos): a real browser check confirmed the two new conditional wizard fields render correctly; a real-API-generated Theft report produced all 10 sections, the fixed disclaimer verbatim, cautious language throughout, correct handling of the police-incident/points-of-entry fields, zero dollar figures, no claim about what items existed/were stolen/their value, and a successful PDF export; a Commercial-claimType + Theft-lossType report correctly won on the Theft template (precedence rule holds against the real API); a generic Property/Water-Damage regression report generated correctly, unaffected by the new dispatch branch.

## Phase 35 — Vehicle/Auto Inspection Report (Document Type)

**Objective:** Add a Vehicle Damage Inspection Report (e.g. hail/collision), generated when `claimType === 'Auto'` (existing enum value, currently unused for anything but the generic template).

**Scope:** First phase requiring genuinely new field types outside the property domain — VIN, vehicle make/model/year, odometer, license plate, panel-by-panel damage entries — and likely new photo-intake UX (panel-tagged rather than room-tagged photos).

**Workflow location:** The wizard needs a materially different "property details" step for Auto claims (vehicle info instead of address/structure fields) — a larger UI change than Phases 31-34.

**Required fields:** New — `vin`, `vehicleMakeModelYear`, `odometer`, `licensePlate`, `vehicleColor` (all optional); panel-by-panel damage is new structured-array territory (panel name + observation), not a flat field set.

**Files/components likely affected:** `Dashboard.jsx`/`EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` (vehicle-specific wizard step/fields, possibly panel-tagged photo UX extending the existing `PHOTO_LOCATIONS` taxonomy in `aiService.js`); `reports.js` (new fields + validation); `aiService.js` (Vehicle manifest + panel-assessment narrative); `properPdfGenerator.js`/`documentGenerator.js` (title/subtitle + panel-table rendering, reusing the existing generic table renderer); `backend/test/`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 31's manifest/single-call architecture; otherwise independent of Phases 32-34.

**Client decision (applied):** Client approved building a real vehicle panel-selection photo workflow now, not the generic room-tagged interim option — implemented as its own `VEHICLE_PANELS` taxonomy reusing the existing `roomOrArea`/`set_area` tagging mechanism (Phase 24), not a new field.

**Risks/considerations:** Largest scope jump of the additive phases. Repairability language ("PDR candidate", "replacement likely") needs the same cautious-language treatment as everything else — must never let the model state a final repairability or total-loss determination (Golden Rule #2). No cost/estimate section was added (matches the Liability/Commercial/Flood/Theft precedent — deferred to Phase 37).

**Acceptance criteria:** Auto-claimType report generates end-to-end with vehicle fields + panel assessment; no repairability/total-loss verdict language ships without adjuster-review qualifying language. — **Met**, verified live.

**Testing/verification steps:** Same pattern as prior phases, plus a check that panel-assessment narrative never emits a bare "repairable"/"total loss" determination without qualifying language. — **Done.**

**Status:** DONE (2026-08-24), per explicit instruction ("implement Phase 35 only"). Not committed/pushed per standing instruction. See PROGRESS.md for full QA detail.

## Phase 36 — Mold Assessment (Supplemental) Report (Document Type)

**Objective:** Add a Mold Assessment — Preliminary Report as a supplemental document attached to an existing report/claim (not a primary loss-type entry point), matching the sample's explicit "NOT a certified mold assessment" framing.

**Scope:** First phase requiring a claim-linking mechanism that doesn't exist yet (`relatedClaimId`/`relatedClaimNumber`).

**Workflow location:** A "Generate Supplemental Document" action on an existing report's detail view — not the primary wizard; the user explicitly triggers this from a report they've already created, linking back to it.

**Required fields:** New — `relatedClaimId` (or `relatedClaimNumber`), `dateOfDiscovery`. Reuses `propertyAddress`/`insuredName` from the linked report rather than re-collecting them.

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/routes/reports.js` (new `relatedClaimId` field + lookup for linked-report context, likely a new sub-route e.g. `POST /api/reports/:id/mold-supplement`); frontend report-detail view (new action + linking UI); `aiService.js` (Mold manifest — smallest narrative surface of any phase: Visual Observations + Recommended Next Steps only; the scope-notice block is fixed/static, never AI-generated); `properPdfGenerator.js`/`documentGenerator.js` (title/subtitle + the fixed scope-notice static section).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 31's manifest pattern; the `relatedClaimId` linkage is new and built as part of this phase.

**Client decisions:** None identified as strictly blocking, but confirm the linking UX (user picks from their existing reports list, vs. only reachable from within an already-open report) before building the UI.

**Risks/considerations:** This is the document type most exposed to Golden Rules #2/#3 if mishandled — the "NOT a certified assessment" disclaimer and the explicit list of what it does NOT include (species ID, air quality, health-risk determination, remediation protocol) must be a fixed static section, never left to AI narrative generation, so it can never be paraphrased away or omitted.

**Acceptance criteria:** Mold supplement generates linked to a real existing report; the "not a certified assessment" scope-notice section is present verbatim (static) in every output; narrative sections stay within visual-observation-only language.

**Testing/verification steps:** Test that the static scope-notice section is exactly identical across generations (it's fully static, so an exact match is the correct test here, unlike narrative sections elsewhere); link-integrity test (supplement correctly references an existing report); narrative marker/verdict-phrase test as in prior phases.

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-08-24), per explicit instruction ("implement Phase 36 only"). Full detail in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 36 entry; summary: implemented as a supplement generated from an already-open EXISTING report (not the primary wizard) via new `POST /api/reports/:id/mold-supplement`, keyed off a new `documentType === 'MoldSupplement'` flag checked first (before any claimType/lossType) in `generateReport()`'s dispatcher. New fields `relatedClaimId` and `dateOfDiscovery`; the linked report's `insuredName`/`insuredEmail`/`propertyAddress`/`policyNumber` and its REVIEWED (non-excluded) photo/imageAnalysis data are reused via the existing `buildEffectiveImageAnalysis`, not re-collected or re-uploaded. Manifest: 4 static sections (Report Information, Insured Information, Background — Related Claim, Adjuster Review Checklist) + the fixed, verbatim, non-AI "NOT a certified mold assessment" scope notice (`MOLD_SCOPE_NOTICE`, exact-match tested) + exactly one structured AI call for 2 narrative slots (Visual Observations, Recommended Next Steps) + a fixed Conclusion/AI-disclosure section — the smallest AI narrative surface of any document type per the phase's own spec. No cost/price section (matches the Liability/Commercial/Flood/Theft/Vehicle precedent of deferring cost math to Phase 37, and keeps the draft clear of any repair/remediation cost determination). The supplement is stored as its own normal `reports` doc (`relatedReportId` linking back to the parent) so every existing generic endpoint — GET `/:id`, `/:id/preview` (frontend), approve, export (PDF/DOCX), download, and `/:id/photos/regenerate` — works on it completely unmodified; ownership/authorization is therefore identical to any other report (userId match), including cross-user denial. Frontend: a "Mold Supplement" action + modal on `ReportPreviewPage.jsx` (the existing report detail view), reachable only from an already-open non-supplement report, plus a linked-report badge when viewing a supplement itself. Backend 14/14 new tests pass (365/365 total, 0 regressions) — static-section exact-match, scope-notice byte-for-byte-identical-across-generations, single-AI-call assertion, repair-retry/failure paths, and dispatch-precedence-over-Flood/Theft/Liability/Commercial/Auto. Live-verified end-to-end against the real Anthropic API + real Firebase project via two throwaway QA accounts (created and fully deleted afterward, along with all test reports/photos) using a real HTTP-driven script exercising the exact same endpoints the UI calls (no browser-automation tool was available in this environment, so this substituted for a manual click-through): 25/25 checks passed, including generation linked to a real parent report, the scope notice appearing verbatim with no species/health-risk/coverage/cost verdicts, cross-user denial on both generation and read, PDF export (with the correct DRAFT filename marker), regeneration via the existing generic endpoint staying on the Mold architecture, and the parent (generic) report generating/exporting correctly afterward, unaffected.

## Phase 37 — Repair Estimate (Line-Item, with Depreciation Schedule)

**Objective:** Add a Repair Estimate document generated from an existing report, with a real structured line-item + depreciation data model — the first document type built on genuinely new structured data rather than mostly-narrative AI content.

**Scope:** A data-modeling phase more than a prompt-engineering phase. Backend data model + generation action + export.

**Workflow location:** A "Generate Document → Repair Estimate" action on an existing (ideally reviewed/finalized) report's detail view — not the wizard.

**Required fields (all new):** `lineItems` (array of `{code, description, qty, unit, unitPrice}` — `lineTotal` computed, never AI-supplied), `overheadProfitPercent`, `taxRate`/`taxBasis`, `depreciationSchedule` (array of `{item, ageYears, lifeExpectancyYears, condition, depreciationPercent}` — RCV/depreciation-$/ACV all computed in code, never AI-computed), `estimateNumber`/`revision`, `revisionHistory`.

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/routes/reports.js` (new sub-route, e.g. `POST /api/reports/:id/estimate`, its own request schema); a new backend module for estimate math (line totals, O&P, tax, depreciation — pure deterministic calculation, explicitly not delegated to the AI); `properPdfGenerator.js`/`documentGenerator.js` (new static table rendering for line items + depreciation schedule, reusing the existing generic table renderer); frontend report-detail view (line-item entry UI — likely the most form-heavy new UI of all nine phases); `backend/test/` (dedicated math-correctness tests).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** An existing report to attach to. Independent of Phases 32-36's manifest pattern since this is data/math-driven, not narrative-driven — AI's role, if any, should be limited to drafting line-item *descriptions* from photos/damage data, with all dollar math computed deterministically in code.

**Client decisions (blocking):** Confirm the estimate's dollar figures are always computed in code from user/adjuster-entered inputs (rates, quantities, %) rather than AI-generated numbers end-to-end — "final repair costs" are explicitly listed under Golden Rule #2 as something AI must not determine, so this must be confirmed explicitly before building the math module.

**Risks/considerations:** Real money math shown to end users (insureds/adjusters) — arithmetic bugs here are higher-stakes than a narrative wording issue elsewhere; needs dedicated unit tests for every calculation, not just end-to-end generation tests.

**Acceptance criteria:** Estimate generates from a real report with correct line totals/O&P/tax/depreciation math verified against hand-calculated expected values; revision history tracked; PDF/DOCX layout matches the sample's table structure.

**Testing/verification steps:** Unit tests for every calculation (line total, subtotal, O&P, tax, RCV/ACV per depreciation row) against known expected values; end-to-end generation test; manual PDF/DOCX visual check against the sample layout.

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-08-24), per explicit instruction ("implement Phase 37 only") and the approved rule: quantities, rates, tax, O&P, depreciation inputs, and revisions come from the user/adjuster; every dollar figure is computed deterministically in code; AI never provides or calculates a dollar amount. Full detail in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 37 entry; summary: two new pure, dependency-free backend modules do all the work with zero AI/Firestore coupling -- `backend/utils/estimateCalculations.js` (line-total/subtotal/O&P/tax/RCV/depreciation-$/ACV math, cents-based rounding throughout to avoid float drift, and `validateLineItems`/`validateDepreciationSchedule`/`validatePercent` rejecting missing/negative/non-finite/out-of-range/duplicate-code/unknown-code input) and `backend/utils/estimateContent.js` (deterministic markdown assembly using the same `##`/pipe-table dialect the existing PDF/DOCX generators already parse -- no export-generator code changes needed). A depreciation row's RCV is the SUM of the `lineTotal`s of the line items it names via `relatedLineItemCodes` (matches the sample's implied Roof/Gutters/Stucco groupings), never a client-supplied number. New routes on `backend/routes/reports.js`: `POST /:id/estimate` (owner-only, same generation-limit/tier gating as Phase 36's Mold Supplement, creates a new `documentType: 'RepairEstimate'` doc linked via `relatedReportId`) and `PUT /:id/estimate` (owner-or-review-grantee access via `getReportAccess`/`hasCapability`, same pattern as the generic content-edit route -- appends exactly one new `revisionHistory` entry per call, requires `changeSummary`, reopens `status` to `'draft'` per Golden Rule #3). `reportTitle`/`tocSections` mapping extended for `documentType === 'RepairEstimate'` so the existing generic PDF/DOCX/preview/approve/export/download endpoints render it correctly, completely unmodified otherwise (same reuse pattern as every prior phase's document type). Frontend: a "Repair Estimate" / "Revise Estimate" action + a form-heavy `RepairEstimateModal` on `ReportPreviewPage.jsx` (dynamic line-item rows with a taxable checkbox, dynamic depreciation rows with toggle-chip line-item linking, live client-side totals preview clearly labeled non-authoritative) -- confirmed the most form-heavy new UI of the nine phases, as PHASES.md anticipated. Backend: 26/26 new pure-function tests pass (391/391 total, 0 regressions) covering hand-verified line-total/subtotal/O&P/tax/RCV/depreciation-$/ACV math, float-drift avoidance, every rejection path (negative/zero/NaN/Infinity/out-of-range/duplicate/unknown-code/oversized-array), and markdown content assembly. Live end-to-end verified against the real Anthropic-configured backend + real Firebase project via a throwaway-QA-account HTTP script (no browser-automation tool available in this environment, matching Phase 36's substitution): 26/26 checks passed, including creation off a directly-inserted "existing eligible report" with hand-verified totals ($17,650 subtotal / $1,765 O&P / $1,334.03 tax with a non-taxable labor line correctly excluded from the tax base / $20,749.03 grand total), cross-user denial on create/read/revise, validation rejection of a negative quantity, a revision correctly appending Rev. 1 while leaving Rev. 0 untouched and requiring `changeSummary`, real PDF export downloaded and confirmed to start with the `%PDF` magic header, real DOCX export succeeding once tier-upgraded (starter tier correctly restricts to PDF-only, same enforcement as every other document type -- no special-cased bypass), and the original linked report still exporting cleanly afterward (existing report types unaffected). The generated PDF was visually inspected end-to-end and matches the client sample's structure: cover page, Table of Contents, Report Information table, Line Item Detail table with highlighted Subtotal/O&P/Tax/Total rows, Depreciation Schedule table, Revision History table, Terms & Conditions, and an Adjuster Review & Sign-Off section, plus the app's existing generic signature page. All QA users/reports were deleted after the run. AI's role in this phase is exactly zero -- it is never called from any estimate route or module.

## Phase 38 — Invoice (Document Type)

**Objective:** Add an Invoice document (contractor billing — services rendered, payments received, change orders, remit-to) matching the sample's structure.

**Scope (approved 2026-08-24):** The client's Invoice sample confirmed Invoice generation IS a wanted feature, scoped narrowly per explicit instruction: **document creation only, not a billing/payment-processing/accounting system.** No Stripe integration, no payment collection, no job/payment-tracking subsystem. An Invoice is generated FROM an existing Phase 37 Repair Estimate, reusing its already-approved, already-computed line items read-only — never re-entered, re-priced, or AI-generated. Every dollar figure (services subtotal, tax, payments-received total, balance due) is computed deterministically in code from that reused data plus adjuster-entered invoice fields (bill-to, dates, tax rate, payment history, change order log, remit-to) — AI is never called anywhere in this document type, matching Phase 37's precedent exactly.

**Design decision (documented in PROGRESS.md):** the Change Order Log is informational documentation only, like the client's own sample invoice (its one logged CO is already folded into a normal services-rendered line, not summed separately) — `changeOrderLog` entries never feed into the totals, avoiding a real double-counting risk without a job-costing subsystem to disambiguate already-billed vs. not-yet-billed change orders (explicitly out of this narrow scope).

**Workflow location:** A "Invoice" / "Revise Invoice" action on an existing Repair Estimate's detail view (`ReportPreviewPage.jsx`) — mirrors Phase 37's own action pattern exactly, one hop further down the chain (base report → estimate → invoice).

**Required fields (implemented):** `billTo` `{name, address}`, `invoiceNumber`/`invoiceDate`/`dueDate`/`jobNumber`, `servicesRendered` (read-only snapshot of the linked Repair Estimate's `lineItems`), `paymentHistory` (array of `{date, description, method, amount}`), `changeOrderLog` (array of `{coNumber, description, amount}`, informational only), `remitTo` `{name, instructions}`, `taxRatePercent`, `paymentTerms`, `warrantyText` (optional).

**Files/components affected:** `backend/utils/invoiceCalculations.js` (new, pure dollar math), `backend/utils/invoiceContent.js` (new, deterministic markdown assembly), `backend/routes/reports.js` (`POST /:id/invoice`, `PUT /:id/invoice`, `reportTitle`/`tocSections` mapping), `backend/test/invoice-report.test.js` (new), `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx` (`InvoiceModal`), `frontend/src/services/api.js`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 37 (Repair Estimate) — an Invoice can only be created from an existing `documentType: 'RepairEstimate'` report; enforced server-side (400 `SOURCE_NOT_ESTIMATE` otherwise).

**Risks/considerations mitigated:** real money math (same cents-based rounding discipline as Phase 37, hand-verified against the client's own sample invoice numbers); Golden Rule #2/#3 upheld — AI never touches a dollar figure, and an un-reviewed/revised invoice always exports watermarked DRAFT via the existing generic export gate.

**Acceptance criteria:** Invoice generates from a real approved Repair Estimate with correct services-subtotal/tax/payments/balance-due math verified against the client's own sample PDF's exact figures; revision history tracked; PDF/DOCX layout matches the sample's structure (Bill To, Invoice Details, Services Rendered, Totals, Payment History, Change Order Log, Payment Terms, Remit-To, Warranty).

**Testing/verification steps:** Unit tests for every calculation against the sample's hand-verified numbers; end-to-end generation/revision/export test; manual PDF visual check against the sample layout.

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-08-24), per explicit instruction ("implement Phase 38 only") with the scope explicitly limited to document creation (no billing/payment-processing/accounting system, no Stripe). Full detail in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 38 entry.
