## Phase 39 — Coverage Determination Letter (Document Type)

**Objective:** Add a Coverage Determination Letter — the carrier's actual per-item approve/deny/pending decision plus RCV/deductible/depreciation payment calculation.

**Scope:** Not to be started until the authoring-model decision below is answered. This is the single highest-risk document type in the whole set because, unlike every other sample, its entire purpose IS a coverage/payment determination — the exact thing Golden Rule #2 says AI must never decide.

**Client decision — RESOLVED 2026-08-24 (approved model, option (a)):** The licensed adjuster enters every coverage decision through a structured form; FlacronAI performs **zero AI drafting** of approval/denial, policy basis, rights/next-steps language, or payment figures anywhere in this document type. FlacronAI only validates, calculates (payment math only), formats, stores, and exports what the adjuster entered — the AI is never called anywhere in this route, this is the strictest AI-surface (zero) of any of the nine document-type phases.

**Workflow location (implemented):** A "Coverage Determination Letter" action on an already-**FINALIZED** base report's detail view (`ReportPreviewPage.jsx`) — never offered on a draft. The modal requires picking an already-**APPROVED (finalized)** Repair Estimate linked to that same report before the rest of the form unlocks. Both eligibility checks (`REPORT_NOT_FINALIZED`, `ESTIMATE_NOT_APPROVED`/`ESTIMATE_NOT_LINKED`/`SOURCE_NOT_ESTIMATE`) are enforced server-side via a pure, unit-tested `validateSourceEligibility` gate — never client-trusted.

**Required fields (implemented):** `addressee` `{name, address}`, `adjusterOfRecord` `{name, title, phone, email}` (new field — today's generic `reviewedBy` only stores uid/email, so this is a distinct, adjuster-typed display identity), `letterDate`, `determinationSummary` (e.g. "Partial Approval"), `deductible` `{description, amount}`, `coverageLimits` (array of `{coverageType, description, limit}`), `perItemDetermination` (array of `{item, determination: approved|denied|pending, policyBasis, relatedLineItemCodes[], pendingNote}` — an approved row must link ≥1 real linked-estimate line-item code, a pending row must carry its own `pendingNote`), `rightsAndNextSteps` (array of `{heading, text}`, entirely adjuster-typed, no default/boilerplate text), `enclosures` (optional array of strings). `paymentCalculation` (Approved RCV / Deductible / Recoverable Depreciation withheld / Initial Payment ACV) is **computed in code, never adjuster-entered directly** — see formula below.

**Payment formula (implemented, hand-verified against the client's own sample figures):** Approved RCV = sum of the linked estimate's line-item totals for codes an "approved" row actually references (a denied/pending item contributes nothing). Recoverable Depreciation withheld = sum of the linked estimate's depreciation-schedule rows whose *entire* `relatedLineItemCodes` set is within the approved set (a row that also touches a non-approved item is excluded entirely, never partially estimated). Initial Payment (ACV) = Approved RCV − Deductible − Recoverable Depreciation. Reproducing the client sample's own numbers exactly (RCV $28,345.80 / deductible $1,500.00 / depreciation $4,182.60 / ACV $22,663.20) is exact-match unit-tested.

**Files/components affected:** `backend/utils/coverageLetterCalculations.js` (new, pure dollar math + validation + the `validateSourceEligibility` gate), `backend/utils/coverageLetterContent.js` (new, deterministic markdown assembly incl. a fixed, exact-match-tested generic RCV/ACV definitional glossary and an attestation/legal-review-required notice), `backend/routes/reports.js` (`POST /:id/coverage-letter`, `PUT /:id/coverage-letter`, `reportTitle`/`tocSections` mapping, `relatedReportId`/`documentType` filters on `GET /`), `backend/test/coverage-letter.test.js` (new), `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx` (`CoverageLetterModal`), `frontend/src/services/api.js`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** An existing **finalized** report and an existing **finalized (approved)** Repair Estimate (Phase 37) linked to that same report — both enforced server-side, not just hidden in the UI.

**Risks/considerations mitigated:** Golden Rule #2 (the one genuine violation risk across all nine phases) — resolved by making the AI surface exactly zero: no AI call exists anywhere in either new module or route. Server-side role authorization: creating/revising this letter requires `canApprove` (not just `canGenerate`) in addition to ownership, since authoring it IS making a coverage determination — the same authority level as approving a report. Explicit adjuster attestation + approval-before-clean-export is inherited unmodified from the app's existing generic `/approve` endpoint (full name/license number/license state/company + `confirmReview: true`), so an un-reviewed/revised letter always exports DRAFT-watermarked. **Jurisdiction-specific wording (appeal/appraisal/statutory-notice language) has NOT been reviewed by legal/compliance counsel for any specific jurisdiction** — the letter's own fixed attestation notice states this explicitly, and it must receive that review before production use.

**Acceptance criteria:** Letter generates only from a finalized report + approved estimate (both gates independently tested); every payment figure matches hand-verified math including the client sample's own numbers; revision history tracked (append-only); PDF/DOCX layout matches the sample's structure (Applicable Policy Coverages, Item-by-Item Coverage Rationale, Items Pending Further Review, Payment Calculation, Understanding Depreciation, Your Rights & Next Steps, Enclosures, Revision History, Adjuster Review & Sign-Off).

**Testing/verification steps:** Unit tests for every calculation/validation path + the source-eligibility gate + role-capability matrix; end-to-end generation/revision/export test; manual PDF visual check against the sample layout.

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-08-24), per explicit instruction ("implement Phase 39 only") using the approved authoring model above. Full detail in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 39 entry.

Full requirement detail lives in the client's change-request Google Doc, the two sample report PDFs (original contractor report vs. redesigned Section-7 sample), and this session's reconciled audit (chat record) — none are repo files; phases below state conclusions and cite exact `origin/main` file:line evidence rather than repeating that prose. Unlike the "only one phase at a time" rule at the top of this file, **Phases 40, 41, 44, and 46 have no dependency on each other and are safely parallelizable**; Phases 42/43 depend on 41, Phase 45 depends on 44, Phase 47 depends on 46, Phase 48 depends on 41/44/45, and Phase 49 depends on all of them — see each phase's own Dependencies field.

## Phase 40 — Watermark Policy Centralization & Approval-Workflow Verification

**Objective:** Make the draft-vs-finalized watermark rule and the Starter-tier branding watermark rule two independently switchable settings driven by one central policy function, fix the misleading unconditional "exports are now clean" UI copy, and manually verify the approval/sign-off workflow end-to-end against the literal client complaint.

**Reason this phase is required:** 2026-09-18 reconciled audit — the draft watermark (`DRAFT — PENDING ADJUSTER REVIEW`) already clears correctly on `/approve` (`isReviewed()`, `backend/routes/reports.js:2735-2736`); the client's supplied sample report was simply never approved (blank sign-off block). Separately, `tier.watermark` (Starter-only, `backend/config/tiers.js`) is an unrelated, pre-existing monetization entitlement that survives approval. Whether Starter's branding watermark should also disappear post-approval is Open Question #16 — pending, not resolved by this phase.

**Scope:** No change to *when* a report becomes reviewed. Refactor the two existing independent conditions (`draftWatermark`, `tier.watermark`) behind one named policy function so either final interpretation is a one-flag change, not an export-path rewrite. Fix `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`'s unconditional success toast. Confirm (don't rebuild) that PDF/DOCX/HTML already honor one shared computed value.

**Reuse:** Existing `isReviewed()`, `draftWatermark`/`watermarkText` computation (`reports.js:4516-4519, 4681, 4816`), `watermarkService.js` overlay, `tiers.js` — this phase wraps/relabels, does not replace.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Extract the watermark decision into one pure function (e.g. `resolveWatermarkPolicy(report, tier, planConfig)` → `{show, text}`), reading a new `PlanConfig` flag (default = today's behavior: Starter keeps its branding watermark post-approval) for Open Question #16.
2. Route all three export paths through this one function (they already consume one computed value at `reports.js:4763-4793` — confirm call sites, don't duplicate logic).
3. Fix `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`'s unconditional toast (`~L74`, "exports are now clean") to reflect tier-conditional reality.
4. Confirm/improve UI discoverability of the `/approve` action (the sample report's blank sign-off block suggests a workflow gap, not a code bug).

**Data model / API implications:** One new boolean in `PlanConfig` (Phase 44); ship with a hardcoded default until Phase 44 lands, then wire through.

**Security / privacy:** None new — reaffirm export gating stays fully server-side; no client-supplied flag can suppress a watermark.

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/routes/reports.js` (watermark computation), `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`/`EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` (equivalent approve-success copy if present).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None — parallelizable with Phase 41/44/46.

**Client/commercial decisions (blocking release, not blocking build):** Open Question #16.

**Automated validation:** Unit tests for `resolveWatermarkPolicy` covering all 4 combinations (draft×tier, reviewed×tier) under both flag settings.

**Manual/runtime validation:** Generate → export before approval (confirm DRAFT text) → approve via the full sign-off flow → export again (confirm correct text per tier/flag) across PDF/DOCX/HTML.

**Backward compatibility / regression:** Legacy `'approved'`/`'completed'` statuses must keep clearing the draft gate exactly as today (`isReviewed()` unchanged).

**Out of scope:** Who can approve a report, the sign-off form itself, report immutability.

**Rollback/recovery:** Pure refactor + one config flag — revert by defaulting the flag to current behavior; no data migration involved.

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-09-18). Open Question #16 resolved by explicit client confirmation ("Yes, the watermark should stay after approval on the free version.") — Starter/free-plan reports keep the FlacronAI branding watermark after approval; only a paid, non-watermarked tier exports clean. No `PlanConfig` flag was introduced (Phase 44 doesn't exist yet) — the policy is a hardcoded rule in the new resolver, matching today's confirmed-correct behavior; a future `PlanConfig` toggle can still wrap it without another export-path rewrite.

Implementation: new pure resolver `backend/utils/watermarkPolicy.js` — `resolveWatermarkPolicy({ reportStatus, tierWatermark })` → `{ show, kind: 'draft'|'branding'|'none', text }`, reading only the server-persisted report status and the server-resolved tier config (never a client-supplied field). Reviewed-status set (`finalized`/`approved`/`completed`) is exported as `REVIEWED_STATUSES` and shared with `reports.js`'s existing `isReviewed()` so the two can't drift. `reports.js`'s export route (`POST /:id/export`) now calls the resolver once and threads the same `{show, text}` through the PDF watermark overlay, `pdfOptions.watermark`/`watermarkText` (consumed by the DOCX generator), and the HTML export — one computed value, three surfaces, as the original scope assumed.

**Real bug found and fixed along the way (not in the original scope, found via the new unit tests):** `properPdfGenerator.js`'s and `documentGenerator.js`'s cover-page "Status" field/draft badge had their own independent `report.status !== 'finalized'` check, recognizing only the literal `finalized` status — a report with a legacy `approved`/`completed` status (reviewed per `isReviewed()`/the watermark policy) would still show a "DRAFT — PENDING ADJUSTER REVIEW" badge and "Draft — pending adjuster review" status label on its cover page, even though its watermark was already correctly the branding/none mark. Both now import `REVIEWED_STATUSES` from the new resolver module and use it for this check, so cover-page status wording and the watermark are always consistent for legacy statuses too.

**Frontend copy fix:** `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`'s `ApproveModal`, `Dashboard.jsx`, and `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` all fired an unconditional `toast.success('Report approved & finalized — exports are now clean')` on approval, which is false for a Starter-tier report. All three now branch on the user's `tier` (already available via `useAuth()` in each component): Starter gets "DRAFT watermark removed (Starter plan reports keep the FlacronAI watermark)", every other tier gets "exports are now watermark-free". `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`'s modal body copy was similarly extended to state the Starter-vs-paid distinction explicitly rather than leaving it implicit.

**Automated validation:** `backend/test/watermark-policy.test.js` (20 tests, all passing) — the resolver's full policy matrix (draft×tier, reviewed×tier, all 3 legacy statuses, missing/unknown status failing safe to draft, a simulated manipulated-payload object proving the resolver has no channel for a client override) plus cross-surface consistency tests that render real PDF (via `pdf-lib` text extraction), DOCX (via `pizzip` XML inspection), and HTML output for each policy outcome and assert the correct text appears and the other watermark text never does. Full backend suite: 629/629 passing (0 regressions). Backend lint: 0 errors (64 pre-existing warnings, unchanged). Frontend suite: 94/94 real tests passing (1 pre-existing, unrelated suite failure — `apiMfaHeader.test.js` — caused by this isolated worktree lacking the real `frontend/.env` Firebase key that the main working tree has; confirmed unrelated to this phase's files). Frontend lint: 0 errors (14 pre-existing warnings, unchanged). Frontend production build: clean.

**Manual/runtime validation:** Exercised `resolveWatermarkPolicy` and the full PDF/DOCX/HTML render pipeline directly (not through a running server) for every required combination — draft+Starter, draft+paid, finalized+Starter, legacy `approved`+Starter, legacy `completed`+Starter, finalized+paid, legacy `approved`+paid, missing/unknown status — confirming: the draft watermark exists pre-approval, disappears after approval, Starter branding persists after approval, and paid-tier finalized output is watermark-free, with no output ever containing both watermark texts at once.

**Files changed:** `backend/utils/watermarkPolicy.js` (new), `backend/routes/reports.js`, `backend/utils/properPdfGenerator.js`, `backend/utils/documentGenerator.js`, `backend/test/watermark-policy.test.js` (new), `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`.

## Phase 41 — Canonical Structured Estimate Data Model & Calculation Engine

**Objective:** Build the versioned, structured Property → Level → Room/Area → Trade → Line Item estimate data model and its deterministic, cents-based calculation engine, owned by the primary report — the foundation every other estimating phase builds on.

**Reason this phase is required:** Change-request Section 7 hierarchy requirement. Reconciled audit's Option C decision: canonical data lives with/linked to the primary report; the existing `RepairEstimate` (`backend/routes/reports.js:3373-3479`, `backend/utils/estimateCalculations.js`) stays a fully separate downstream document with its own independent approval-before-invoice lifecycle — reused here for its calculation *approach* only, never as this feature's live source of truth (avoids two independently editable totals).

**Scope:** Backend data model, validation, and calculation engine only. No AI pricing (Phase 43), no PDF rendering (Phase 42).

**Reuse:** `estimateCalculations.js`'s cents-based math and validation style (`cleanString`, required/range checks, `MAX_LINE_ITEMS`-style bounding); `RepairEstimate`'s `revision`/`revisionHistory` pattern for versioning — capped this time (the existing `revisionHistory` array has no observed cap; do not repeat that gap).

**Exact tasks:**
1. Define schema: `Estimate` (linked to a primary report, `schemaVersion`, `status`, `revision`), `EstimateRoom` (level, room, height, floor/wall/ceiling SF, perimeter, measurementSource, verificationStatus), `EstimateLineItem` (code, trade, category, action, description, qty, unit, materialCost/laborCost/equipmentCost, unitPrice, lineTotal — all cents-based, server-computed), evidence links (photoId/damageFindingId).
2. Implement the centrally configurable unit/action vocabulary superset (includes `LOAD`/`VISIT`/`Secure`/`Stabilize`/`Allowance`/`Finish`/`Texture`/`Manage`/`Monitor`/`Dry`), canonical code + display label pair, admin-editable via Phase 44's `PlanConfig`.
3. Implement rollups: line → category → room → level → trade → direct cost → final total (+ tax/O&P/manual adjustments, contractor/adjuster-entered only, never AI-decided).
4. Implement add/delete/duplicate/move/edit/override-with-reason, each recalculating immediately server-side; preserve the original AI-suggested value alongside any override.
5. Implement a bounded, subcollection-backed audit trail (not an unbounded array).
6. Implement concurrency-safe writes via `db.runTransaction`, following `appendStagedPhoto`'s proven idiom (`backend/utils/photoDraftStaging.js:26-45`).
7. Add the `schemaVersion` + legacy-fallback contract: a report with no structured estimate renders via today's summary-only path (Phase 42 consumes this).
8. Implement bidirectional evidence linking: an `EstimateLineItem` references supporting photo/damage-finding IDs, **and** the referenced photo/finding record gains a reverse `relatedLineItemIds` index, kept in sync on every write (not derived ad hoc) — see Calculation Contract below.

**Calculation contract (explicit monetary semantics):**
- `materialCost`/`laborCost`/`equipmentCost` are optional **per-line-item component costs**, not per-unit; when present, `unitPrice = materialCost + laborCost + equipmentCost` (cents) and the server rejects a submitted `unitPrice` that doesn't reconcile to its components within rounding tolerance; when components are absent, `unitPrice` is entered/suggested directly.
- `lineTotal = round(quantity × unitPrice)`, integer cents throughout, no floats — same discipline as `estimateCalculations.js`.
- Rollup order: line → category subtotal → room subtotal → level subtotal → trade subtotal → **direct cost** (sum of room/level totals) → taxable basis (direct cost minus items flagged non-taxable) → tax (taxable basis × rate) → overhead/profit (basis configurable — direct cost, or direct cost + tax — contractor/adjuster-controlled, matching the Invoice O&P precedent from commit `e3a95f0`) → permits/general conditions/manual adjustments (labeled, user-entered lines, never AI-generated) → **final total**.
- Every total at every level is server-recomputed from stored line items on every read/write; a client- or AI-supplied total is never persisted as-is.
- The retained Section 7 summary-category totals must be produced by summing these same detailed line items (not maintained as a separate figure) — enforced by a dedicated reconciliation unit test, not visual inspection alone.
- One currency (ISO 4217) per estimate, stored once at the estimate level; a line item cannot specify a different currency without explicit multi-currency/conversion support (out of scope this phase) — mixed-currency input is rejected with a clear error, not silently converted.

**Data model / API implications:** New collection/subcollection (favor a subcollection given `RepairEstimate`'s observed 1MB-doc-size exposure); new endpoints for estimate CRUD, mirroring `loadOwnedReport`/`getReportAccess` ownership checks; reverse evidence index lives alongside existing photo/damage-finding records.

**Security / privacy:** Same ownership/role checks as every other report sub-resource; server recomputes every total from stored inputs, never trusts a client-supplied total.

**Files/components likely affected:** New `backend/utils/canonicalEstimateCalculations.js` (or extension of `estimateCalculations.js` — decide at implementation time), new route additions in `backend/routes/reports.js`, new `backend/test/canonical-estimate-*.test.js`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None — foundational; must land before Phase 42/43.

**Automated validation:** Unit tests for every rollup level against hand-verified totals (mirroring Phase 37/39's exact-match precedent); validation-rejection tests (negative qty, unknown code, oversized array, duplicate code); a concurrency test mirroring `photo-draft-staging.test.js`'s fake-Firestore contention pattern.

**Manual/runtime validation:** Create an estimate via API against a real report, verify totals by hand, confirm an override preserves the original AI-suggested value.

**Backward compatibility / regression:** A regression test asserting a legacy report (no `schemaVersion`) is provably unaffected by this phase's code.

**Out of scope:** AI pricing, PDF/DOCX/HTML rendering, property data as a pricing input (accept a generic locale string for now).

**Rollback/recovery:** Fully additive — no existing collection/field touched; safe to disable via the `schemaVersion` gate without deleting data.

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-09-18), per explicit instruction ("implement Phase 41 only"). Full detail in `CONTEXT.md`'s "Current Active Phase" section; summary below.

Implementation: two new, dependency-free-of-Firestore modules. `backend/utils/canonicalEstimate.js` is the pure validation + cents-based calculation engine (schema normalization, line-item/permit/general-condition/manual-adjustment validation, component reconciliation with override provenance, the documented roll-up order, category/room/trade rollups, the bidirectional evidence-index computation). `backend/utils/canonicalEstimateStore.js` is the Firestore persistence layer, reusing `appendStagedPhoto`'s transactional idiom (`db.runTransaction`, 15 max attempts) to read the parent report + the singleton `reports/{id}/canonicalEstimate/current` doc, validate/recompute, and atomically write both the estimate doc and the parent report's `photos[].relatedLineItemIds` reverse index, plus one entry in the bounded `reports/{id}/canonicalEstimateAudit` subcollection (not an unbounded array — the exact gap flagged against `RepairEstimate.revisionHistory`). Two new routes on `backend/routes/reports.js`, reachable at the primary versioned path `GET`/`PUT /api/v1/reports/:id/canonical-estimate` (and the backward-compatible unversioned `/api/reports/...` alias, same as every other report route via `server.js`'s `API_PREFIXES`): `GET` (owner or any view+ grantee; returns `{estimate: null, legacy: true}` for a report with none) and `PUT` (owner-with-`canEditReports` or review-grantee, same access pattern as `PUT /:id/estimate`; 409 `REPORT_FINALIZED` when the parent report's status is `finalized`, matching the existing `isFinalizedContentEdit` precedent exactly). The `RepairEstimate` document and its routes/calculations (`estimateCalculations.js`) were not modified at all — a fully separate, untouched lifecycle, per the approved architecture. Backend: 41 new tests pass (31 pure calculation/validation, 10 transactional persistence), 671/671 full suite (0 regressions), 0 lint errors (64 pre-existing warnings, unchanged). No frontend files were touched (Phase 42 owns the UI) so no frontend re-test was needed. See `CONTEXT.md` for the full schema field list and the exact roll-up order as implemented.

**Correction (2026-09-18, same-day focused review):** the tax/O&P roll-up order above was initially documented and implemented incorrectly — O&P was folded into the sales-tax basis. A focused review against the already-verified, authoritative rule in `estimateCalculations.js`/`invoiceCalculations.js` (O&P is added to the total but is never itself taxed; verified case: $550.00 services, O&P 10% = $55.00, tax = 8% of $550.00 = $44.00, total = $649.00) found and fixed the discrepancy in `canonicalEstimate.js`'s `computeRollups`: `taxableBasisCents` no longer includes `overheadProfitCents`. 4 new regression tests were added (the exact $550/$55/$44/$649 case, O&P-added-once/never-taxed, non-taxable-line-item exclusion, explicitly-taxable-permit inclusion) and 2 existing tests' hardcoded expected values were corrected. The documented API path was also corrected to identify `/api/v1/reports/:id/canonical-estimate` as the primary versioned path (server.js already mounts every report route under both `/api` and `/api/v1`; no routing code changed). See `CONTEXT.md`'s "Current Active Phase" section for the full corrected calculation order and verified-rule detail.

## Phase 42 — Section 7 Rendering, Editor & Report Integration

**Objective:** Render the canonical estimate (Phase 41) as the report's Section 7 — existing high-level summary retained, new room/trade/line-item detail beneath it — across PDF/DOCX/HTML, plus the frontend editor UI, without touching Sections 1-6/8-9 or breaking TOC/pagination.

**Reason this phase is required:** Change-request core deliverable. Reconciled audit: current dynamic TOC (`backend/utils/properPdfGenerator.js`, commit `e3a95f0`) already tracks physical pages via in-content heading detection (`pageCounter`/`recordTocHeadingPage`) with dedicated regression tests (`backend/test/pdf-toc.test.js`) — extend that mechanism, don't rebuild it; use continuous physical pages with in-flow subheadings, not artificial `7A-7F` lettering (the redesigned sample's own footer-numbering mismatch demonstrates why).

**Scope:** Rendering + editor UI only; consumes Phase 41's data, does not change how it's calculated.

**Reuse:** The existing `pageCounter`/`recordTocHeadingPage` TOC mechanism — Section 7's detail must be drawn as part of the same linear render pass so Section 8/9's TOC entries keep resolving correctly automatically. Existing generic table-drawing helpers used by `RepairEstimate`/Invoice PDF rendering. `RepairEstimateModal`'s form patterns for the new line-item editor.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Extend `properPdfGenerator.js`/`documentGenerator.js`/HTML export to draw the retained summary table, then in-flow room/trade/line-item detail ("Area 1 — ..." subheadings, continuous pages), sourced from Phase 41; fall back to today's markdown-table rendering when no structured estimate exists.
2. Escape all rendered text properly (eliminate the sample's `R&R;`-artifact class of bug).
3. Build the Section 7 editor UI (add/delete/duplicate/move/edit/override-with-reason, live recalculation, verification-status badges, evidence/photo linking) reusing `RepairEstimateModal`'s interaction patterns.
4. Confirm existing finalized-report immutability blocks silent Section 7 edits post-approval, consistent with every other section.

**Data model / API implications:** Frontend calls Phase 41's endpoints; no new backend data model.

**Security / privacy:** Editor respects existing role/ownership checks; server remains the sole source of computed totals.

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/utils/properPdfGenerator.js`, `backend/utils/documentGenerator.js`, HTML export path, `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`/`EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`/`ReportPreviewPage.jsx`, `backend/test/pdf-toc.test.js` (extend).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 41.

**Automated validation:** Extend `pdf-toc.test.js`'s exact pattern (TOC-matches-real-page, footer-label-matches-physical-page, multi-page-section-first-page-reference) to documents with the new detailed Section 7; assertion tests confirming Sections 1-6/8-9 are unchanged when Section 7 grows.

**Manual/runtime validation:** Generate a report with a multi-room estimate, export PDF/DOCX/HTML, visually confirm TOC accuracy, footer page numbers, no escaping artifacts, and unchanged Sections 1-6/8-9.

**Backward compatibility / regression:** A report with no structured estimate renders exactly as today (regression-tested).

**Out of scope:** Any change to Sections 1-6/8-9; AI pricing generation (Phase 43).

**Rollback/recovery:** Legacy fallback means this can ship disabled (no structured estimate = old behavior) with zero risk to existing reports.

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-09-18).

Implementation: rather than teaching PDF/DOCX/HTML each a second, bespoke drawing routine for the detailed estimate, a new pure module `backend/utils/canonicalEstimateContent.js` renders Phase 41's canonical estimate into ONE normalized markdown string (`buildSection7DetailMarkdown`) and splices it (`injectSection7Detail`) into an in-memory copy of the report's own `content` -- never persisted to Firestore -- immediately beneath the existing "## SECTION 7" heading and before the next top-level heading (or at the end, if Section 7 is last). Every export format (`properPdfGenerator.js`/`documentGenerator.js`/`generateHTML`) already parses that same `##`/`###`/`|table|`/`- bullet` markdown dialect out of `report.content` (the same mechanism `estimateContent.js` uses for the deterministic RepairEstimate/Invoice documents), so the new detail automatically inherits correct pagination, the existing dynamic TOC's page-tracking (`recordTocHeadingPage`, unmodified), page-break table-header repeat, and per-format text escaping (PDFKit literal text, DOCX `escapeXml`, HTML `escapeHtml`) with zero new rendering/escaping code to audit. `backend/routes/reports.js`'s export route fetches the canonical estimate (`getCanonicalEstimate`) once per export, builds the detail markdown, and passes a shallow-cloned `renderReport` (only `.content` differs) to whichever generator the requested format calls -- `format: 'pdf'|'docx'|'html'` all consume the identical spliced content, so there is exactly one calculation/formatting path, not three.

**Section 7 structure:** a "### Detailed Repair Estimate Breakdown" sub-heading with metadata bullets (Pricing Source -- "Manually entered by preparer" today, or "Pricing Source generated by: Flacron Engine" once Phase 43 sets `pricingSource: 'ai_suggested'`, which Phase 42 code never does; Status -- "Preliminary / Editable" pre-finalization, "Final — Approved" once reviewed; Estimate Revision; Currency), then one "### Area: {room}" sub-heading per room, each with a "**Trade / Category: {trade}**" block per trade containing a compact Description/Repair Action/Qty/Unit Price/Line Total table plus an extended bullet per line item (material, component cost breakdown, taxable, confidence, status, override provenance, evidence count, assumptions) -- avoiding one overcrowded mega-table per the spec. A "### Financial Summary" table reproduces Phase 41's exact server totals (line-item subtotal, permits, GC, manual adjustments, direct cost, O&P + rate, taxable basis explicitly labeled "excludes Overhead & Profit," tax + rate, Total Estimate) with a closing note stating O&P is never folded into the tax basis under the current rules.

**Editor:** `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx`'s new `CanonicalEstimateEditor` (a "Detailed Estimate (Section 7)" button alongside the existing Repair Estimate button, offered on the same primary-report document types, hidden for RepairEstimate/Invoice/CoverageDeterminationLetter/MoldSupplement derivative documents). Supports create/edit/reorder(move up/down)/remove line items with stable client-generated IDs (never array indexes), room/trade grouping fields, description/repair action/material, quantity+unit (validated unit dropdown), material/labor/equipment cost inputs with a live computed unit price, an explicit override checkbox+reason (provenance-preserving, mirrors the backend contract), taxable/confidence/status controls, assumptions, permits/general conditions/manual adjustments lists (own add/remove, taxable flag each), O&P%/tax%/currency/region fields, and a bidirectional evidence-photo picker (thumbnails fetched the same way `SectionedReportEditor`'s existing `PhotoPickerModal` does, scoped to only this report's own uploaded photos -- a cross-report ID is structurally impossible since the picker only ever lists `reportsAPI.getPhotos(report.id)`'s own results). States handled: loading, load-error+Retry, empty (seeds one blank line item), saving (button disabled, guarded re-entrantly against double-submit), a clearly-labeled client PREVIEW total (replaced by the PUT response's authoritative `totals` on success), validation errors (client-side pre-check + server error surfaced verbatim), 409 `REVISION_CONFLICT` (banner with an explicit "Discard & Reload" action -- never silently overwritten or silently discarded), 409 `REPORT_FINALIZED`/read-only mode (finalized report or non-`canEdit` viewer renders every control disabled with a Lock-badge banner, matching the rest of the page's immutability pattern), 403 unauthorized, and network errors (Retry button that resubmits the same payload). Closing with unsaved changes prompts a confirmation (same `window.confirm` pattern already used elsewhere in this codebase) rather than silently discarding.

**Server authority / revision conflicts:** `backend/utils/canonicalEstimateStore.js`'s `upsertCanonicalEstimate` gained an optional `body.baseRevision` check (backward compatible -- omitted entirely, it's a no-op, preserving today's last-write-wins for any future non-interactive caller): if the caller's `baseRevision` doesn't match the currently persisted revision, the write is rejected as `REVISION_CONFLICT` (409, `err.currentRevision` included) before any validation/compute runs, so a stale edit can never silently clobber a newer save. The editor always sends the revision it loaded. Every other server-authority guarantee is Phase 41's, unchanged: the PUT response's `totals` are the only ones ever displayed after save; a manipulated client totals payload is ignored (already tested in Phase 41 and re-exercised at the rendering layer in the new content tests); finalized-report immutability and ownership/role checks are the same `REPORT_FINALIZED`/403 codes the route already returned.

**Legacy fallback:** `buildSection7DetailMarkdown` returns `''` for a null/line-item-less estimate, making `injectSection7Detail` a documented no-op -- a report with no canonical estimate (or an estimate that predates line items) renders Section 7 exactly as before, with no empty heading and no fabricated content. A document type whose numbered "SECTION 7" heading isn't the cost-estimate section (e.g. a Liability report's "Recommendations") is intentionally left to today's number-anchored matching rather than a bespoke per-type exception -- documented and locked by its own test, since canonical estimates are a generic, report-type-agnostic feature and adding claim-type-specific injection targeting was judged out of this phase's scope.

**RepairEstimate relationship:** untouched. No route, calculation module, or lifecycle of the existing standalone `RepairEstimate`/Invoice documents was read or modified by this phase.

**Automated validation:** 3 new backend test files (`canonical-estimate-content.test.js` -- 15 pure unit tests for the markdown builder/injector, including the $550/$55/$44/$649 reference case reproduced in the rendered summary, hostile-input table/heading-injection safety, and a manipulated-field-is-ignored check; `canonical-estimate-export.test.js` -- 7 tests generating REAL PDF bytes (parsed via the existing `pdfTextExtract` helper), REAL DOCX XML (via `pizzip`, matching `watermark-policy.test.js`'s convention), and REAL HTML strings (via `reportsRouter._test.generateHTML`), covering: detail-beneath-existing-summary with exact totals, dynamic-TOC Section 8 landing on its true later physical page once a 40-line-item estimate is injected, DOCX/HTML totals matching the PDF, HTML script-injection escaping, legacy no-op fallback across all 3 formats, the non-numbered-Section-7 document-type behavior, and finalized-report cover/watermark conventions being unaffected; 4 new tests appended to `canonical-estimate-persistence.test.js` for the revision-conflict contract (stale rejected + unmodified store, matching baseRevision succeeds, omitted baseRevision preserves last-write-wins)). Full backend suite: **694/694 passing** (23 new, 0 regressions). Backend lint: 0 errors (64 pre-existing warnings, unchanged). Frontend: 0 lint errors (14 pre-existing warnings, unchanged), clean production build, **94/94 real tests passing** (the same 1 pre-existing environment-only failure this worktree has had since Phase 40 -- missing `frontend/.env` Firebase key, unrelated to this phase's files).

**Manual/runtime validation:** exercised the exact composition the export route performs (`getCanonicalEstimate` → `buildSection7DetailMarkdown` → `injectSection7Detail` → `generatePDF`/`generateDOCX`/`generateHTML`) against real generated bytes for a short (1 line item) and a long (40 line items across 8 rooms/5 trades) estimate, confirmed identical totals across all three formats and against Phase 41's own hand-verified reference case, confirmed the TOC's Section 8 entry lands on the real later page once Section 7 grows, and confirmed no output ever double-renders the summary or shows a blank/broken table for the no-estimate legacy path. **Honest limitation:** the editor UI itself (browser rendering, click-through states, thumbnail loading) was not visually exercised in a running browser in this session -- no browser-automation tool was available; correctness was verified via lint/build/unit-test coverage of its logic (payload construction, validation, error-state branching) plus the underlying API contract's own test coverage, not a live click-through. Evidence photos are shown as a text reference (count + inline detail bullet) in PDF/DOCX/HTML exports, not embedded thumbnails per line item -- a deliberate scope-minimizing choice (potentially hundreds of line items x up to 30 photos each would otherwise bloat every export) documented here rather than silently limited; the desktop editor itself does show real thumbnails.

**Files changed:** `backend/utils/canonicalEstimateContent.js` (new), `backend/utils/canonicalEstimateStore.js` (added `baseRevision` conflict check), `backend/routes/reports.js` (export route wiring + `REVISION_CONFLICT` → 409 mapping), `backend/test/canonical-estimate-content.test.js` (new), `backend/test/canonical-estimate-export.test.js` (new), `backend/test/canonical-estimate-persistence.test.js` (4 new tests appended), `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx` (new `CanonicalEstimateEditor` + button wiring), `frontend/src/services/api.js` (`getCanonicalEstimate`/`saveCanonicalEstimate`).

### 2026-09-19 — focused Phase 42 completion-audit correction (4 checks; real gaps found and fixed in 2 of 4)

**CHECK 1 (Section 7 replacement vs. duplication) — real bug, fixed.** The original implementation *appended* the canonical detail beneath the legacy AI-narrative Section 7 body, so both were rendered together. Corrected: `injectSection7Detail` now **replaces** the legacy body between the "## SECTION 7" heading and the next top-level heading (heading line itself, and Section 8+, both untouched) -- never both at once. The frontend desktop preview previously bypassed this entirely (it rendered raw `report.content`, so a canonical estimate never showed up there at all) -- fixed by having `GET /:id/canonical-estimate` also return the server-computed `detailMarkdown`, and adding a small frontend mirror of the same splice algorithm (`frontend/src/utils/canonicalEstimateContent.js`, pure, no deps) so PDF/DOCX/HTML/desktop preview all now follow the identical replace-not-append selection rule. Regression tests added/rewritten (backend `canonical-estimate-content.test.js`/`canonical-estimate-export.test.js`; frontend `canonicalEstimateContent.test.js`) proving: (1) canonical data replaces the legacy body, (2) old Section 7 text is absent, (3) Section 8+ remain byte-identical, (4) a legacy-only report (`detailMarkdown === ''`) is byte-identical to its input, (5) PDF/DOCX/HTML/desktop preview all apply the same rule.

**CHECK 2 (frontend editor test coverage) — real gap, fixed.** No dedicated frontend tests for `CanonicalEstimateEditor` existed; only backend renderer/persistence tests did. This codebase has no React component-render test infrastructure (no `@testing-library/react`/`jsdom` dependency anywhere), so rather than add one, the editor's entire business logic (state-shape mapping, validation, payload building, preview math, array reorder/remove/toggle, save-error classification, duplicate-submit/unsaved-changes/read-only guards) was extracted into a new pure module `frontend/src/utils/canonicalEstimateEditor.js` and the component refactored to consume it -- matching this codebase's existing convention for `estimateInvoiceEligibility.js`/`invoiceTotals.js`/`reportImmutability.js`. `frontend/src/__tests__/canonicalEstimateEditor.test.js` (41 tests) covers every item the audit listed: loading/empty state, add/edit/delete/reorder with stable IDs, cost components + manual override, permits/GC/adjustments/tax/O&P (including the $550/$55/$44/$649 case reproduced at preview level), authoritative server totals replacing the preview, photo linking/unlinking, validation errors, 409 conflict/403 unauthorized/network+retry classification, duplicate-save prevention, unsaved-changes warning, and finalized read-only.

**CHECK 3 (bidirectional evidence discoverability) — real gap, fixed.** Phase 41 already persisted the photo→line-item reverse index (`photo.relatedLineItemIds`), but `GET /:id/photos` never returned it and no photo UI surfaced it -- the reverse direction was invisible. Minimal fix: the route now includes `relatedLineItemIds` per photo (one-line passthrough), and the shared `PhotoReview.jsx` component (already backing both `Dashboard.jsx`'s report gallery and the standalone `/photos` library -- no redesign, one shared touch-point) gained a small `LinkedEstimateBadge` ("Linked to N estimate line item(s)") shown wherever a photo's analysis panel already renders. Deliberately count-only (no line-item descriptions fetched into the gallery) to avoid a second API call/duplicated estimate copy in an unrelated surface.

**CHECK 4 (test report accuracy) — the prior report's phrasing was accurate but incomplete; here are the exact numbers.** Before this audit: backend 694/694 tests passing (0 failures); frontend **15 of 16 test files passed, 94 of 94 tests within those 15 files passed, and 1 file (`apiMfaHeader.test.js`, 4 tests) failed to even execute** -- not a test assertion failure but a module-load-time crash (`FirebaseError: auth/invalid-api-key`) from `frontend/src/config/firebase.js` calling `getAuth()` with an empty `VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY` (no `frontend/.env` exists in this worktree, only the blank `.env.example`). Confirmed by direct experiment: supplying a syntactically-valid placeholder key (format `AIza...`, no real project, no network access needed -- Firebase Auth only validates the key's *format* at init time) makes all 4 tests in that file pass with zero code changes. A local-only, gitignored `frontend/.env` with placeholder (non-real) values was added to this worktree to unblock full local test execution going forward; it contains no real credentials and is invisible to `git status`/diff. **With it present, the true, complete result is 18/18 frontend test files passing, 143/143 tests passing, 0 failures** (94 pre-existing + 45 new from this audit + 4 previously-blocked). Both `PHASES.md` and `CONTEXT.md` are updated to state 697/697 backend and 143/143 (18/18 files) frontend from this point forward, not the prior ambiguous "94/94 (1 pre-existing failure)" phrasing.

**Additional automated validation (post-correction):** targeted Phase 42 backend tests 67/67 passing; full backend suite **697/697 passing** (0 regressions, +3 from CHECK 1's new regression tests); backend lint 0 errors (64 pre-existing warnings, unchanged); full frontend suite **143/143 passing, 18/18 files** (+45 from CHECK 2/CHECK 1 frontend tests, +4 previously-blocked now unblocked); frontend lint 0 errors (14 pre-existing warnings, unchanged); clean frontend production build; `git diff --check` clean; focused secret scan of every new/changed file clean; scope scan (no Phase 43+/OpenAI/RealtyAPI/Stripe-add-on/mobile references) clean; `mobile/` and the original working tree untouched; no commits/pushes/merges/PRs.

**Files changed in this correction:** `backend/utils/canonicalEstimateContent.js` (replace-not-append rewrite), `backend/routes/reports.js` (GET route now returns `detailMarkdown`; comment updates), `backend/test/canonical-estimate-content.test.js` + `canonical-estimate-export.test.js` (rewritten/extended assertions), `frontend/src/utils/canonicalEstimateContent.js` (new), `frontend/src/utils/canonicalEstimateEditor.js` (new), `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx` (desktop-preview wiring + refactor to use the extracted module), `frontend/src/components/PhotoReview.jsx` (`LinkedEstimateBadge`), `frontend/src/__tests__/canonicalEstimateEditor.test.js` + `canonicalEstimateContent.test.js` (new), `frontend/.env` (new, local-only, gitignored, placeholder values).

## Phase 43 — OpenAI Preliminary Pricing Service

**Objective:** A server-only, isolated OpenAI adapter that suggests preliminary material/labor/equipment/unit pricing for estimate line items, fully separate from the existing Claude/watsonx report/vision pipeline.

**Reason this phase is required:** Client-confirmed decision. Reconciled audit: `aiService.js`'s Claude/watsonx `generateWithFallback` chain (and the vision pipeline) must not be touched — the documented 2026-07-18 OpenAI removal was a provider-strategy choice for narrative/vision generation, not a ban on a narrowly scoped new adapter.

**Scope:** Backend pricing service + schema validation + mocked contract tests. Live verification blocked on the OpenAI API key (dependency, not implementation work).

**Reuse:** `backend/config/anthropic.js`'s adapter shape (`getClient`/`generateText`/`checkHealth`) as the template for a new, never-cross-imported `backend/config/openai.js`.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Build `backend/config/openai.js`: server-only client, structured-output request (material/labor/equipment cost, unit price, currency, assumptions, confidence, pricing date), strict JSON-schema validation of the response.
2. Build a pricing service taking Phase 41's line-item inputs (country/state/city/ZIP, room/area, damage type, repair action, material, qty, unit, current date) and returning suggested values — the server always recomputes `qty × unitPrice` itself.
3. Label all AI-suggested values `Pricing Source generated by: Flacron Engine` / `Status: Preliminary / Editable` in both API response and rendered output.
4. Add timeout/retry/rate-limit handling and per-report batching for cost control; no sensitive claim PII beyond locale/scope in prompts.
5. Add a manual-fallback path (empty/zero suggested values, user fills in manually) if the provider is unavailable — must never block estimate creation.
6. Structure the adapter/service boundary so a future Claude pricing provider could implement the same interface without touching call sites.
7. Tag every suggestion with `pricingProviderVersion`/`pricingModelVersion` and `pricingGeneratedAt` (timestamp), plus a `pricingCacheKey` (locale + scope + model version) with an explicit expiry (e.g. 30 days); a suggestion past expiry is flagged stale and eligible for regeneration on next request, never silently served as current.
8. Enforce per-report/per-organization token and request-count limits, reusing Phase 44's `PlanConfig`, to bound cost.
9. Validate returned currency against Phase 41's single-currency-per-estimate contract; reject/flag a mismatch rather than silently converting.
10. Never overwrite a line item the user has already manually overridden — a regeneration request only populates fields still in the original AI-suggested (non-overridden) state, per Phase 41's original-vs-overridden flag.

**Regional input contract (Phases 46/47 integration):** This service consumes only Phase 46's normalized location fields (country/state/city/postal code/coordinates) and, where available, Phase 47's US property context (property type/construction era only) plus Phase 41's room/trade/repair-scope fields — it never calls Google or RealtyAPI directly. Public-record square footage is never substituted for Phase 41's room-level field/AR measurements; quantities always come from the estimate, never the property record. Missing/unavailable property-provider data never blocks a pricing request — the service falls back to whatever normalized location fields exist, down to country/state alone.

**Data model / API implications:** New endpoint (e.g. `POST /:id/estimate-detail/price-suggestions`); suggested values stored on the line item per Phase 41's original-vs-overridden fields, never overwriting a user override; cache keyed/expired as above.

**Security / privacy:** OpenAI key server-side secret only, never logged, never sent to the frontend; minimal prompt data (no insured PII, no photos).

**Files/components likely affected:** New `backend/config/openai.js`, new `backend/services/pricingService.js`, `backend/test/pricing-service.test.js` (mocked provider).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 41; OpenAI API key for live verification (build/mocked-test complete without it).

**Automated validation:** Schema-validation tests against a mocked response (valid/malformed/timeout/rate-limited); a server-recomputation test proving client/AI-supplied totals are never trusted directly.

**Manual/runtime validation (blocked until key arrives):** One live call per supported locale sample; confirm a malformed response degrades to manual-fallback rather than crashing.

**Backward compatibility / regression:** None — purely additive endpoint.

**Out of scope:** Tax/O&P handling (stays user-controlled, unchanged); any OpenAI use outside this service.

**Rollback/recovery:** Feature disables by simply not calling the pricing endpoint; no other data model depends on it.

**Status:** Complete — Deployment TTL Enablement Pending (see 2026-09-22 live-validation entry below: implementation and live OpenAI provider validation are both complete; only the pre-production Firestore TTL deployment step, tracked as its own checklist item, remains)

### 2026-09-19 — Phase 43 implementation

Implemented in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`, per the phase's own scope above. No OpenAI API key is available in this worktree, so every automated test mocks the SDK boundary (`callModel`/`client` dependency injection, the exact convention this codebase's `image-analysis-batching.test.js` already uses for `callVisionApi`) — the real OpenAI API was never called.

**Architecture:** `backend/config/openai.js` (new) — a lazy, never-throws-at-require-time adapter modeled on `config/anthropic.js`'s shape (`getClient`), used ONLY by this phase; `aiService.js`'s Claude/watsonx report/vision pipeline is completely untouched. `buildPricingRequest` (pure) assembles a Chat Completions `response_format: {type:'json_schema', json_schema:{name, schema, strict:true}}` request; `generateStructuredPricing` calls it, parses the response, and maps every SDK failure into one categorized error (`.code`/`.transient`/`.retryAfterMs`) via `categorize()` — a raw SDK error message/stack is logged server-side only and never returned. `backend/services/pricingProviders/` holds the provider contract: `openaiPricingProvider.js` (thin — builds the sanitized, non-PII prompt/schema and parses raw items, does zero business validation/money math) and `registry.js` (selects the ONE configured provider purely from `PRICING_PROVIDER` env, default `'openai'` — a client request body can never choose provider/model). `backend/services/pricingService.js` is the provider-agnostic orchestration layer: request validation, Firestore-backed caching (`pricingSuggestionsCache/{fingerprint}`, TTL via `PRICING_CACHE_TTL_DAYS`), bounded transient retry with `Retry-After`-aware backoff (`PRICING_MAX_RETRIES`/`PRICING_RETRY_BASE_MS`), strict per-item validation reusing `canonicalEstimate.js`'s own `UNIT_VOCABULARY`/`isValidCurrency`/`validateMoney`/`isFiniteNumber`/`MAX_QTY`, and server-side `unitPrice`/`lineTotal` computation that NEVER reads anything the provider might claim as a total (the schema doesn't even ask for one). `backend/utils/pricingFingerprint.js` (new, pure, Node's built-in `crypto` only) hashes ONLY normalized locale + repair-scope fields (country/state/city/postalCode/room/damageType/repairAction/material/quantity/unit/currency/a month-truncated pricing date/provider/model/promptVersion/schemaVersion) — no reportId/userId/PII ever enters the hash, which is what makes a cache document safe to reuse across different reports/users by construction.

**Design decision (structural, not just a prompt instruction):** the provider is asked ONLY to suggest `materialUnitCost`/`laborUnitCost`/`equipmentUnitCost`/`trade`/`category`/`confidence`/`assumptions` per item. `room`/`damageType`/`repairAction`/`description`/`material`/`quantity`/`unit` are ALWAYS taken from the caller's own request, never from the provider's response (proven by a dedicated test injecting an "attacker-controlled" provider response that tries to change the scope — the server-returned proposal item still has the original request's scope, unchanged). This makes "AI never invents what is being repaired, only price components for what the user said is being repaired" a structural property, not a request to the model that could be ignored.

**Endpoint:** one new route, `POST /:id/estimate-detail/price-suggestions` on the existing `backend/routes/reports.js` router (auto-mounted at both `/api` and `/api/v1`), following the exact `GET`/`PUT /:id/canonical-estimate` pattern for auth/access/errors: `authenticateAny` + `requireApiScope('reports:write')` + a new dedicated `pricingLimiter` (`backend/middleware/rateLimiters.js`, 10/min per user by default, `PRICING_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MIN`), 404 if the report doesn't exist or the caller has no access grant, 403 `TEAM_PERMISSION_DENIED`/`SHARE_PERMISSION_DENIED` for an owner-without-`canEditReports`/a below-`review` grantee, 409 `REPORT_FINALIZED` if the report is finalized (blocks even generating a new proposal), a categorized `{success,error,code}` response for every `PRICING_*` code the service can throw, and a `recordAuditLog` entry containing only counts/fingerprint-adjacent metadata (item count, cache status) — never the prompt/response text. **Generates and returns a proposal only — persists nothing.** `AbortController`/`req.on('close', ...)` wiring cancels the in-flight provider call if the client disconnects.

**Frontend workflow (extends the existing editor, no second editor/save path built):** `frontend/src/utils/pricingSuggestions.js` (new, pure, Vitest-unit-tested, same no-jsdom convention as `canonicalEstimateEditor.js`) provides the request-payload builder, a client-side pre-validator, an error classifier (`classifyPriceSuggestionsError`, mirroring `classifyCanonicalEstimateSaveError`'s pattern), and the safety-critical `mergeProposedPricingIntoLineItems(existingLineItems, proposedItems, acceptedProposalItemIds)`: matches a proposal to an existing draft item first by `sourceProposalId` (idempotent re-apply — regenerating + re-accepting the same proposal updates the same item rather than duplicating it, whether that item was originally merged into an existing target or appended as new) then by `targetLineItemId`; SKIPS (never overwrites) an item with `overrideActive === true`, reporting the skip back to the UI; appends a brand-new draft line item (via the existing `emptyCanonicalLineItem()`/`newStableId('li')`) when there's no match. `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx`'s `CanonicalEstimateEditor` gained a "Preliminary Pricing (Flacron Engine)" collapsible panel (hidden entirely when `readOnly`, per-line-item "Price this" checkboxes, country/state/city/postal inputs, Generate/Cancel/Regenerate/Retry controls covering loading/error/empty/ready states, and a per-item Accept checkbox + Apply button) that merges accepted items into the SAME draft state the pre-existing, unmodified Save button already persists through the pre-existing, unmodified `PUT /:id/canonical-estimate` — Save/conflict-banner/revision-conflict handling needed zero changes and were confirmed still functioning via the unmodified `save()` path. `frontend/src/services/api.js` gained `reportsAPI.getPriceSuggestions(id, data, signal)` next to `getCanonicalEstimate`/`saveCanonicalEstimate`.

**Scoped completion of a gap Phase 41/42's own docs flagged as deferred here (2 minimal, additive edits to those "Complete" files, both regression-tested):**
1. `backend/utils/canonicalEstimate.js`'s `validateLineItems` no longer unconditionally discards a submitted `providerModel` object — it now preserves it (3 length-capped strings only, never trusted for money) so it survives the pricing-proposal accept→apply→save round trip for audit. A legacy caller that omits it still gets exactly `{provider:null,model:null,promptVersion:null}`, proven by a dedicated regression test.
2. `validateAndComputeCanonicalEstimate`'s estimate-level `pricingSourceMeta.source` is now computed SERVER-SIDE from the just-validated line items (`'ai_suggested'` if any line item is, else `'manual'`) instead of being hardcoded `'manual'` — completing exactly what `canonicalEstimateContent.js`'s own Phase-42-era comment said Phase 43 would finish. Proven end-to-end through the real, unmodified `buildSection7DetailMarkdown` rendering pipeline: an estimate with an `ai_suggested` line item now genuinely renders "Pricing Source: Pricing Source generated by: Flacron Engine" (and never the provider/model name), while an all-manual estimate is byte-identical to before (still "Manually entered by preparer").
Every other Phase 41/42 file/behavior — the $550/$55/$44/$649 roll-up math, the finalized-report immutability guard, the export renderers, the watermark policy — is untouched; both edits are additive and regression-tested.

**Privacy/security:** the prompt payload contains ONLY locale + repair-scope fields (proven by a test asserting a fabricated set of PII strings never appears in the built prompt); no insured name/email/phone/policy/claim number/photos/signatures/payment/narrative text ever reaches the provider. A raw OpenAI SDK error (including any string that could contain a key/auth header) never reaches an HTTP response or a log line outside `config/openai.js`'s own server-side `console.error`. The `pricingSuggestionsCache` document schema is `{fingerprint, item, generatedAt, expiresAt}` only — no reportId/userId/PII field, proven by a dedicated test.

**Caching/cost-limit behavior:** cache hit/miss/expiry/explicit-regenerate-bypass all tested; a cache hit skips the provider entirely; an expired entry is treated as a miss and regenerated; fingerprint determinism (same normalized input → same hash across independent Firestore instances) tested; a cache-hit proposal is still subject to the same override-skip rule as a fresh one (tested). Config seam for a future Phase 44 `PlanConfig` (not built here): `OPENAI_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_MODEL`/`OPENAI_TIMEOUT_MS`/`OPENAI_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`/`PRICING_PROVIDER`/`PRICING_MAX_ITEMS_PER_REQUEST`/`PRICING_MAX_RETRIES`/`PRICING_RETRY_BASE_MS`/`PRICING_CACHE_TTL_DAYS`/`PRICING_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MIN`, all documented with defaults in `backend/.env.example`.

**Live-validation checklist (verbatim, recorded for later use once the client provides a real OpenAI key):**
1. Place the client-provided key in a local/server secret only (never commit it).
2. **Enable the Firestore TTL policy on `pricingProposals` (deployment step, not gated on the OpenAI key — do this whenever the production/staging Firestore project is next touched):** target collection group `pricingProposals`, field `expiresAt`. This repo has no `firebase.json`/`firestore.indexes.json` (or any other Firestore infra-as-config file) to declare the policy in, so it is a one-time manual step against the real Firestore database, via either:
   - `gcloud firestore fields ttls update expiresAt --collection-group=pricingProposals --enable-ttl --project=<FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID>` (also useful to confirm state: `gcloud firestore fields ttls describe expiresAt --collection-group=pricingProposals --project=<FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID>`), or
   - Firebase Console → Firestore Database → Indexes → TTL tab → "Create policy" → collection group `pricingProposals`, field `expiresAt`.
   Until this is enabled, expired proposal documents are already fully inert (the 20-minute application-level check in `pricingProposalStore.js`'s `resolveAppliedProposals` rejects any expired proposal regardless of TTL state — this is what actually blocks an expired proposal being used) but Firestore will NOT physically delete them, so `reports/*/pricingProposals/*` will accumulate indefinitely until this policy is turned on. Not a correctness/security risk (only a storage-cost/housekeeping one), but do not skip it before production launch.
3. Confirm the configured `OPENAI_MODEL` is accessible with that key.
4. Run one minimal sanitized U.S. pricing case end-to-end and inspect the raw response.
5. Validate the structured response passes schema validation and maps correctly into a canonical estimate proposal.
6. Test one non-U.S. location input.
7. Manually review pricing plausibility (sanity-check dollar amounts, don't just check schema validity).
8. Record real latency, token usage, and estimated cost for a sample call.
9. Verify timeout and rate-limit handling against the real API (not just mocks).
10. Confirm no sensitive fields appear in the real provider request or in server logs.
11. Remove any local test artifacts and the test secret afterward.
12. Only then update Phase 43's status to fully "Completed".

Phase 44 does not depend on any of the 11 items above and may proceed once approved — nothing in Phase 44's own scope (PlanConfig/photo-capacity enforcement) reads from or depends on live OpenAI pricing behavior.
