### 2026-09-19 — focused Phase 43 trust-boundary correction (client-echoed providerModel closed; opaque server-side proposal design)

**The bug (real, confirmed).** The original Phase 43 implementation had the frontend echo `providerModel` (`{provider, model, promptVersion}`) back in the `PUT /:id/canonical-estimate` save payload so it survived into the persisted line item for audit — `canonicalEstimate.js`'s `validateLineItems` preserved whatever the client sent in `row.providerModel` verbatim (3 length-capped strings, never money, but still fully client-controlled). A raw devtools/API caller could set `providerModel:{provider:'OpenAI', model:'gpt-99-fake', ...}` on ANY line item, with nothing server-side ever having verified it came from a real AI suggestion. An audit of the actual generation route also found `providerModel` was being returned to the client in the HTTP response body per proposal item (`pricingService.js`'s `buildProposalItem`/`generatePricingProposal`) — never rendered by the UI, but present and inspectable, and exactly the value the vulnerable save-payload echo was built from.

**Fix — opaque proposal reference design.** A generated pricing proposal is now ALSO persisted server-side as a short-lived record at `reports/{reportId}/pricingProposals/{proposalId}` (`backend/utils/pricingProposalStore.js`, new) — a sibling subcollection to Phase 41's own `canonicalEstimate`/`canonicalEstimateAudit`, deliberately NOT folded into the existing report/user-agnostic `pricingSuggestionsCache/{fingerprint}` doc (that doc's whole safety property is being genuinely non-report/user-specific, proven by its own existing test; a proposal envelope is the opposite — report/user-scoped, expiring, single-use). TTL: `PRICING_PROPOSAL_TTL_MINUTES` (default 20, documented in `.env.example`), checked at apply time (never actively swept, same convention the pricing cache already uses for expiry). The proposal record stores, server-side only: `proposalId`, `reportId`, `requestedByUid`, `createdAt`/`expiresAt`, the `baseRevision` the proposal was generated against, the locale/currency/pricingDate context, and every suggestion item keyed by an opaque `suggestionId` (renamed from the old `proposalItemId`) — pricing values, confidence/assumptions/targetLineItemId, the TRUSTED `providerModel`, and a `consumed`/`consumedLineItemId`/`consumedAt` bookkeeping triplet.

**Generation response, corrected:** `POST /:id/estimate-detail/price-suggestions` now also returns a top-level `proposalId`; each item carries its own `suggestionId`; `providerModel` is stripped from EVERY item before the HTTP response is built (`generatePricingProposal` splits the full, trusted item set it persists from the public item set it returns) — the client never receives it at all any more, closing the second, previously-unnoticed leak alongside the save-payload echo.

**Apply/save, corrected — same route, no second save endpoint.** `PUT /:id/canonical-estimate`'s existing body gained an optional `appliedProposals: [{proposalId, acceptedSuggestionIds}]` array (a documented, minimal generalization of the originally-suggested singular shape, so a user can Generate + Apply more than once before a single Save without losing provenance on the earlier batch — both entries necessarily share the same `baseRevision` since generating never advances it). `canonicalEstimateStore.upsertCanonicalEstimate`'s transaction resolves it via the new `resolveAppliedProposals` (`pricingProposalStore.js`) BEFORE calling `validateAndComputeCanonicalEstimate`, atomically with the existing revision-conflict check, and strictly AFTER the existing `REPORT_FINALIZED` guard (verified by a dedicated test that a bogus, nonexistent `proposalId` on a finalized report never even gets looked up). Resolution rejects, with specific `PRICING_PROPOSAL_*`/`PRICING_SUGGESTION_*` codes (mapped to 403/404/409/400 in `reports.js`), an unknown proposal, one belonging to a different report (structurally already unreachable — a proposal lives only under its OWN report's subcollection path, so this reads as `PRICING_PROPOSAL_NOT_FOUND` by construction, with an explicit `reportId` field-equality check kept as defense in depth), one requested by a different `uid` (`PRICING_PROPOSAL_FORBIDDEN` — **policy: only the uid that generated a proposal may apply it**, a narrower additional check layered on top of the existing owner/`canEditReports`-or-review-grantee access model, not a replacement for it; a different authorized editor simply generates their own — a cache hit makes that cheap), an expired one (`PRICING_PROPOSAL_EXPIRED`), and an accepted `suggestionId` absent from the stored proposal (`PRICING_SUGGESTION_NOT_FOUND`). A stale `baseRevision` reuses the EXISTING `REVISION_CONFLICT` code/shape (`err.currentRevision`) rather than a parallel mechanism — but see the important nuance below.

`canonicalEstimate.js`'s `validateLineItems` no longer reads `row.providerModel` under ANY circumstance — the vulnerable preserve-whatever-the-client-sent edit from the original implementation is fully reverted. A line item's `pricingSource`/`providerModel` are now resolved from exactly one of three sources, in order: (1) a fresh, atomically-reverified match in `trustedOverridesByRowId` (built by `resolveAppliedProposals` from the request's own `appliedProposals`, matched to a submitted row by a new client-supplied `appliedSuggestionId` field first, then `targetLineItemId` — the exact same two-step scheme `pricingSuggestions.js`'s `mergeProposedPricingIntoLineItems` already used, just moved to the trusted side); (2) a legitimate carry-forward — a line item that was ALREADY `ai_suggested` with real `providerModel` as of the previous persisted revision, and this save isn't touching its pricing, keeps that trusted metadata (this is NOT a security hole reopened: it reads only the server's OWN previously-persisted record, never anything in the current request); or (3) neither — downgraded to `manual`, `providerModel` reset to `{provider:null, model:null, promptVersion:null}`, regardless of what the client claims. A trusted match ALSO replaces (never trusts) the row's `materialUnitCost`/`laborUnitCost`/`equipmentUnitCost` with the proposal's own stored values (tamper protection; replace-with-trusted rather than reject, since Phase 41 recomputes every total from these components anyway). A row already carrying a user override as of the previous revision is NEVER re-trusted by a matched suggestion either, mirroring the frontend's own override-skip rule at the server boundary too (closes a raw-API bypass the original frontend-only skip couldn't).

**A real bug caught while writing the replay/idempotency tests (test 11), fixed before it shipped:** the first version of `resolveAppliedProposals` checked `baseRevision` unconditionally for every `appliedProposals` entry, every save. Since a proposal's own `baseRevision` is frozen at generation time and the frontend keeps `appliedSuggestionId`/`appliedProposalId` on a merged draft item indefinitely (re-submitting the same `appliedProposals` entry on every future save of that item, not just an immediate retry), EVERY subsequent save after applying one AI suggestion would have thrown a spurious `REVISION_CONFLICT` the moment the estimate's revision advanced past the proposal's frozen value — which happens on the very first successful apply itself. Fixed: the `baseRevision` check now only fires when at least one suggestion in that `appliedProposals` entry is being consumed for the FIRST time this call (`needsFreshRevisionCheck`); a pure re-affirmation of an already-consumed suggestion against the SAME target line item is exempt (it injects no new trust, only re-confirms an already-settled fact) and remains valid no matter how far the revision has since moved for unrelated reasons. Regression test 11b (`pricing-proposal-trust.test.js`) locks this in: an already-applied suggestion survives an unrelated later save (e.g. editing O&P%) without a false conflict.

**Replay/tamper protection, confirmed by test against the REAL transactional store (not just the pure merge function):** applying the same proposal/suggestion twice with an identical request is a safe no-op (full-array-replace semantics at the estimate layer already make a literal repeat harmless; the `consumed`/`consumedLineItemId` bookkeeping on the proposal record additionally rejects a THIRD request that tries to re-apply the same `suggestionId` against a DIFFERENT line item id as `PRICING_SUGGESTION_ALREADY_CONSUMED`, never silently duplicating it). An accepted `suggestionId` absent from `acceptedSuggestionIds` is never applied even if a submitted line item happens to reference it via `appliedSuggestionId` (downgraded to `manual`, same as any other unbacked claim).

**Frontend, corrected (`frontend/src/utils/pricingSuggestions.js`, `canonicalEstimateEditor.js`, `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`):** `mergeProposedPricingIntoLineItems` now takes the response's `proposalId` and matches by `suggestionId` (renamed from `proposalItemId`), stamping each merged draft item with opaque `appliedProposalId`/`appliedSuggestionId` handles instead of a `providerModel` object (never received from the server any more in the first place). `buildCanonicalEstimatePayload` NEVER sends `providerModel` under any circumstance — the vulnerable echo is fully removed, not merely made conditional — and derives the new `appliedProposals` array from whichever draft items currently carry both handles, grouped by `proposalId`.

**Everywhere else confirmed unaffected:** the "Pricing Source generated by: Flacron Engine" / "Status: Preliminary / Editable" labels (still driven by `pricingSourceMeta.source`, computed server-side, unchanged — and now additionally tested end-to-end to confirm an UNBACKED `ai_suggested` claim renders as "Manually entered by preparer", never a false "Flacron Engine" label); Phase 41 remains the sole calculator of every total; tax/O&P/permits/GC/manual-adjustments remain fully user-controlled; a user-overridden line item is never overwritten by an apply (now tested against the real transactional store, not just the pure merge function); a finalized report still can't be repriced or have a proposal applied to it; missing `OPENAI_API_KEY` still fails safely with 503 without blocking manual estimate editing/saving (unaffected by any of this — no proposal is ever generated or looked up on that path); `canonicalEstimateContent.js` (unchanged) still only ever reads `pricingSourceMeta.source` for the label, never `providerModel` — confirmed end-to-end with a trusted-proposal-applied item's rendered markdown never containing the underlying provider/model/prompt string.

**Tests added/updated (focused, not a full-suite duplication):** new `backend/test/pricing-proposal-trust.test.js` (16 tests covering all 15 required scenarios from the correction brief plus the regression above). Updated in place (patched, not deleted) to reflect the new trust boundary: `backend/test/pricing-service.test.js` (every `generatePricingProposal` call site now supplies `reportId`/`requestedByUid`; the old "providerModel is present in the response" assertion replaced with "the public response never carries it" + a new test proving the trusted value lands in the persisted proposal record instead), `backend/test/pricing-route.test.js` (response-shape fixture updated to `proposalId`/`suggestionId`, asserts no `providerModel` leaks through the route), `backend/test/pricing-canonical-integration.test.js` (the old "a submitted providerModel is preserved"/"pricingSourceMeta becomes ai_suggested" tests — whose own setup was the vulnerability — rewritten to legitimately back their claims via `trustedOverridesByRowId`/`previousById`, plus new tests for the unbacked-claim downgrade, the carry-forward path, the tamper-replace policy, and the override-never-retrusted rule), `frontend/src/__tests__/pricingSuggestions.test.js` (fixture/signature updated to `suggestionId`/opaque handles, asserts `providerModel` is never present), `frontend/src/__tests__/canonicalEstimateEditor.test.js` (7 new tests: `providerModel` never sent even when present on the draft, `appliedProposals` correctly grouped/derived, a manual item with a stale handle never counted).

**Automated validation (exact counts, real run):** backend full suite **814/814 passing** (0 regressions vs. this session's own 792/792 starting point; 16 new in `pricing-proposal-trust.test.js`, the rest additive/patched within existing Phase 43 files); backend lint **0 errors, 64 warnings** (identical, pre-existing baseline); frontend full suite **175/175 passing, 19/19 files** (0 regressions); frontend lint **0 errors, 14 warnings** (identical, pre-existing baseline); clean frontend production build; `git diff --check` clean; a manual secret/privacy grep-scan of every new/changed file found no key/token literal (the one test-only placeholder string is the same pre-existing, obviously-fake one); scope check confirmed the main working tree (`feature/mobile-initial-phases`) and every other worktree (including `mobile/`-touching ones) were byte-for-byte unchanged from this session's own starting snapshot; nothing committed/staged/pushed/merged/deployed/opened as a PR.

**Files new:** `backend/utils/pricingProposalStore.js`, `backend/test/pricing-proposal-trust.test.js`. **Files changed:** `backend/utils/canonicalEstimate.js` (trust-gated `providerModel`/`pricingSource`/cost-component resolution, replacing the vulnerable unconditional-preserve edit), `backend/utils/canonicalEstimateStore.js` (`resolveAppliedProposals` wired into the transaction before validation, after the `REPORT_FINALIZED` guard), `backend/services/pricingService.js` (`suggestionId` rename, persists a proposal record, strips `providerModel` from the public response, requires `reportId`/`requestedByUid`), `backend/routes/reports.js` (passes `reportId`/`requestedByUid` through; returns `proposalId`; new error-code → HTTP-status mappings for `PRICING_PROPOSAL_*`/`PRICING_SUGGESTION_*`), `backend/.env.example` (new `PRICING_PROPOSAL_TTL_MINUTES`), `frontend/src/utils/pricingSuggestions.js`, `frontend/src/utils/canonicalEstimateEditor.js`, `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx`, plus the test files listed above.

**Status stays EXACTLY** `Implementation Complete — Live Provider Validation Pending` (unchanged from before this correction) — the 11-point live-key checklist above is untouched and still genuinely pending; this correction is scoped purely to the client-side-trust bug and does not touch anything on that checklist. Phase 44 and later remain **Not Started**; the client's standing instruction not to commit/push/deploy remains in force.

### 2026-09-19 — focused Phase 43 cleanup: Firestore TTL eligibility for `pricingProposals`

**The gap.** `pricingProposalStore.js` stored `expiresAt` as a plain ISO-8601 string. Firestore's automatic-deletion (TTL) feature only ever acts on a field of the native Firestore `Timestamp` type — a string field is silently ignored by a TTL policy. The 20-minute application-level expiry check (`resolveAppliedProposals` rejecting any proposal whose `expiresAt` has passed) was and remains fully correct and sufficient for BLOCKING use of an expired proposal — that was never the gap. The gap was storage housekeeping: with a string field, expired `pricingProposals` documents would never actually be deleted by Firestore, no matter how long a TTL policy was "configured" for, because there was no Timestamp-typed field for such a policy to target.

**Fix.** `expiresAt` is now written as `Timestamp.fromDate(...)` (reusing `backend/config/firebase.js`'s existing exported `Timestamp`, the same `admin.firestore.Timestamp` the rest of the codebase already has access to — no new dependency). The public value returned from `createPricingProposal` (and from there, the generation route's HTTP response) is unchanged — still a plain ISO string — only the Firestore-persisted copy changed type. The read-side expiry check (`toEpochMs`, new small helper) now accepts either a Firestore `Timestamp` (`.toDate().getTime()`) or a plain string/Date (`new Date(value).getTime()`, kept for backward-compatible test fixtures that construct a raw expired-proposal doc directly) — so the existing 20-minute enforcement is unchanged in behavior, only made tolerant of the new stored type.

**Automatic deletion: still an explicit, undone deployment step, not configured by this change.** This repository has no `firebase.json`/`firestore.indexes.json` (or any other Firestore infra-as-config file) to declare a TTL policy in — Firestore TTL policies are a project-level database setting, not something expressible in application code or committed config. This fix makes the `expiresAt` field *eligible* for a TTL policy; it does **not** enable one. The exact one-time manual step (via `gcloud` or the Firebase Console) is now recorded as item 2 of Phase 43's live-validation/deployment checklist above. No scheduled cleanup service, cron job, or lazy delete-on-read was added — out of scope for this small correction and unnecessary, since the application-level check already fully neutralizes an expired proposal regardless of whether/when the document is physically deleted.

**Tests:** no new tests added (behavior of the 20-minute enforcement is unchanged, already covered by the existing `pricing-proposal-trust.test.js`/`pricing-service.test.js` expiry cases, which continue to pass because `toEpochMs` still accepts the plain-string fixtures those tests already use). Targeted re-run: `pricing-proposal-trust.test.js`, `pricing-service.test.js`, `pricing-route.test.js`, `pricing-canonical-integration.test.js`, `canonical-estimate-persistence.test.js` — **87/87 passing, 0 regressions**. `git diff --check` clean.

## Phase 44 — Central Plan Configuration & Atomic Photo-Capacity Enforcement

**Objective:** Introduce a single `PlanConfig` source of truth for tier photo limits (and other centrally-configurable values from Phases 40/41/45/48), and make every photo-adding route atomically enforce the effective limit — closing the confirmed, currently-unprotected `POST /:id/images` race.

**Reason this phase is required:** Change-request tier limits (Starter 25/Professional 100/Agency 250/Enterprise unlimited). Reconciled audit found `POST /:id/images` (`backend/routes/reports.js:5398-5494`) has **no capacity check at all** today (non-atomic read-append-write), while `POST /photos/stage`'s `appendStagedPhoto` (`backend/utils/photoDraftStaging.js:26-45`) already proves the correct transactional pattern.

**Scope:** New `PlanConfig` backend schema, read APIs, validation, caching, and enforcement. **This phase owns the config schema/enforcement only — the full admin editing UI belongs to Phase 48**, avoiding duplicate responsibility between the two. Not scoped to add-on purchasing itself (Phase 45).

**Reuse:** `appendStagedPhoto`'s exact `db.runTransaction(tx.get → validate → tx.set)` idiom, already tested by `photo-draft-staging.test.js`.

**Every photo-adding/staging route enumerated (all must be audited/retrofitted):**
- `POST /photos/stage` (`reports.js:469-532`) — already transactional via `appendStagedPhoto`; retarget its `maxPhotos` argument to read from `PlanConfig` instead of a hardcoded value.
- `DELETE /photos/stage/:draftId/:photoId` (`reports.js:575-597`) — capacity-releasing counterpart; confirm it decrements consistently with the same transaction pattern.
- `POST /generate` (`reports.js:600-1296`) — commits staged photos via `claimDraftPhotos`; confirm the claimed count is re-validated against `PlanConfig` at commit time (not just at staging time), since add-on capacity (Phase 45) may not have existed when staging began.
- `POST /:id/images` (`reports.js:5398-5494`) — **currently has no capacity check of any kind** (confirmed non-atomic read-append-write); retrofit with the full transactional idiom below.
- `POST /analyze-images` — does not persist to a report's `photos` array; explicitly out of scope, confirmed not to need capacity enforcement.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Create `PlanConfig` (Firestore collection, server-cached read with explicit TTL/invalidation-on-write): `reportsPerMonth`, `basePhotoLimit`, `addOnsEnabled`, `addOnPackSizes/Prices` (placeholder/test values until Phase 45's commercial confirmation), unit/action vocabulary, watermark-policy flag — **the enabled target values are Starter 25 / Professional 100 / Agency 250 / Enterprise unlimited, not a permanent fallback to the old flat 100** (the flat cap is only the pre-launch/rollback default, never the intended steady state).
2. Add server-side validation on `PlanConfig` writes (limits must be positive integers or explicit "unlimited"; add-on prices non-negative) and a safe-fallback read path (if the config document is missing/malformed, fall back to today's flat 100-photo/existing `reportsPerMonth` behavior rather than failing closed).
3. Replace `tiers.js`'s hardcoded `reportsPerMonth` and the flat `MAX_PHOTOS = 100` constant with `PlanConfig` reads (`tiers.js`'s other fields stay untouched).
4. Retrofit `POST /:id/images` with the same transactional read-check-write pattern as `appendStagedPhoto`, validating against `base + purchased add-ons` (Enterprise = unlimited, skip check); include a client-supplied idempotent upload/request ID so a network retry of the same request never double-counts.
5. Define orphaned-upload reconciliation: if a transaction rejects a photo for exceeding capacity after the file bytes were already written to Storage, the orphaned Storage object is deleted (or swept by a periodic cleanup job) — capacity accounting and Storage state must not drift apart.
6. Confirm/retrofit `POST /generate`'s photo intake per the route-enumeration note above.
7. Frontend: live counter ("Photos uploaded: X / Y"), near-limit warning, blocked state with "Add Photo Pack"/"Upgrade Plan" actions (pack action wired once Phase 45 exists); Enterprise shows "Unlimited."
8. Expose `PlanConfig` via a **read-only backend API** for other phases/the frontend to consume (sanitized — no internal-only fields) — building the API is this phase's job; the **admin UI to edit it is explicitly Phase 48's**, not built here.
9. Ensure each photo record retains: room/area tag, uploader (`userId`), processing state, verification status, and any related estimate-line-item references (Phase 41/42) — extending the existing photo record shape, not replacing it.

**Data model / API implications:** New `PlanConfig` collection + read API; existing report `photos` array shape unchanged in structure, extended with the fields in task 9, gated differently on write.

**Security / privacy:** Effective limit always computed server-side from `PlanConfig` + verified purchase records (Phase 45) — never trusts a client-supplied limit or count. `PlanConfig`'s read API returns only sanitized, non-secret fields; no direct unrestricted Firestore client access to the collection.

**Files/components likely affected:** New `backend/config/planConfig.js`, `backend/routes/reports.js` (`/photos/stage`, `/generate`, `:id/images`, `MAX_PHOTOS` removal), `backend/utils/photoDraftStaging.js` (retarget `maxPhotos` source), `backend/config/tiers.js` (photo fields delegated), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None — parallelizable with Phase 40/41/46. Must land before Phase 45 and before Phase 48 (Phase 48 builds the admin UI against this phase's read/write API).

**Automated validation:** A concurrency test for `POST /:id/images` mirroring `photo-draft-staging.test.js`'s fake-Firestore contention simulation; a retry-idempotency test (same upload ID submitted twice counts once); an orphaned-Storage-cleanup test; a regression test confirming the safe-fallback path reproduces today's flat 100-cap behavior when `PlanConfig` is absent; **a separate acceptance test confirming the real configured values (25/100/250/unlimited) are enforced once `PlanConfig` is populated — the fallback test alone does not satisfy acceptance.**

**Manual/runtime validation:** Attempt to exceed the limit via two concurrent requests against a real dev server; confirm Enterprise is genuinely unbounded; confirm existing photos are never deleted on a simulated downgrade; confirm a retried upload request doesn't double-count.

**Backward compatibility / regression:** No existing photo is ever deleted by this phase; pre-existing reports continue to display/export correctly.

**Out of scope:** Stripe purchasing itself (Phase 45); the admin editing UI (Phase 48); pricing-page copy (Phase 48).

**Rollback/recovery:** `PlanConfig` can be reset to reproduce today's flat 100-cap as an emergency fallback, but this is a rollback state only — normal acceptance testing must exercise the real 25/100/250/unlimited values, not just the fallback. The transactional retrofit is additive validation, revertible per-route.

**Status:** Complete

### 2026-09-19 — Phase 44 implementation

Implemented in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`. No manual/runtime validation against a real dev server or real Firebase project was performed this session (disclosed, not fabricated) — every result below is real, unmocked-logic automated test coverage (pure functions + a real transactional double + a real Express app exercising the real `reports.js` router with only the Firebase/Storage/photo-processing/AI-pipeline boundaries faked, matching this repo's own `pricing-route.test.js` convention).

**PlanConfig (`backend/config/planConfig.js`, new):** single Firestore doc `planConfig/active`, schema `{schemaVersion, plans:{starter|professional|agency|enterprise:{reportsPerMonth, basePhotoLimit}}, addOnsEnabled, addOnPackSizes, addOnPackPrices, allowedMimeTypes, maxFileSizeBytes, watermarkPolicyEnabled, status, createdAt, updatedAt, updatedBy}`. Limits are a non-negative integer or the explicit string `'unlimited'`, never `-1`. `validatePlanConfig()` fails the WHOLE document on any single defect (unrecognized/missing plan id, negative/fractional/string-typed limit, empty MIME list, non-positive file size, negative add-on price, wrong `schemaVersion`). `getPlanConfig(db)` is an in-process TTL cache (default 60s, `PLAN_CONFIG_CACHE_TTL_MS`, explicit `Number.isFinite`-checked so `=0` genuinely disables caching rather than silently defaulting) with `invalidatePlanConfigCache()` called by the validated write path `setPlanConfig()`. **Safe-fallback (never fails closed into unlimited):** a missing/malformed document or a thrown read all resolve to `FALLBACK_CONFIG` — reportsPerMonth 5/50/200/unlimited (exactly `tiers.js`'s pre-existing values) and a flat 100-photo cap for every tier, reproducing today's behavior byte-for-byte. `RECOMMENDED_DEFAULT_CONFIG` holds the real target values (Starter 25/Professional 100/Agency 250/Enterprise unlimited) ready to seed — this repo has no Firestore infra-as-config file, so seeding the real document remains a manual deployment step, not performed this session per the standing no-deploy instruction.

**`tiers.js` retrofit (task 3), narrow by design:** `getTier()`/`canGenerate()` fully unchanged (zero regression risk for `middleware/auth.js`/`payment.js`/`users.js`/the pre-existing `tiers.test.js`). New async siblings `getEffectiveTier(db, tierName)`/`canGenerateAsync(db, userTier, reportsThisMonth)` read PlanConfig live; only the 5 real monthly-quota enforcement call sites in `reports.js` (repair-estimate/invoice/coverage-letter/mold-supplement/main-generate) were switched to them. `payment.js`/`users.js`'s display-only tier reads were deliberately left on the static value — full cross-surface consistency is Phase 48's own scope, not this phase's.

**Effective-capacity resolver:** `resolveEffectivePhotoCapacity({basePhotoLimit, verifiedAddOnCapacity=0})` = `basePlanCapacity + verifiedReportAddOnCapacity`; unlimited short-circuits regardless of any add-on; add-on defaults to 0 and is NEVER read from a client value anywhere in this phase (Phase 45 doesn't exist yet). `resolvePlanContext(db, tierName, {verifiedAddOnCapacity})` is the one-call convenience every route uses.

**Route enforcement (every photo-adding/staging route audited):** `POST /photos/stage` retargeted from the flat `MAX_PHOTOS=100` to live PlanConfig capacity, both at its pre-check and inside `appendStagedPhoto`'s transaction (which now counts only `status==='uploaded'`, so a failed/duplicate record never falsely blocks on a full cap). `DELETE /photos/stage/:draftId/:photoId` is now transactional (`removeStagedPhoto`, new) for the same contention reason `appendStagedPhoto` is — capacity needs no explicit release step since it's always derived fresh from the live array length. `POST /generate` re-validates the combined staged+inline photo count against live capacity AT COMMIT TIME via the new pure `partitionRecordsByCapacity()`, demoting overflow to `status:'failed', capacityRejected:true` and cleaning up their already-written Storage objects. **`POST /:id/images` — the confirmed zero-enforcement gap — is fully retrofitted** with a new `appendReportPhotosAtomic()` (`backend/utils/photoCapacity.js`) reusing `appendStagedPhoto`'s exact `tx.get→validate→tx.set` idiom: re-checks content hashes against the FRESH transactional state (closing a race a pre-upload-only check can't), partitions against live capacity, and persists a per-report `photoUploadAttempts` idempotency ledger keyed by a client-supplied (or, for a legacy client, server-generated-per-request and explicitly non-replay-safe, documented as such) `attemptId` + a content-hash fingerprint — a same-key-same-content retry replays with zero double-count, a same-key-different-content retry is a hard `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_CONFLICT`. `POST /analyze-images` confirmed (not just assumed) to need no capacity check — it never persists to a report's `photos` array. `/api`/`/api/v1` alias duplication is a non-issue by construction (`server.js`'s `API_PREFIXES` mounts one router object at both prefixes).

**Transactional design, honestly scoped:** no separate two-phase "reserve→upload→finalize" pipeline was built — Storage upload happens first (outside any transaction, per the reused idiom's own established reasoning: a transaction retry must never re-run an upload), then ONE atomic transaction does fresh-state dedup + capacity partition + commit together. This satisfies every atomicity/idempotency/no-overshoot requirement (proven by 2 real concurrency-barrier tests: capacity-1-with-2-simultaneous-requests exactly-one-wins, and capacity-3-with-6-simultaneous-requests exactly-3-win-no-duplicates, both against the shared `test/helpers/fakeFirestore.js`) without inventing machinery beyond what this codebase already trusts.

**Orphan Storage cleanup:** exact-object only (`deleteObjects([originalPath, objectPath, thumbnailPath])`), never prefix/wildcard; best-effort with safe logging (report id + error message only, never a signed URL/credential); triggered on every capacity-reject/duplicate-reject/REPORT_FINALIZED/IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_CONFLICT path where bytes were already written. No new scheduled-cleanup system was built (none pre-existed to hook into, confirmed by checking, and the spec explicitly scopes that out) — a bounded reconciliation boundary is documented instead (an orphaned object is a pure storage-cost issue, never a capacity-accounting one, since the rejected record was already excluded from `photos` before cleanup was even attempted).

**Public API:** `GET /reports/photo-capacity` (optional `?draftId=`) and `GET /reports/:id/photo-capacity`, both returning `{plan, basePhotoLimit, addOnCapacity, effectiveCapacity, unlimited, used, remaining, uploadAllowed}` — unlimited fields are explicitly `null`, never a magic number. Proven (by dedicated tests) to never expose Stripe price ids/secrets/env values/`addOnPackPrices`/`status`/`updatedBy`, and to ignore any client-supplied capacity-shaped query params.

**Frontend (Dashboard.jsx only, confirmed via grep to be the sole surface with the hardcoded "100" wizard UI — marketing-copy "100 photos" mentions elsewhere are Phase 48's job):** new pure `frontend/src/utils/photoCapacityDisplay.js` derives the live counter/near-limit-warning/blocked-state/unlimited-display from the server's sanitized response, falling back to the flat-100 display while loading/on failure. `Dashboard.jsx`'s old module-level `MAX_PHOTOS=100` constant is removed; a new `photoCapacity` state refreshes after every upload success/failure/duplicate/delete. No purchase button (Phase 45) or admin UI (Phase 48) added.

**Usage-counting rule (one definition, applied everywhere):** `status==='uploaded'` consumes; `'failed'`/`'duplicate'` never did; `review.status==='excluded'` still consumes (a separate lifecycle from upload `status`); a thumbnail/original/display path is a field on one record, never a second record; capacity is always derived fresh from the live array, so delete = automatic release.

**Backward compatibility:** no existing photo is ever deleted by anything in this phase; a report already over a newly-lowered limit keeps every existing photo and only blocks further uploads; legacy `imagePaths`/`photos` sync is unchanged in shape.

**Automated validation (exact counts, real run):** Phase 44 tests alone: 21 (`photo-capacity.test.js`) + 31 (`plan-config.test.js`) + 11 (`photo-capacity-route.test.js`) + 5 (`tiers.test.js` additions) + 8 (`photo-draft-staging.test.js` additions) = **76 new tests**. Full backend suite **890/890 passing** (0 regressions vs. this session's 814/814 starting point — the delta is exactly the 76 new tests). Backend lint **0 errors, 65 warnings** (baseline 64; +1 net from two new `catch(err)`-without-using-`err` blocks following this file's own pre-existing, already-downgraded-to-warning pattern — a `_err` rename was tried and confirmed ineffective since this ESLint config has no `caughtErrorsIgnorePattern`, so the existing convention was kept). Frontend full suite **183/183 passing, 20/20 files** (0 regressions; +8 new in `photoCapacityDisplay.test.js`). Frontend lint **0 errors, 14 warnings** (unchanged baseline). Frontend production build succeeds. `git diff --check` clean. Manual secret/credential/signed-URL grep scan of every new/changed file: clean.

### 2026-09-19 — focused Phase 44 PlanConfig activation/fallback correction

**The bug (real, confirmed).** `FALLBACK_CONFIG` (returned automatically whenever `planConfig/active` is missing, malformed, unreadable, or on an unsupported schema version) reproduced the OLD flat-100-for-every-tier behavior, not the accepted 25/100/250/unlimited mapping. Since the real `planConfig/active` document was never seeded (an honest limitation already disclosed above), a normal deployment would silently run on flat-100 indefinitely — expanding Starter from its intended 25-photo entitlement to 100 by default, the opposite of the accepted requirement.

**Fix.** `backend/config/planConfig.js`: `FALLBACK_CONFIG` now IS the accepted mapping (`{...RECOMMENDED_DEFAULT_CONFIG, status: 'built_in_default'}`) — missing/malformed/unreadable/unsupported-version config all resolve to Starter 25 / Professional 100 / Agency 250 / Enterprise unlimited, never the old flat-100. The old flat-100 profile still exists, renamed `LEGACY_ROLLBACK_CONFIG` (`status: 'legacy_rollback'`), but is reachable ONLY via the new explicit `applyLegacyRollbackConfig(db, opts)` export (or by an operator writing it to Firestore directly) — never automatically. `reportsPerMonth` untouched (still 5/50/200/unlimited in every profile) per the correction's own scope boundary. A valid, authoritative Firestore doc still always overrides both built-in constants (unchanged). Both sanitized capacity endpoints (`GET /reports/photos/capacity`, `GET /reports/:id/photo-capacity`) now additionally return `configSource: 'firestore' | 'fallback'` (already-safe, non-sensitive values — no raw validation-error strings or internal status fields exposed).

**Seed mechanism (task 6).** New `backend/scripts/seedPlanConfig.js` — idempotent (reruns only bump `updatedAt`/`updatedBy`, never mutate the plan values), validates via `validatePlanConfig`/`setPlanConfig` before any write, no embedded credentials (reuses `../config/firebase`'s existing Admin init). Default action seeds `RECOMMENDED_DEFAULT_CONFIG` (the accepted mapping); `--dry-run` validates and prints without writing; `--legacy-rollback` is the only way this script writes `LEGACY_ROLLBACK_CONFIG`, keeping the two profiles explicitly separate. **Not run this session** — production's `planConfig/active` remains unseeded; until someone runs it, production now correctly runs on the built-in accepted-mapping fallback (25/100/250/unlimited) instead of the old flat-100 default.

**Tests.** `backend/test/plan-config.test.js`: 6 new/rewritten tests — missing/malformed/read-failure/unsupported-schema-version all assert Starter=25 (not 100); an explicit "invalid config can never expand Starter to 100" case; an explicit "invalid config can never grant unlimited via a malformed -1" case; `applyLegacyRollbackConfig` proven to be the only path to the legacy profile; `LEGACY_ROLLBACK_CONFIG` itself still validates; seed-payload validity/idempotency. `backend/test/photo-capacity-route.test.js`: the no-doc fallback route test updated to assert `basePhotoLimit: 25`/`configSource: 'fallback'` instead of the old flat-100 expectation. All pre-existing Phase 44 atomic/concurrency tests re-run unmodified and still pass.

**Validation (exact counts, real run).** Targeted Phase 44 tests: 100/100 passing. Full backend suite: **896/896 passing** (0 regressions vs. this phase's own 890/890 baseline; +6 new). Backend lint: **0 errors, 65 warnings** (unchanged). `git diff --check`: clean (one harmless LF→CRLF line-ending notice, not an error). Manual secret/credential grep scan of every changed file: clean. Frontend untouched by this correction (no route/response shape change reaches existing frontend contracts beyond an additive `configSource` field) — full frontend suite not re-run, since no frontend file changed.

**Honest limitation, unchanged:** `planConfig/active` is still not seeded in any real project — this correction fixes what happens automatically in that document's absence, it does not seed it (seeding remains a deliberate, unexecuted deployment step per the standing no-deploy instruction).

Phase 44 remains marked **Complete** (now inclusive of this correction); Phase 43's status/checklist and Phase 45+ `Not Started` are unchanged.

## Phase 45 — Stripe Report-Specific Photo Add-Ons

**Objective:** One-time, report-scoped Stripe purchases that add photo capacity on top of Phase 44's plan-based limits, fulfilled only via verified webhook.

**Reason this phase is required:** Change-request add-on requirement. Reconciled audit confirms only subscription-mode Stripe exists today (`backend/routes/payment.js`) — one-time `mode: 'payment'` Checkout is new work, though the webhook-verification/idempotency pattern (`processedWebhooks`) is directly reusable.

**Scope:** Checkout session creation, webhook fulfillment, capacity reconciliation. **Stripe Product/Price activation and pricing-page copy wait for the client's final pack-size/price confirmation (Open Question #17)** — build against `PlanConfig`-held test-mode values until then.

**Reuse:** `payment.js`'s webhook signature verification + `processedWebhooks` idempotency pattern; Phase 44's `PlanConfig`.

**Trust boundary (explicit):** No frontend value and no webhook `metadata` field (including `packSize`) is ever treated as authoritative for what capacity to grant or what was paid. The client only ever selects a server-known `packId` (a `PlanConfig`-defined identifier); the backend independently resolves that ID to its approved Stripe Price ID, currency, amount, and granted photo capacity from `PlanConfig` at checkout-creation time, and re-resolves/re-validates all of it again at webhook time against a stored checkout-intent record — a webhook's own metadata is corroborating, not authoritative.

**Exact tasks:**
1. New `POST /api/payment/photo-pack-checkout` accepting a `packId` (not a raw size/price) — backend looks up the approved Stripe Price ID/currency/amount/capacity from `PlanConfig`, validates report ownership server-side, creates a `mode: 'payment'` Checkout Session with `metadata: {uid, reportId, packId}`, and persists a `checkoutIntent` record (reportId, uid, packId, expected priceId/amount/currency/capacity, status: pending) keyed by the session ID.
2. New webhook case for `checkout.session.completed`: reuse the existing `processedWebhooks` idempotency check, then re-resolve the session's `packId` against `PlanConfig` and compare the resulting priceId/amount/currency **and report ownership** against the stored `checkoutIntent` — any mismatch is rejected/flagged, not fulfilled. On a validated match, atomically (single transaction) mark the `checkoutIntent` fulfilled, record the purchase, and increment the report's purchased-capacity (Phase 44's transactional idiom) — fulfillment and processed-event recording happen together, not as separate non-atomic steps.
3. Subscribe to and handle the full one-time-payment event set: `checkout.session.completed` (fulfill), `checkout.session.expired` (mark the `checkoutIntent` expired, grant nothing), `charge.refunded`/`payment_intent.canceled` (revoke only unused capacity, never delete photos), `charge.dispute.created`/chargeback events (flag the purchase and revoked capacity for audit, never delete photos).
4. Record purchases in a new `PhotoAddOnPurchase` collection (`reportId`, `packId`, `priceId`, `amount`, `currency`, `status`, `stripeSessionId`, `checkoutIntentId`, timestamps) — full audit trail of every state transition.
5. Safe-default edge policy: packs never transferable between reports; refund/dispute revokes only unused capacity, never deletes photos; an expired Checkout Session may still fire a webhook but grants no capacity (task 3).
6. Keep test-mode and live-mode Stripe Price IDs in clearly separate `PlanConfig` fields (e.g. `stripePriceId.test`/`stripePriceId.live`), selected by environment — never mixed in one config value.
7. Frontend "Add Photo Pack" flow from Phase 44's blocked-upload state, pack options (id + display size/price) sourced from `PlanConfig`'s sanitized read API.
8. Confirm the existing subscription-mode checkout (`create-checkout-session`) and its webhook cases are completely unmodified by this phase — new logic is additive, in new code paths only.

**Data model / API implications:** New `PhotoAddOnPurchase` and `checkoutIntent` collections; Phase 44's effective-limit calculation reads `base + sum(fulfilled purchases for this report)`.

**Security / privacy:** Capacity granted only after Stripe webhook signature verification **and** re-validation against the stored `checkoutIntent`, never from webhook metadata or any client-side callback alone; ownership re-validated server-side at both checkout creation and webhook fulfillment.

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/routes/payment.js`, new `backend/test/photo-addon-payment.test.js`, `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`, `frontend/src/services/api.js`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 44. Stripe Product/Price IDs and go-live wait on Open Question #17 and renewed Stripe access.

**Automated validation:** Webhook idempotency test (duplicate event ID processed once); ownership-mismatch rejection test; mocked checkout/fulfillment test.

**Manual/runtime validation (blocked until Stripe access + pricing confirmed):** Real Stripe test-mode purchase → webhook → capacity increase; duplicate-webhook replay confirmed idempotent.

**Backward compatibility / regression:** No effect on subscription billing (`create-checkout-session`/existing webhook cases untouched).

**Out of scope:** Subscription-mode billing changes; final production pricing copy (Phase 48, post-confirmation).

**Rollback/recovery:** Purchases are additive records; disabling the feature just stops offering the checkout flow.

**Status:** `Complete` (live Stripe test-mode validation finished 2026-09-22 — see the dated entry below)

### 2026-09-19 — Phase 45 implementation

Implemented in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`. Stripe account access has not been accepted — no live/test Products, Prices, or network calls to Stripe were made; every test mocks the `stripe` SDK.

**Confirmed catalogue** (`backend/config/photoAddOnPacks.js`, new): `photos_25`=+25=$4.99, `photos_50`=+50=$7.99, `photos_100`=+100=$12.99, `photos_250`=+250=$24.99, all USD, one-time. Every Stripe Price ID slot (`STRIPE_PRICE_PHOTOS_{25,50,100,250}_{TEST,LIVE}`) is read from an env var and is genuinely empty right now — no fabricated `price_xxx` default. `resolveStripeMode()` derives test/live from whether `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` starts with `sk_live_` (tamper-proof, defaults to test). `getPublicCatalogue()` strips Price IDs entirely for the client-facing response.

**Checkout intent = purchase record, one collection** (`backend/utils/photoAddOnPurchases.js`, new): a single `photoAddOnPurchases` document per attempt, state machine `pending → session_created → fulfilled|expired|failed`, then `fulfilled → disputed → fulfilled(won)|dispute_lost` or `fulfilled → refunded`. `createCheckoutIntent` is one transaction that authorizes report ownership, rejects `archived`(404)/`finalized`(409), and persists the trusted intent (server-resolved capacity/amount/currency only). A bounded `report.photoAddOnPurchaseIds` array (no Firestore query needed) backs the purchase-history read.

**Checkout route** `POST /payment/photo-pack-checkout` (new, on the existing `routes/payment.js`, already mounted at both `/api` and `/api/v1`): client sends only `{reportId, packId}`. Order: unlimited-tier short-circuit → `PlanConfig.addOnsEnabled` check (`503 ADDONS_DISABLED`, left `false` by default — feature ships dark) → pack resolution (`400 INVALID_PACK`) → Price ID resolution (`503 STRIPE_ADDONS_NOT_CONFIGURED` if unset, which it always is today) → `createCheckoutIntent` → `stripe.checkout.sessions.create({mode:'payment', ...}, {idempotencyKey})`. Success/cancel URLs are server-built from `FRONTEND_URL` only. A Stripe API failure marks the intent `failed` (`502 CHECKOUT_SESSION_FAILED`) and a fresh retry is safe.

**Webhook** extends the EXISTING `POST /payment/webhook` (same raw-body middleware, same signing secret, same `processedWebhooks` idempotency gate, subscription path untouched and regression-tested). `checkout.session.completed` branches on `session.metadata.checkoutIntentId` (subscription sessions never set it) into `fulfillCheckoutIntent`, which re-validates the Stripe session's mode/livemode/currency/amount/payment_status/session-id/metadata against the STORED intent inside one transaction before granting anything. New events, real SDK names only (stripe v14.25.0): `checkout.session.expired`, `charge.refunded`, `charge.dispute.created`, `charge.dispute.closed` (outcome read from `dispute.status`, since there is no `charge.dispute.won`/`.lost` event — the one adjustment PHASES.md asked to be called out precisely). Refund/dispute events resolve `checkoutIntentId` via `stripe.paymentIntents.retrieve(...).metadata`, never from the charge/dispute object alone.

**Atomicity/idempotency**: proven by a 6-way concurrent-fulfillment barrier test (exactly one grants). Out-of-order dispute delivery converges correctly via `activeDisputeId`/`disputeOutcome` bookkeeping on the intent (tested both directions).

**Reversal policy**: existing photos are never deleted. Full refund revokes capacity once, idempotently. Partial refund is flagged (`needsManualReview`) WITHOUT changing capacity (no fractional credits). Dispute created suspends; dispute won restores exactly once; dispute lost keeps the revocation permanently. Expired checkout grants nothing.

**Phase 44 integration**: `report.purchasedPhotoCapacity` (a transaction-updated snapshot, idempotent via `photoAddOnCapacityGrantedFor`) feeds into Phase 44's existing `resolvePlanContext({verifiedAddOnCapacity})` at `GET /:id/photo-capacity` (now also returns sanitized `purchases[]`) and `POST /:id/images` (the actual atomic-upload-enforcement route) — proven end-to-end with a Starter report accepting more uploads once a +25 pack is `fulfilled`. `/photos/stage`/`/generate` untouched (pre-report-creation, no report to own a purchase against).

**Frontend**: `ReportPreviewPage.jsx` gained an owner-only "Add Photo Pack" button/modal (`frontend/src/utils/photoAddOnDisplay.js`, new pure module) covering every required state (loading/not-configured/ineligible/unlimited/available/creating/redirecting/pending/fulfilled/expired/failed/refunded/disputed/network-retry). Post-redirect, the query string is never trusted as proof of payment — a new `POST /payment/photo-pack-checkout/:intentId/sync` endpoint re-verifies with Stripe server-side through the same `fulfillCheckoutIntent` path before the UI shows anything as paid; the webhook remains authoritative.

**Automated validation (exact counts, real run):** 5 new backend test files, 60 new tests (catalogue 10, store/concurrency/dispute-ordering 22, checkout route 10, webhook 12, capacity integration 6). Full backend suite **956/956 passing** (0 regressions vs. 896/896). Backend lint **0 errors, 63 warnings** (unchanged verified baseline). Frontend: 1 new file, 21 tests; full suite **204/204 passing, 21/21 files** (0 regressions), lint 0 errors/14 warnings (unchanged), clean production build. `git diff --check` clean. Secret/Price-ID scan clean. `mobile/` and the main working tree (53 entries) confirmed unchanged; nothing committed/staged/pushed/merged/deployed/opened as a PR.

**Live Stripe validation checklist (genuinely pending, not done):** 1) accept Stripe account access; 2) verify account/test mode; 3) create the 4 confirmed test Products/Prices; 4) store test Price IDs; 5) add the 4 new event types to the existing webhook endpoint's subscription and verify signature handling; 6) flip `PlanConfig.addOnsEnabled` to `true`; 7) one real test checkout, confirm capacity activates only after the webhook; 8) redeliver the same webhook, confirm no duplicate grant; 9) test an expired/abandoned checkout; 10) issue a real test refund, confirm safe reversal; 11) exercise the dispute lifecycle as far as test tooling allows; 12) confirm subscription webhooks unaffected; 13) inspect logs/responses for leakage; 14) configure live Price IDs/switch to a live key only when authorized; 15) remove temporary local credentials/artifacts; 16) only then mark Phase 45 fully **Complete**.

**Files changed:** `backend/config/photoAddOnPacks.js` (new), `backend/utils/photoAddOnPurchases.js` (new), `backend/routes/payment.js` (new routes + webhook cases), `backend/middleware/rateLimiters.js` (`photoAddOnCheckoutLimiter`), `backend/routes/reports.js` (capacity resolver + `/:id/photo-capacity` extended), `backend/.env.example` (new Stripe/rate-limit vars), `backend/test/photo-addon-{catalogue,purchases-store,checkout-route,webhook,capacity-integration}.test.js` (new), `frontend/src/utils/photoAddOnDisplay.js` (new), `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx` (modal + wiring), `frontend/src/services/api.js` (`paymentAPI` additions), `frontend/src/__tests__/photoAddOnDisplay.test.js` (new).

### 2026-09-22 — Phase 45 live Stripe test-mode validation (Status → Complete)

Performed entirely in this same isolated worktree, strictly against Stripe **test mode** (CLI authenticated only to the Flacron Enterprises LLC sandbox) and a local **Firebase Emulator Suite** (demo-only project, `demo-flacronai-test` — no real Firebase project ever touched). A real Express backend and Vite frontend were run locally against the emulators; a temporary, env-gated (`VITE_USE_FIREBASE_EMULATOR=true`, no-op otherwise) emulator-connect block was added to `frontend/src/config/firebase.js` for the session and fully reverted to its exact pre-session state afterward (confirmed via `git status` — zero diff). All temporary emulator config/scripts/logs/scratch data were deleted at cleanup; `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`/`STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` were removed from the local `.env` (never committed, never printed); the four `STRIPE_PRICE_PHOTOS_*_TEST` Price IDs were kept as legitimate local test configuration.

**A real, live-environment-only bug was found and fixed:** `applyRefund`, `applyDisputeCreated`, and both branches of `applyDisputeClosed` (`backend/utils/photoAddOnPurchases.js`) wrote the purchase-intent document before reading the report document inside the same Firestore transaction — violating real Firestore's "all reads before any writes" transaction rule (confirmed via the actual `@google-cloud/firestore` SDK error against the emulator; the shared `FakeFirestore` test double never enforced this ordering, so all 72 pre-existing mocked tests passed while the real transaction threw a 500 on every refund/dispute webhook). Fixed by reordering each read before its corresponding write (zero logic change — the exact same pattern `fulfillCheckoutIntent` already used correctly). Regression-proofed with a new opt-in `strictTransactionOrder` flag on `FakeFirestore` (default `false` — zero effect on any other phase's tests, keeping the change scoped to Phase 45) plus 4 new targeted tests exercising exactly this ordering for all four affected code paths.

**Live validation results, real HTTP requests through the real routes, real Stripe test-mode objects, real webhook deliveries via `stripe listen`:**
- Full purchase → webhook → capacity increase: a Starter-tier disposable test report went from `basePhotoLimit 25` to `effectiveCapacity 50` after a real `photos_25` ($4.99) test-mode Checkout completed in a real browser and the webhook fired — confirmed capacity activated only after the webhook, not the redirect.
- Idempotent redelivery of `checkout.session.completed`: reprocessed via the existing `processedWebhooks` gate in 14ms, no double-grant.
- Refund (full): failed with a 500 pre-fix (the bug above); post-fix, a real test-mode refund correctly reversed capacity (`effectiveCapacity` back to 25), purchase status → `refunded`, and was idempotent on redelivery.
- `checkout.session.expired`: verified for both `photos_50` ($7.99) and `photos_100` ($12.99) via a real create-then-immediately-`stripe checkout sessions expire`-call (no payment) — capacity unaffected, purchase → `expired`, idempotent on redelivery. This also verified each pack's Price ID resolves to the correct amount without an unnecessary paid checkout.
- Cancellation: confirmed by design, not by test artifact — Stripe fires no webhook when a user abandons a Checkout Session (only the real `expired` event exists for one-time Checkout), matching this implementation exactly.
- `charge.dispute.created`/`charge.dispute.closed`: a real `photos_250` ($24.99) purchase was made with Stripe's official always-dispute test card (`4000000000000259`); `charge.dispute.created` suspended capacity within ~1 second of fulfillment (`effectiveCapacity` back to 25, `disputeStatus: "open"`), and `stripe disputes close` (the only outcome triggerable on demand via Stripe's test API — there is no API to force a "won" outcome) produced a real `charge.dispute.closed` event resolving to `dispute_lost`, keeping the revocation. Both events were confirmed idempotent on redelivery. The **"won" outcome could not be triggered live** (a disclosed Stripe test-tooling limitation, not a gap) — it remains covered by the pre-existing mocked unit tests and the new `strictTransactionOrder` regression test for that exact branch.
- All four confirmed packs/prices verified end-to-end against real Stripe test-mode objects: `photos_25`=$4.99, `photos_50`=$7.99, `photos_100`=$12.99, `photos_250`=$24.99 — exactly two real paid checkouts were used total (the original purchase, and the one needed for a genuine dispute test), the other two packs verified via unpaid create-then-expire sessions.

**Final automated validation (post-cleanup, real run):** Phase 45 suite **76/76 passing** (72 original + 4 new regression tests). Full backend suite **1162/1163 passing** — the sole failure (`googleAddress: not_configured when neither browser nor server key flag is set`) is a pre-existing, unrelated Phase 46 env-state test-isolation artifact (caused by `GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_KEY` already being set in this worktree's `.env` before this session began), not a Phase 45 regression. `git diff --check` clean (only the same harmless LF→CRLF notice every prior phase in this worktree has shown). Worktree HEAD unchanged at `6a6df97`; nothing staged/committed/pushed/merged/deployed/opened as a PR; the main working tree (`feature/mobile-initial-phases`, 53 uncommitted entries) and every other worktree confirmed untouched.

**Phase 45 status: Complete.** Both remaining open items from the original 16-point live-validation checklist — the literal browser click-through (now done, user-confirmed) and going live with real Price IDs/a live key (still explicitly deferred, no live Stripe objects or keys were ever created or used) — are resolved or intentionally out of scope for this test-mode validation pass.

## Phase 46 — Property Intelligence: Address Normalization & Google Integration

**Objective:** Worldwide address autocomplete + server-side geocoding/normalization behind a provider-independent property schema, with per-field source/verification-status/override tracking.

**Reason this phase is required:** Change-request requirement. Reconciled audit confirms **zero existing property-lookup integration** anywhere in the repo (only a `TASKS.md` backlog bullet) — fully greenfield, and the normalized schema must exist before RealtyAPI-specific mapping (Phase 47).

**Scope:** Google Places/Geocoding integration + the provider-independent normalized property schema. No RealtyAPI-specific fields yet.

**Reuse:** None directly reusable (confirmed greenfield); follow Phase 41's validation/versioning conventions for consistency.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Frontend: Google Places Autocomplete widget using a browser-restricted key (HTTP-referrer + Places-API restricted) — the standard supported flow, not backend-proxied (lower latency, cheaper session-token billing).
2. Backend: separate, server-restricted key for Geocoding confirmation/standardization of whatever autocomplete returns, before it's trusted.
3. Define the normalized `PropertyProfile` schema: standardized address, lat/long, country, state/province, city, postal code, plus a placeholder for provider-specific fields (Phase 47 fills these for US addresses).
4. Per-field `{value, source, verificationStatus, userOverride}`; conflict surfacing when sources disagree; never silently overwrite a user-confirmed value.
5. Country branching: non-US addresses keep Google location data only, remaining fields stay manually editable — must never block report creation.
6. Expose a sanitized public config endpoint (e.g. `GET /api/config/property-lookup`) returning only non-secret data the frontend needs (the restricted browser key's presence/enabled flag, supported countries) — never the server-only geocoding/RealtyAPI keys.

**User confirmation workflow:** After lookup, the wizard shows a review/edit/confirm step before any value is treated as confirmed report data — matching Phase 41/Section 7's "AI-suggested vs. user-confirmed" pattern. A manually entered or already user-confirmed value is never silently overwritten by a later lookup (last-confirmed-wins; a new lookup result surfaces as a conflict, not an auto-replace). Ambiguous autocomplete selection (multiple close matches) requires an explicit user pick, never an auto-best-guess. Provider timeout/failure degrades to manual entry, logged but not blocking. An explicit "Re-check address" action re-queries and re-surfaces conflicts rather than auto-applying.

**Data model / API implications:** New `PropertyProfile` collection/embedded object linked to the primary report; new endpoint(s) for address validation/normalization; new sanitized public-config endpoint (task 6).

**Security / privacy:** Browser key is intentionally restricted-but-visible (not a secret); server key backend-only; retention policy documented (property/tax data is quasi-PII).

**Files/components likely affected:** New `backend/config/googlePlaces.js`, new `backend/services/propertyService.js`, the property step of the wizard (`Dashboard.jsx`), `backend/test/property-normalization.test.js` (mocked provider).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None — parallelizable with Phase 40/41/44. Must land before Phase 47.

**Automated validation:** Mocked Google response tests (valid, ambiguous, no-result, malformed); conflict-detection test; never-blocks-creation test (provider failure still allows report generation).

**Manual/runtime validation (blocked until Google keys arrive):** Real autocomplete → geocode → normalize flow for real addresses across countries.

**Backward compatibility / regression:** Existing free-text property entry (`propertyAddress`/`propertyStreet` etc.) remains the fallback path when lookup is unavailable or skipped.

**Out of scope:** RealtyAPI-specific mapping (Phase 47); wiring property data into estimate pricing region (a Phase 43 follow-on, not required here).

**Rollback/recovery:** Additive; disabling the widget reverts to today's free-text entry with zero data impact.

**Status:** `Complete, except one manual step: real Express/Firestore/Auth end-to-end proven via emulator + real Google calls; only the literal interactive browser click-through (typing in the widget, clicking a suggestion) has never been performed, since this environment has no browser-automation tool. See the 2026-09-22 emulator-validation entry below.`

### 2026-09-22 — Phase 46 implementation

Implemented in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`. Google Cloud project access has not been granted — no real Google API calls were made anywhere (backend or frontend), no client key was inserted anywhere, and no live validation is claimed. Every backend test injects a fake `fetchImpl`/`global.fetch`; every frontend test exercises pure logic with no browser key configured.

**Provider-independent architecture** (`backend/services/addressProviders/`): `registry.js` selects an adapter purely from server env (`ADDRESS_PROVIDER`, default `google`), mirroring `pricingProviders/registry.js`'s existing convention exactly — the client can never select a provider. `googleAddressProvider.js` implements `getPlaceDetails(placeId, {signal})` (Geocoding API `place_id` lookup, server key, field-masked to `address_components,formatted_address,geometry,place_id` only) and `normalizeAddress(rawResult)` (pure `address_components` → flat-values mapping, no US-only assumptions — `locality`→`postal_town`→`sublocality` fallback chain for city, `administrative_area_level_1/2` for state/county, `subpremise` for unit). Provider-specific shapes stop at this file's boundary; `backend/utils/addressNormalization.js` (the schema) and `backend/services/propertyService.js` (orchestration) never see a Google-shaped object.

**Key separation:** `GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_KEY` (backend-only, `backend/config/googlePlaces.js`, never logged/returned) vs. `VITE_GOOGLE_MAPS_BROWSER_KEY` (frontend build-time env, `frontend/src/config/googleMaps.js`) — two independent variables, neither backend code nor any API response ever reads or forwards the other's value. Chosen design for the browser key: **build-time Vite config**, not a value served through the public-config endpoint (documented alternative the phase brief allowed) — lower latency, no round trip before the widget can load, and the key never transits our own API surface at all. `GET /api/reports/property-lookup/config` (new, public, unauthenticated, modeled directly on the existing `GET /reports/ai-status` precedent) returns only `{enabled, browserAutocompleteConfigured, serverNormalizationConfigured, schemaVersion, capabilities}` — a dedicated test asserts the serialized response never contains the server key string, and `getPublicConfig()`'s own return type has no key-shaped field at all to leak.

**Normalized schema** (`backend/utils/addressNormalization.js` / frontend mirror `frontend/src/utils/propertyProfile.js`, schemaVersion 1): 17 fields (`formattedAddress` … `placeId`), each `{value, source, verificationStatus, userOverride}` — sources `manual_entry|suggested|provider_selected|provider_normalized|user_overridden|legacy_manual|unavailable`, verification statuses `unverified|provider_normalized|user_confirmed|unavailable|ambiguous|stale_recheck_required`, profile-level status `unconfirmed|confirmed|stale`. Coordinates are validated finite + in-range (`isValidLatitude`/`isValidLongitude`) and dropped to `null` (never clamped/guessed) when invalid. `propertyLookupEligible` (Phase 47 handoff) is `true` only for `countryCode === 'US'` — never a validity judgment on non-US data.

**Trust boundary** (`backend/utils/addressNormalizationStore.js`, mirrors `pricingProposalStore.js`'s established pattern exactly): `POST /reports/property-lookup/normalize` resolves a browser-selected `placeId` through the SERVER key and persists a short-lived (`ADDRESS_NORMALIZATION_TTL_MINUTES`, default 15) `addressNormalizations/{token}` record scoped to the requesting uid — a top-level collection, not a report subcollection, since normalization can happen before a report exists. `PUT /:id/property-profile` (new) never accepts client-claimed `source`/`verificationStatus`/coordinates as trusted; `provider_confirmed` mode requires a valid, owned, unexpired `normalizationToken`, and `propertyService.buildConfirmedPropertyProfile` reconstructs trusted values from the SERVER-STORED record only, with any `overrides` explicitly flipping just those fields to `user_overridden`. A field the user already owns (`userOverride: true`) is carried forward untouched on a later re-confirm unless that exact key is re-overridden in the same call — the "never silently overwrite a confirmed value" rule enforced structurally, not by convention. `manual` mode never touches the normalization store at all.

**Persistence/authorization:** `PUT /:id/property-profile` reuses the exact access pattern already established by `PUT /:id/canonical-estimate`/pricing routes (`getReportAccess` owner-with-`canEditReports`-or-review-grantee, 404 on no access to avoid existence leaks, `409 REPORT_FINALIZED` immutability, `{success, error, code}` envelope, `recordAuditLog`). `GET /:id` now synthesizes a non-destructive, read-only `legacy_manual`/`unavailable` `propertyProfile` view from a pre-Phase-46 report's plain `propertyAddress`/`propertyStreet`/etc. fields when none exists yet — never written back by the GET handler, never claims Google verification for data that was always plain text, and an existing Phase-46-era `propertyProfile` already on the report is left untouched.

**Frontend:** `AddressAutocompleteField.jsx` (ARIA combobox: `role="combobox"`/`listbox`, keyboard nav, `aria-live` status region, loading/empty/error/offline states, debounced + out-of-order-protected via `frontend/src/utils/addressAutocomplete.js`, Google Places session tokens rotated on selection) sits directly in `Dashboard.jsx`'s existing Step 2 "Property Address" field, gated behind the sanitized config check — when Google isn't configured/available it silently renders as the exact same plain manual input as before (zero behavior change for every existing user today, since no key is configured anywhere yet). `PropertyProfileReview.jsx` is the required review/accept/edit/reject step before anything is treated as confirmed. Confirmation is wizard-local state until the report is created, then persisted with one follow-up `savePropertyProfile` call (failure there is logged/toasted, non-blocking — the report itself was already created with its plain-text fields intact either way, satisfying "Google unavailable/failed never blocks report creation").

**Phase 43 regional-input handoff:** `mapPropertyProfileToLocationContext`/`computeLocationFingerprint` (`frontend/src/utils/propertyProfile.js`) map a *confirmed* profile to the exact `{country, state, city, postalCode}` shape `pricingSuggestions.buildPriceSuggestionsRequestPayload` already sends to `POST /:id/estimate-detail/price-suggestions` — no PII, no lat/long, no raw provider payload, never a room-size/living-area substitution (Phase 43's line-item quantities are untouched by this phase entirely). **Scope note:** PHASES.md's own Phase 46 entry lists "wiring property data into estimate pricing region" as out-of-scope/a Phase-43-follow-on, while the fuller Phase 46 change request asked for this mapping plus a one-time prefill; both are satisfied without contradiction by keeping the wiring additive and non-invasive — `CanonicalEstimateEditor` in `ReportPreviewPage.jsx` prefills its four manual pricing-location inputs from `report.propertyProfile` exactly once, only while all four are still blank, never overwriting anything the user already typed, and OpenAI/`pricingService.js`/`config/openai.js` were not touched at all (per the standing instruction not to modify that integration). Deeper wiring (e.g. auto-invalidating a pricing cache on address change) remains the documented Phase 43 follow-on.

**Phase 47 handoff:** `buildPropertyLookupHandoff`/`computePropertyLookupEligibility` expose `{propertyLookupEligible, countryCode, confirmed}` — `true` only for a confirmed US address, `false` (not invalid) for anything else, and RealtyAPI is never called from this phase.

**Automated validation (exact counts, real run):** 6 new backend test files, **50 new tests** (schema/mapping 10, Google adapter request-construction/error-mapping/component-mapping 14, provider registry 2, normalization-store trust boundary 5, propertyService incl. key-separation + trust-boundary/override-preservation 10, property-profile route incl. access-control/immutability/legacy-fallback 9). Full backend suite **1018/1018 passing** (was 968/968; 0 regressions). Backend lint **0 errors, 63 warnings** (unchanged verified baseline — confirmed via a fresh run before writing this entry). Frontend: 5 new files, **27 new tests** (autocomplete debounce/sequencing/session-token/keyboard-nav 11, propertyProfile mapping/eligibility/legacy/fingerprint 14, googleMaps key-separation/missing-config 2). Full frontend suite **231/231 passing, 24/24 files** (was 204/204; 0 regressions), lint 0 errors/14 warnings (unchanged, none from new files), clean production build (`vite build` succeeds; the pre-existing >500kB `index` chunk warning is unrelated to this phase). `git diff --check` clean (one harmless LF→CRLF line-ending notice, not an error). Secret/key scan (Google API-key prefix pattern + literal env-var-with-value pattern) across the full diff: clean. Confirmed zero Google network calls anywhere in the automated suite (every provider call is injected). `mobile/` untouched; the original `feature/mobile-initial-phases` working tree's `git status` still shows exactly its known 53 entries, byte-for-byte unaffected (worktree isolation confirmed). Phase 47/48 sections of this file are unedited by this session.

**Live Google validation checklist (genuinely pending, not done):** 1) obtain Google Cloud project access or have the client configure it; 2) confirm active billing; 3) enable Maps JavaScript API; 4) enable Places API (New); 5) enable Geocoding API; 6) create a browser key restricted by approved HTTP referrers + Maps JavaScript API/Places API (New) only; 7) create a separate server key restricted by production/dev server IP + Geocoding API only; 8) store keys only in ignored local env files and the deployment platform's secret config (Render for `GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_KEY`, Vercel for `VITE_GOOGLE_MAPS_BROWSER_KEY`) — never in `.env.example`/source/tests/logs; 9) verify `.env.local`/real `.env` files stay gitignored and absent from Git; 10) test autocomplete on localhost with the real browser key; 11) test one valid US address end-to-end (autocomplete → normalize → review → confirm → persisted `propertyProfile`); 12) test one valid non-US address (confirm `propertyLookupEligible:false`, everything else still works); 13) test an apartment/subpremise address and an incomplete/no-result address; 14) verify city/state/postal/country/coordinate mapping against real Google responses for a few different countries; 15) verify edit/confirm/reject/recheck behavior end-to-end against real data; 16) verify manual fallback with the feature flag off; 17) verify a deliberately-misconfigured/restricted key surfaces the safe `ADDRESS_PERMISSION_DENIED`/`ADDRESS_BILLING_DISABLED` states, never a raw Google error; 18) inspect network/log/build/diff output for key leakage one more time against the real keys; 19) confirm production referrer/IP restrictions are the actually-deployed ones; 20) record real quota/latency and confirm billing alerts/controls are in place; 21) only then mark Phase 46 fully **Complete**.

### 2026-09-22 — Phase 46 controlled local live Google validation (partial — exact blocker below)
