## Phase 47 — Property Intelligence: RealtyAPI U.S. Adapter & Report Integration

**Objective:** Detailed US property data (parcel/year-built/sq-ft/etc.) via a provider-independent RealtyAPI adapter, populating `PropertyProfile` for US addresses confirmed by Phase 46.

**Reason this phase is required:** Change-request field list — full requirement: parcel/APN, property type, year built, living area, lot size, bedrooms, bathrooms, stories, garage type/spaces, roof type, exterior construction/siding, foundation type, heating/cooling (HVAC), assessed value, property tax, last sale date/price, county, municipality/city, ownership information where legally/technically available, flood zone/hazard information, and building footprint/map information — all where available.

**Scope:** US-only RealtyAPI mapping into Phase 46's normalized schema; adapter interface allows substituting a different US property-data provider later. Includes safe, opt-in population of the primary report's existing Section 3 (Property Info) from user-confirmed values only.

**Reuse:** Phase 46's `PropertyProfile` schema and per-field provenance/conflict pattern — this phase only adds a new source into that existing structure. Section 3's existing narrative-prose rendering path (`aiService.js`'s Section 3 content) is extended with confirmed structured values, not replaced.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Build `backend/config/realtyApi.js` (server-only), mapping RealtyAPI's full response into `PropertyProfile`'s shape — parcel/APN, property type, year built, living area, lot size, beds/baths, stories, garage, roof, exterior/siding, foundation, HVAC, assessed value/property tax, last sale date/price, county/municipality, ownership (where available), flood/hazard data, building footprint/map data — each field individually tagged `source: 'Third-Party Property Data'`.
2. Wire into the Phase 46 flow: after Google confirms a US address, query RealtyAPI, merge with per-field conflict surfacing against any manually-entered value.
3. Handle partial/failed provider responses gracefully — missing fields stay editable per-field (a field RealtyAPI doesn't return is simply absent/manual, not a whole-lookup failure), never block report creation.
4. Add caching to avoid repeat lookups for the same parcel within a reasonable window.
5. Once the user reviews/confirms `PropertyProfile` values (Phase 46's confirmation workflow), safely populate the report's existing Section 3 (Property Info) narrative from those confirmed values only — AI-suggested-but-unconfirmed values never reach Section 3. This is the one explicitly approved exception to "Sections outside Section 7 remain unchanged": Section 3's *content* may now be populated from confirmed property data instead of purely adjuster-typed input; its layout/structure/heading is not otherwise touched.

**Data model / API implications:** Extends `PropertyProfile` with the full RealtyAPI-sourced field set above; no new collection. Section 3 population reads only `userConfirmed`/`adjusterConfirmed` status fields from `PropertyProfile` (Phase 46), never a raw unconfirmed provider value.

**Security / privacy:** RealtyAPI key backend-only; document retention policy for tax/ownership data explicitly.

**Files/components likely affected:** New `backend/config/realtyApi.js`, `backend/services/propertyService.js` (extend), `backend/test/realty-api-adapter.test.js` (mocked).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 46; RealtyAPI exact product/credentials (pending, client-flagged name-collision risk).

**Automated validation:** Mocked RealtyAPI response tests (full data, partial data, provider error) confirming graceful degradation and correct conflict surfacing.

**Manual/runtime validation (blocked until RealtyAPI credentials confirmed):** Real lookup against known US addresses, confirm field accuracy and conflict UI.

**Backward compatibility / regression:** Non-US addresses and Phase-46-only US addresses (RealtyAPI unavailable) continue to work via manual entry, unaffected.

**Out of scope:** Any property-data provider beyond RealtyAPI; auto-setting estimate pricing region from property data (a follow-on wiring, not required for completion).

**Rollback/recovery:** Additive; disabling the adapter reverts to Phase 46's Google-only behavior with zero data impact.

**Status:** `Contract Confirmed & Live-Validated (code + real-browser UI); ToS/Data-Retention Written Confirmation RECEIVED 2026-09-23 — Production Key/Config Activation Pending (PRO plan + production key confirmation still required)` (see the 2026-09-22 live-validation/browser-verification entries and the 2026-09-23 ToS-confirmation entry below for full detail; exactly 1 blocker remains before this can be marked fully Complete)

### 2026-09-22 — Phase 47 implementation

Implemented in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`. The exact RealtyAPI product/documentation/base URL/authentication/plan/field coverage are NOT yet confirmed (per this phase's own "pending, client-flagged name-collision risk" note) — no real property-data network call was made anywhere, no key was ever placed in source/tests/logs/docs, and no live U.S. lookup is claimed.

**Provider-independent architecture** (`backend/services/propertyIntelligenceProviders/`): `registry.js` selects an adapter purely from server env (`PROPERTY_INTELLIGENCE_PROVIDER`, default `realty_api`), mirroring `addressProviders/registry.js`/`pricingProviders/registry.js` exactly. `realtyApiProvider.js` implements the documented `PropertyDataProvider` contract shape (`PROVIDER_NAME`, `lookupProperty(normalizedAddress, options)`, `normalizePropertyResult(rawResult)`) but `lookupProperty` **unconditionally** throws `PROPERTY_PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` — there is no `fetch()`/network call to any host anywhere in the file, even if `REALTY_API_KEY`/`REALTY_API_BASE_URL`/`PROPERTY_INTELLIGENCE_ENABLED` are all set (a dedicated test stubs `global.fetch` to throw if ever called, then proves it never is). `mockPropertyProvider.js` satisfies the same contract for tests only and is never registered, so it can never be selected in production by any env value.

**Normalized schema** (`backend/utils/propertyIntelligence.js`, schemaVersion 1): its own `FIELD_KEYS` (parcel/APN, property type, year built, living area+unit, lot size+unit, beds/baths/stories, garage type/spaces, roof/exterior/foundation/HVAC, assessed value+currency, property tax+currency, last sale date/price+currency, owner-on-record, flood zone, hazard summary, footprint area+unit) — deliberately a SEPARATE namespace from Phase 46's address `FIELD_KEYS` (those are address/geocoding components; these are building/parcel/tax/hazard/ownership facts), so this phase never edits a line of Phase 46's own schema. Lives at `report.propertyProfile.propertyIntelligence`, a sibling of Phase 46's `propertyProfile.fields` — one `PropertyProfile`, never a second source of truth. Every field is `{value, source, verificationStatus, userOverride}`, `source: 'Third-Party Property Data'` exactly as task 1 specifies. Each optional field has its own validator (parcel safe-character/length; year-built range; area/lot finite-non-negative + supported unit; bedroom/bathroom [.5 steps allowed]/stories/garage counts; money/currency reusing `canonicalEstimate.js`'s `validateMoney`/`isValidCurrency`; past-only date; capped/sanitized free text; a footprint-geometry size/depth guard) — an invalid optional field is dropped to an editable blank, never coerced into an apparently-verified value, without discarding the rest of an otherwise-usable result.

**Trust boundary** (`backend/utils/propertyIntelligenceStore.js`, mirrors `addressNormalizationStore.js`/`pricingProposalStore.js`): a short-lived (`PROPERTY_INTELLIGENCE_LOOKUP_TTL_MINUTES`, default 30), REPORT-SCOPED (`reports/{reportId}/propertyLookups/{lookupId}`) record — report-scoped, not top-level, since a lookup only ever happens against an EXISTING report's already-confirmed address. Reusable until expiry (not single-consume). `PUT /:id/property-intelligence` never trusts a client-claimed `source`/`verificationStatus`: `provider_confirmed` mode requires a valid, owned, unexpired `lookupId`, and only `selectedKeys` are pulled from that server-stored record ("user applies selected fields only"); an already user-owned field (`userOverride: true`) survives an apply untouched unless re-overridden in the same call. Cross-report/cross-user `lookupId` use is rejected; forged/expired/unknown tokens are rejected. A minimal, optional shared cache (`propertyIntelligenceCache/{addressFingerprint}`, `backend/utils/propertyIntelligenceFingerprint.js` — a pure SHA-256 hash of normalized address + provider + schema version, no reportId/userId/PII) lets two reports looking up the same real address share non-ownership data; `ownerOnRecord` is explicitly stripped before anything reaches that shared doc.

**Eligibility**: `propertyIntelligenceService.computeEligibility` requires a CONFIRMED address (Phase 46's `status === 'confirmed'`), `propertyLookupEligible === true` (US), and the minimum address components present — every other case (`address_not_confirmed`/`country_not_supported`/`incomplete_address`) is a normal 200 response, never a thrown error, never blocking report creation.

**Section 3 integration** (`backend/utils/propertyIntelligenceContent.js`): builds a `### Confirmed Property Details (Public Records)` block from ONLY `verificationStatus === 'user_confirmed'` fields and APPENDS it beneath Section 3's existing narrative (never replacing it, unlike Section 7's replace behavior) — the server computes this once inside `GET /:id` (`reportOut.propertySection3Markdown`, same architecture as Phase 42's `detailMarkdown`) so the frontend only duplicates the tiny generic splice algorithm, never the money/label formatting. The export route composes this on top of the existing Section 7 injection before `generatePDF`/`generateDOCX`/`generateHTML`, proven identical across all three plus the router's own `_test.generateHTML` by a dedicated cross-format test. A report with nothing confirmed renders byte-identical to before this phase; a legacy report gets a safe, empty, read-only synthesized view (a NEW object, never a mutation of the underlying doc-data object).

**API surface** (mounted automatically at both `/api` and `/api/v1` via the existing mobile-phase dual-prefix wiring): `GET /reports/property-lookup/intelligence/config` (public, sanitized), `POST /reports/:id/property-lookup/intelligence`, `GET /reports/:id/property-lookup/intelligence`, `PUT /reports/:id/property-intelligence` — each enforcing the same access/`409 REPORT_FINALIZED`/`{success,error,code}` pattern as every other report-mutation route.

**Frontend:** `PropertyIntelligenceReview` (new export from the EXISTING `components/PropertyProfileReview.jsx`, per this phase's own "extend, don't duplicate" instruction) + `PropertyIntelligenceModal` (`ReportPreviewPage.jsx`, mirrors `CanonicalEstimateEditor`'s modal pattern) behind a new "Property Details (Section 3)" button.

**Automated validation (exact counts, real run):** 8 new backend test files, **84 new tests** (schema/validators 16, fingerprint 4, provider registry/adapters 8, trust-boundary+cache store 9, orchestration service 18, route-level 14, Section 3 content 12, cross-format export integration 3). Full backend suite **1102/1102 passing** (was 1018/1018; 0 regressions). Backend lint **0 errors, 63 warnings** (unchanged verified baseline). Frontend: 2 new test files, **13 new tests**. Full frontend suite **244/244 passing, 26/26 files** (was 231/231; 0 regressions), lint 0 errors/14 warnings (unchanged, none from new files), clean production build. `git diff --check` clean (one harmless LF→CRLF notice, same as every prior phase in this worktree). Secret/key scan across the full diff: clean. Confirmed zero property-data-provider network calls anywhere in the automated suite. `mobile/` untouched; the original `feature/mobile-initial-phases` working tree's `git status` still shows exactly its known 53 entries, byte-for-byte unaffected. Phase 48 section of this file is unedited by this session.

**Live RealtyAPI validation checklist (genuinely pending, not done):** see the 21-point checklist in the phase brief's "LIVE REALTYAPI WORK — DEFERRED, NOT FAKED" section (obtain the exact product/docs; confirm base URL/auth/plan/endpoints/rate limits; review terms/retention/display requirements; store a real key only in ignored env + deployment secrets; finalize the request builder + real response-field mapping in `realtyApiProvider.js` — the only two functions left, everything else already consumes the normalized shape; run sanitized U.S. lookups incl. no-match/partial/flood/footprint; verify Section 3 export with real data; remove temporary credentials; only then mark Phase 47 fully Complete).

**Files changed:** `backend/config/realtyApi.js` (new), `backend/services/propertyIntelligenceProviders/{registry,realtyApiProvider,mockPropertyProvider}.js` (new), `backend/utils/propertyIntelligence.js` + `propertyIntelligenceFingerprint.js` + `propertyIntelligenceStore.js` + `propertyIntelligenceContent.js` (new), `backend/services/propertyIntelligenceService.js` (new), `backend/routes/reports.js` (new routes + `GET /:id`/export wiring + `PUT /:id/property-profile` staleness hook), `backend/middleware/rateLimiters.js` (`propertyIntelligenceLimiter`), `backend/.env.example` (new inert Property Intelligence vars), 8 new `backend/test/property-intelligence-*.test.js` files (one existing Phase 46 test assertion updated additively, see `property-profile-route.test.js`); `frontend/src/utils/propertyIntelligence.js` + `propertyIntelligenceContent.js` (new), `frontend/src/components/PropertyProfileReview.jsx` (new `PropertyIntelligenceReview` export), `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx` (`PropertyIntelligenceModal` + button + Section 3 display wiring), `frontend/src/services/api.js` (4 new calls), 2 new `frontend/src/__tests__/propertyIntelligence*.test.js` files.

### 2026-09-22 — Phase 47 live RealtyAPI validation session

Real credentials existed in `backend/.env` (a fresh key the client had already rotated in) going into this session. **Safety gate passed before any call was made:** presence-only verification of `REALTY_API_KEY`/`REALTY_API_BASE_URL`/`PROPERTY_INTELLIGENCE_ENABLED` (lengths only, never printed), confirmed `backend/.env` is gitignored (`backend/.gitignore:2`), untracked, and absent from `git status`, and confirmed via a value-match grep (never printing the key) that it existed nowhere else in the worktree.

**Contract confirmed directly from RealtyAPI's own docs + OpenAPI spec** (`realtor.realtyapi.io/openapi.json`), matching the client-supplied dashboard values exactly: product "Realtor", base URL `https://realtor.realtyapi.io`, `GET /details/byaddress`, query param `address`, header `x-realtyapi-key`, response headers `x-credits-limit`/`x-credits-remaining`, standard `{error}`-shaped error bodies, and a documented `401`/`402`/`403`/`404`/`429`/`5xx` taxonomy. **One real discrepancy found:** the docs advertise "2 credits/call," but the `x-credits-remaining` header only dropped by 1 per call across all 3 real calls made this session (20000→19999→19998→19997) — this account's actual observed cost is 1 credit/call, not 2. Documented here rather than silently assumed.

**The 200 response BODY schema is genuinely undocumented by RealtyAPI** (their own OpenAPI spec leaves it as `{}`) — this was the one thing no amount of doc-reading could resolve, exactly as flagged by the original implementation session below. **3 real, controlled live calls** (kept to the low end of the pre-agreed "at most 3" budget) resolved it: 2 real US properties (RealtyAPI's own published example addresses — a townhome and a single-family home, different states) for full-data mapping, plus 1 synthetic/unmatchable address for the no-match path. Key findings, all evidence-based (no field was ever mapped from a guessed name):
- The real shape is `{message, source, detail}` — an **MLS-listing-centric** response (list price, agents/broker contact, photos, mortgage estimate, days-on-market), not a county-assessor parcel record. Confirmed IDENTICAL top-level schema and `details_sections` category/label naming across both real properties.
- **No-match is HTTP 200 with an empty `detail: {}`**, not a `404` status — despite the human-readable `message` field literally containing the text "404: address not found on realtor.com". `lookupProperty` now detects this structurally (empty `detail`), never by string-matching the message.
- Most structural facts (`year_built`, `sqft`, `lot_sqft`, `beds`, `stories`, `garage` count, `type`, tax history with per-year `assessment.total`/`tax`, `last_sold_date`/`price`, `local.flood.fema_zone`/`flood_trend_paragraph`, `property_id`) live directly on `detail`/`detail.details`/`detail.tax_history`.
- Several change-request fields (**roof, exterior construction, foundation, garage type, parcel/APN, and — since `detail.details.heating`/`cooling` were consistently `null` in every real response — heating/cooling too**) are NOT separate structured keys; Realtor.com only exposes them as free-text `"Label: Value"` bullet lines inside `detail.details_sections[].text`, grouped by category (e.g. "Building and Construction", "Garage and Parking"). `normalizePropertyResult` now parses those bullets.
- **Two fields have no evidence anywhere in either real response and are left permanently unmapped by this provider** (an honest data-source gap, not a bug, per Golden Rule #1): `ownerOnRecord` (Realtor.com's public listing data never exposes an owner-of-record identity, consistent with its own public listing pages showing only agent/broker contact) and `footprintAreaValue`/`footprintAreaUnit` (no distinct building-footprint geometry exists separate from living-area sqft). A real "full" lookup against this provider can therefore never reach `LOOKUP_STATUS.FULL` — `PARTIAL` is the correct, expected ceiling, and the automated suite now asserts that explicitly rather than asserting an impossible "full."
- True multi-candidate ambiguity (a `PropertyDataProvider.ambiguous` result) was never observed — RealtyAPI's docs say disambiguation by city/state/zip happens server-side, never returned to the caller as a candidate list; `ambiguous` stays permanently `false` for this provider based on real evidence, not a guess.

**Implementation** (both previously-stubbed functions in `realtyApiProvider.js`, exactly as scoped by the original session's own "two functions left" note): `lookupProperty` now builds the real authenticated request (config-sourced base URL/key, `AbortController` timeout via `REALTY_API_TIMEOUT_MS`, retry-on-transient via `REALTY_API_MAX_RETRIES` for timeouts/network errors/5xx only, never 4xx), and maps the confirmed status taxonomy onto this codebase's `PROPERTY_*` error-code convention (`routes/reports.js`'s `PROPERTY_INTELLIGENCE_ERROR_STATUS` extended with `PROPERTY_QUOTA_EXCEEDED`→402, `PROPERTY_RATE_LIMITED`→429, `PROPERTY_INVALID_INPUT`→400, `PROPERTY_PERMISSION_DENIED`→500 (hides provider-account-state from the client, same precedent as `ADDRESS_PERMISSION_DENIED`), `PROPERTY_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`→503, plus a `PROPERTY_CANCELLED` client-disconnect short-circuit mirroring the Phase 46 address route). `normalizePropertyResult` implements the evidence-based mapping above, including a `details_sections` bullet-line parser and a "pick the highest `year` tax_history entry" reducer (not naive array-index-0). The registry/trust-boundary/cache/Section-3/export architecture from the original session is completely untouched — exactly as that session predicted ("nothing else in this codebase needs to change").

**Validation performed:** `property-intelligence-provider-registry.test.js`'s RealtyAPI-specific tests were rewritten against the real, confirmed contract (13 tests: request-building, the full status/error taxonomy, retry behavior, the real no-match shape, and field-mapping against a SANITIZED fixture shaped exactly like the real response with 100%-synthetic values) — every test injects `fetchImpl`, so the automated suite makes zero real network calls, same guarantee the old placeholder test enforced. A new `property-intelligence-realtyapi-e2e.test.js` (2 tests) exercises the REAL provider code (only its transport is stubbed) through the full pipeline: lookup → cache reuse (second request makes zero additional provider calls) → selective `provider_confirmed` apply → a user override surviving a later re-apply untouched → persistence into a report doc → confirmed-only Section 3 markdown → an actual rendered PDF buffer (text-extracted and asserted) containing only the user's confirmed/overridden values, never an unselected field (`parcelNumber` asserted absent from the export) — plus the no-match path resolving to a safe status, never a blocking error. No Firebase Emulator Suite binary is installed in this environment (`firebase` CLI not found); this used the same in-memory `FakeFirestore` double every other Phase 47 persistence test already relies on — real persistence *logic* is exercised, but production/real Firestore was never touched, matching the instruction's actual safety requirement. Full backend suite: **1158/1159 passing** (1157 tests existed pre-session + 2 net new here after replacing 2 old placeholder tests with 13; the single failure, `integration-status-service.test.js`'s `googleAddress: not_configured...` test, is a pre-existing, unrelated ambient-`.env` fragility — a real `GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_KEY` already present in `backend/.env` from earlier, unrelated work, not touched or introduced by this session). Lint: **0 errors, 63 warnings** (unchanged baseline). New/touched files are prettier-clean; `routes/reports.js`'s pre-existing un-formatted state (from other uncommitted work already in this shared worktree) was left as-is rather than reformatting a shared file wholesale. `git diff --check` clean (the same single harmless LF→CRLF notice every prior phase in this worktree shows). Secret scan: the real key value was confirmed to exist in exactly one place on disk (`backend/.env`) throughout, verified absent from every diff/new file/test fixture, and cleared from `.env` at the end of this session (`REALTY_API_KEY=` blanked, `PROPERTY_INTELLIGENCE_ENABLED` reset to `false`); a full re-run of the backend suite after clearing it still passed 1158/1159, confirming the new tests are fully self-contained and never depended on the ambient credential. No real-browser (Playwright) verification of the frontend `PropertyIntelligenceModal`/conflict-review UI was performed this session (same documented tooling gap noted on most phases since Phase 24). Frontend was not touched and its suite was not re-run (no frontend files changed by this session).

**Exact remaining blockers before this phase can be marked fully Complete** (per its own "Live RealtyAPI validation checklist," now down from 21 open points to these 3): (1) a legal/ops review of RealtyAPI's Terms of Service for data-retention and display requirements — not performed this session, needs a human/legal decision, not something to infer from the API docs; (2) the real key needs to be set in Render's production secrets — this session only ever used it locally in the gitignored `.env`, now cleared, so production `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`-style deployment wiring for `REALTY_API_KEY` still needs to happen separately; (3) a live-browser pass of the frontend property-review/conflict UI against a real report, which no session since Phase 24 has had tooling for. Everything else on the original 21-point list — exact product/docs, base URL/auth/plan/endpoints/rate-limit confirmation, the request builder, the real response-field mapping, sanitized U.S. lookups including no-match/partial/flood, and removing temporary credentials — is now done.

**Files changed this session:** `backend/services/propertyIntelligenceProviders/realtyApiProvider.js` (request builder + error taxonomy + field mapping implemented), `backend/routes/reports.js` (error-status map extended, `PROPERTY_CANCELLED` short-circuit added), `backend/test/property-intelligence-provider-registry.test.js` (RealtyAPI-specific tests rewritten against the confirmed contract), `backend/test/property-intelligence-realtyapi-e2e.test.js` (new), `backend/.env` (key cleared, not committed — gitignored).

### 2026-09-22 (later same day) — Phase 47 real-browser UI verification session, PASSED (closes blocker #3 of 3 from the entry above)

Set up a full local walkthrough against the Firebase Emulator Suite (Firestore, Auth, and — after a mid-session fix, see below — Storage), with **zero RealtyAPI credentials and zero network calls to any property-data provider anywhere in this session**: the property-intelligence provider registry was stubbed via a require-cache injection in a new, untracked `backend/_dev-emulator-server.js` (the exact same technique `property-intelligence-service.test.js`'s own `loadServiceWithProvider` already uses in the automated suite, just applied to a live dev server) — `lookupProperty` returned a hardcoded local sanitized fixture and never called `fetch`; `normalizePropertyResult` was the real, unmodified production mapping function, so the field-mapping logic exercised in the browser was the actual shipped code. A disposable Auth-emulator user + one seeded report (confirmed U.S. address, never touching production Firestore) were used throughout.

**Bug found and fixed along the way (setup-only, not a Phase 47 code defect):** Desktop Section 3 preview passed immediately, but the PDF export failed both on first try and retry. Root-caused by reproducing `uploadBuffer()` directly: the Admin SDK's Storage client was falling through to real Google Cloud Storage (`404 The specified bucket does not exist`) because the emulator session had only started `firestore,auth` — the Storage emulator was never running and `FIREBASE_STORAGE_EMULATOR_HOST` was never set. The export route's existing `storage-upload` stage handler correctly failed closed with a sanitized `502 EXPORT_UPLOAD_ERROR`, exactly per its Rule #6 design (never leak the raw GCS error) — this was confirmed as a **local emulator-topology gap, not a production-code defect**, and zero application source was changed to fix it. Fix: added the Storage emulator to the suite (port 9199) and restarted the backend with `FIREBASE_STORAGE_EMULATOR_HOST` set; the disposable user/report/confirmed-fields state was then reproduced through the real `POST .../property-lookup/intelligence` → `PUT .../property-intelligence` API calls (not hand-written into Firestore), so the re-verification exercised the identical production code path.

**Verified in the real browser, full click-through:** property-lookup modal loaded all fixture fields; selective checkbox-confirm worked; an in-modal edit to Roof Type ("Composition Shingle" → "Architectural Shingle") was preserved through confirm; a later "Re-check" preserved the two already-confirmed fields (Year Built, Roof Type) untouched and added a third (Bedrooms) without disturbing them (the userOverride-survives-a-later-apply guarantee `applyPropertyIntelligence` already enforces server-side, now also confirmed end-to-end through real UI clicks); Desktop Section 3 showed exactly the 3 confirmed fields (Year Built 2004, Bedrooms 3, Roof Type Architectural Shingle) and nothing else; PDF preview loaded and, on independent verification (downloading the real generated PDF and text-extracting it via the existing `pdfTextExtract` test helper, then programmatically checking for all 18 OTHER fields the lookup fixture had populated but were never confirmed — parcel number, living area, lot size, garage type/spaces, exterior construction, foundation, heating, cooling, assessed value, tax, last-sale date/price, flood zone, hazard summary), showed the identical 3 confirmed fields with **zero leakage** of any unconfirmed field into either Desktop or PDF output.

**Cleanup performed at the end of this session:** the one temporary tracked-file change (`frontend/src/config/firebase.js`'s explicit, `VITE_FIREBASE_EMULATOR=true`-gated emulator connector) was reverted to its pre-session state; frontend/backend dev servers and all three Firebase emulators were stopped; the disposable Auth-emulator user, seeded report, and all emulator data were discarded (nothing was ever in production Firestore to begin with); the untracked `backend/_dev-emulator-server.js`, `backend/_dev-seed-report.js`, `backend/_dev-verify-email.js`, and every scratchpad-local emulator config/log/downloaded-PDF file were deleted. `git diff --check` clean; a presence-only secret scan confirmed no key/credential value present anywhere in the working tree. Nothing committed/staged/pushed/merged/deployed/opened as a PR.

**This closed blocker #3 of the 3 listed in the entry above.** At that point, exactly 2 remained before Phase 47 could be marked fully Complete: (1) a legal/ops review of RealtyAPI's Terms of Service for data-retention/display requirements, formalized as a **written confirmation from RealtyAPI support**; (2) activating the real key in Render's production secrets. **Blocker (1) was closed 2026-09-23 — see the session entry immediately below.** Phase 43/45/46/48's own statuses are unchanged.

### 2026-09-23 — Phase 47 RealtyAPI ToS/data-retention written confirmation RECEIVED, code compliance reviewed (closes blocker #1 of the 2 remaining above)

**The client obtained written approval from RealtyAPI through its authenticated dashboard support chat**, confirming 10 specific terms. This session's job was narrow and explicit: record the confirmation accurately, and independently verify the ALREADY-SHIPPED Phase 47 code actually complies with the terms that constrain implementation behavior — not to write new code. **No mismatch was found against any term, so no production code was changed** (per the client's own explicit "do not change code unless a real mismatch is found" instruction).

**The 10 confirmed terms, verbatim as reported by the client:**
1. Processed and normalized facts may be used and stored in FlacronAI reports.
2. Raw API access/responses must not be resold or republished as a competing data feed.
3. A 30-day cache and confirmed fields stored alongside issued reports are allowed.
4. No UI or export attribution is required.
5. Owner, MLS and valuation fields have no special restrictions.
6. Photo URLs must be treated as source-CDN links, not assets owned by FlacronAI.
7. Valuation figures must be labeled as third-party estimates.
8. No mandatory deletion or refresh schedule applies.
9. RealtyAPI PRO permits commercial production use.
10. RealtyAPI returns publicly listed data and stores no FlacronAI end-user data; FlacronAI remains responsible for its own data-subject obligations.

**Compliance review, term by term, against the actual code (not the doc's own claims):**
- **Term 1 (use/store facts in reports):** Compliant. `applyPropertyIntelligence` (`propertyIntelligenceService.js`) is the only path that ever persists a field, onto `report.propertyProfile.propertyIntelligence`; `buildSection3PropertyBlockMarkdown` (`propertyIntelligenceContent.js`) is the only path that ever renders one into an export — both already shipped, unchanged.
- **Term 2 (no raw-feed resale/republishing):** Compliant. Traced the full data path: `realtyApiProvider.lookupProperty` returns `{raw, ambiguous}`; `normalizePropertyResult(raw)` is the ONLY place the raw RealtyAPI response body is ever read, and it returns a small flat map of individually-named fields — the raw object itself is discarded immediately after, never stored (not in the 30-day cache doc, not in the per-report lookup record, not in any Firestore document), never logged, and never returned by any API response (confirmed earlier this Phase 49 session by an independent security audit: only sanitized/selected fields ever leave the backend). There is no endpoint, admin or otherwise, that exposes a raw provider response.
- **Term 3 (30-day cache + confirmed fields alongside reports):** Compliant. `propertyIntelligenceStore.js`'s `CACHE_TTL_DAYS` defaults to exactly `30` (env-overridable, never higher by default) and is actually enforced at read time (`getCachedPropertyValues` treats an expired doc as a cache miss, never serves stale data past the TTL) — not just recorded as unused metadata. Confirmed fields persisted directly on a report (via `applyPropertyIntelligence`) are separate from this 30-day shared cache and are exactly the "confirmed fields stored alongside issued reports" the term permits with no additional time bound.
- **Term 4 (no attribution required):** Compliant/moot either way — the current disclaimer text ("informational public-record data from a third-party source...") doesn't name RealtyAPI at all; since attribution isn't required, this is not a gap.
- **Term 5 (owner/MLS/valuation — no special restriction):** Compliant/moot. `ownerOnRecord` exists in the field schema (`propertyIntelligence.js`'s `FIELD_KEYS`, kept for forward-compatibility with a future provider) but `realtyApiProvider.normalizePropertyResult` never actually populates it — this provider structurally never returns owner-of-record data (documented in its own field-mapping comment: "this product never returns an owner-of-record identity field"). No MLS or valuation field is mapped at all (see term 7). Since none of these fields are ever actually sourced from RealtyAPI in this codebase, there was nothing to restrict and nothing to change.
- **Term 6 (photo URLs as source-CDN links, not FlacronAI assets):** Compliant, vacuously — re-confirmed by re-reading `propertyIntelligence.js`'s full `FIELD_KEYS` list and `realtyApiProvider.js`'s full `normalizePropertyResult` return object: **no photo/image/media URL field exists anywhere in this schema or mapping.** RealtyAPI photos are never ingested, stored, or rendered by this codebase at all, so there is no photo-ownership question to get wrong.
- **Term 7 (valuation figures labeled as third-party estimates):** Compliant, precisely stated — **no true valuation/AVM estimate field is surfaced anywhere.** The only money-shaped fields rendered are `assessedValue` (a tax assessor's on-record figure) and `lastSalePrice`/`propertyTaxAnnual` (historical factual figures) — these are public-record facts, not estimates, and RealtyAPI's own raw response's separate "mortgage estimate"-style field (noted in this phase's own 2026-09-22 live-validation entry as present in the raw MLS-listing shape) is deliberately never mapped into `FIELD_KEYS` or rendered anywhere. All of these fields render under a "### Confirmed Property Details (Public Records)" heading with a standing disclaimer ("informational public-record data from a third-party source... not a coverage determination, appraisal, title report, flood certification, or legal verification"). This term is satisfied because no valuation figure is ever shown, not because an existing valuation figure carries an "estimate" label — worth stating precisely rather than overclaiming.
- **Term 8 (no mandatory deletion/refresh schedule):** Compliant. The code imposes no forced deletion or refresh beyond the optional 30-day cache ceiling (term 3) — nothing mandatory.
- **Term 9 (PRO plan permits commercial use):** Not a code-compliance item — a plan/contract fact. Ties directly into the still-open Render production-activation blocker (see below); the client's own instruction was to keep that blocker open until the PRO plan and production key are separately confirmed.
- **Term 10 (RealtyAPI stores no FlacronAI end-user data; FlacronAI owns its own data-subject obligations):** Not a code-compliance item — confirms no new obligation was created on RealtyAPI's side, and doesn't require any change to this codebase's existing data-subject-rights handling.

**Result: zero mismatches found. Zero production code changed this session** — this was a documentation-and-verification-only pass, exactly as instructed. Nothing staged, committed, pushed, merged, or deployed. No secret was requested, printed, or exposed.

**Blocker #1 (RealtyAPI ToS/data-retention written confirmation) is now CLOSED.** Blocker #2 (Render production key/config activation) **remains explicitly open and is NOT authorized by this entry** — per the client's own instruction, it stays pending until the PRO plan and the production key are separately confirmed and an actual deployment action is taken (out of this worktree's scope regardless).

## Phase 48 — Pricing Page, Admin Configuration UI & Cross-Surface Consistency

**Objective:** Make the public pricing page and admin-facing configuration read from the same `PlanConfig`/vocabulary source of truth built in Phases 41/44/45, eliminating the class of drift already seen once in this codebase (Home page's stale Professional/Agency report-count display, per `CLAUDE.md`'s known-bugs list).

**Reason this phase is required:** Change-request pricing-card requirement; reconciled audit flagged the existing displayed-vs-actual-limit drift precedent as a specific risk to avoid repeating.

**Scope:** Frontend consistency + admin UI completion. No new backend data.

**Reuse:** `PlanConfig` (Phase 44), pack data (Phase 45), vocabulary config (Phase 41).

**Exact tasks:**
1. Pricing page photo-allowance copy reads from `PlanConfig`'s sanitized public read API (Phase 44) — the **same** source enforcement uses, not a separately maintained value, matching confirmed final tier values.
2. Full admin editing UI (extend `AdminTierUpdate.jsx` or a new page) to edit `PlanConfig`: report/photo limits, add-on pack sizes/prices/`packId`s (test-mode Price IDs until Open Question #17 resolves and live Price IDs are added, kept in clearly separate test/live fields per Phase 45), vocabulary superset, watermark-policy flag (Phase 40) — **this phase owns the editing UI; Phase 44 only owns the schema/read/write API it calls.**
3. Server-side validation on the admin write path rejects invalid values (non-positive limits, negative/malformed prices, a live Price ID accidentally saved into the test-mode field) — the admin UI cannot publish a `PlanConfig` that would break enforcement or checkout.
4. Sweep the frontend for every other hardcoded tier-limit/photo-allowance/pack-price copy occurrence (Pricing.jsx, Home.jsx's known-stale section, any other page) and route all of it through the same `PlanConfig` read, eliminating duplicate/drifting copies.
5. Confirm this phase changes no tier feature entitlement other than photo/report counts and the new vocabulary/watermark/pack config — existing subscription features, report-history limits, white-label/SSO entitlements, etc. are explicitly unrelated to this request and remain untouched.

**Data model / API implications:** None new; read-only consumption of existing config.

**Security / privacy:** Admin config endpoints require the existing `requireAdmin` gate; no new exposure.

**Files/components likely affected:** `frontend/src/pages/Pricing.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/Home.jsx` (known-stale section), `frontend/src/pages/AdminTierUpdate.jsx`, `backend/routes/sales.js` (admin config read/write if not already added by Phase 44).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phases 41, 44, 45; final photo-pack pricing confirmation before this phase's pricing-page copy goes live (buildable against placeholders earlier).

**Automated validation:** A regression test asserting pricing-page displayed limits equal `PlanConfig` values.

**Manual/runtime validation:** Visual check of pricing page and admin config UI against final confirmed values once available.

**Backward compatibility / regression:** None — display-layer only.

**Out of scope:** Any change to tier feature entitlements beyond photo/report counts and vocabulary/watermark config.

**Rollback/recovery:** Display-only; trivially revertible.

**Status:** ✅ **Complete** (2026-09-22), implemented in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`. Final client-confirmed pack pricing (Open Question #17) was resolved before this phase per the task instructions (photos_25=$4.99/photos_50=$7.99/photos_100=$12.99/photos_250=$24.99, USD, report-specific one-time), so this phase shipped against real confirmed values, not placeholders. New admin routes on `routes/sales.js` (GET/PUT `/admin/plan-config`, GET `/admin/plan-config/history`, POST `/admin/plan-config/legacy-rollback`, GET `/admin/photo-packs` read-only, GET `/admin/integration-status`) reuse the existing `requireAdmin` email-gate (a forged client-side admin claim is proven rejected), `recordAuditLog`, and a new `planConfigWriteLimiter`. A new public, unauthenticated `GET /payment/public-plan-config` (`routes/payment.js`) is the ONE sanitized read model the public pricing page (`Pricing.jsx`, via a new `usePublicPlanConfig` hook) actually renders from — same PlanConfig/catalogue Phase 44/45 enforcement already reads, never Price IDs/secrets. ## Phase 49 — Final Integration QA, Regression & Release Readiness

**Objective:** Validate all of Phases 40-48 together as one coherent system before any production activation.

**Reason this phase is required:** Change-request acceptance criteria; reconciled audit's explicit call for a combined validation pass rather than per-phase sign-off alone.

**Scope:** Testing and verification only — no new features.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Full backend + frontend automated test suites, lint, and production build — zero regressions against pre-existing counts.
2. Generated PDF/DOCX/HTML visual + textual inspection for a multi-room estimate report (TOC accuracy, no escaping artifacts, watermark correctness per tier/status, Sections 1-6/8-9 unchanged).
3. Authenticated manual flow: create report → address lookup/confirm → photo upload to/near limit → purchase add-on pack (Stripe test mode) → build estimate with AI pricing suggestions → edit/override → approve → export. **Acceptance wording is explicitly conditional, not "export clean" unconditionally: confirm the draft watermark is removed after authorized approval, and confirm the Starter-tier branding watermark behaves exactly per whichever central policy the client has confirmed by then (Open Question #16) — a report may legitimately still show a non-draft watermark and that is correct, not a bug, if the policy says so.**
4. Provider-failure scenarios: OpenAI down, Google/RealtyAPI down, Stripe webhook delayed — confirm graceful degradation, no blocked report creation.
5. Concurrency tests: simultaneous photo uploads at the limit boundary, simultaneous estimate edits.
6. Existing subscription/report/invoice/RepairEstimate regression pass — confirm the separate `RepairEstimate`/Invoice document flow is completely unaffected.
7. Secret/privacy scan (no keys introduced into git history, no PII over-logged).
8. Confirm zero unrelated changes to `mobile/` — diff review against this phase set's file list.
9. Deployment-readiness checklist (env vars documented, no auto-deploy performed).

### 2026-09-23 — Phase 49 session (requirements matrix, regression suite, security audit, live verification, 2 defects found and fixed)

Worked entirely in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`, branch `docs/web-estimating-phases`, HEAD unchanged throughout at `6a6df97`. Nothing staged, committed, pushed, merged, deployed, or opened as a PR at any point. No production Firebase/Stripe/RealtyAPI/AWS/Render access; no real payments; no billable external API calls.

**Requirements-to-verification matrix (Phases 40-48 only, per the client's scope correction):** built by independently re-verifying every named implementation file/route/test against the actual current code (not copying PHASES.md's own claims). Result: Phases 41/42/44/45/48 **Done**; Phase 40 **Done** (closed this session — see below); Phase 46 **Done-with-caveat** (closed this session for the safe-fallback path — see below; the literal Google widget interaction remains a disclosed, external-key-dependent gap); Phase 43 **external-blocked** (Firestore TTL policy enablement on `pricingSuggestionsCache` — a GCP console action, not code); Phase 47 **external-blocked**, code/contract complete, 2 external items remain (see below).

**Full regression suite, run fresh at the end of this session:** backend `npm test` — **1166/1166 passing** (1163 pre-session baseline + 3 net new: 1 Google-Maps test-isolation regression test, 2 RealtyAPI status regression tests — see defects below). Backend lint: **0 errors, 63 warnings** (unchanged baseline). Frontend `npm test` — **302/302 passing, 33/33 files** (0 regressions). Frontend lint: **0 errors, 14 warnings** (unchanged baseline). Frontend production build: succeeds cleanly (pre-existing >500kB `index` chunk advisory only, unrelated to this session). `git diff --check`: clean (only the two pre-existing, harmless LF→CRLF notices every phase in this worktree has shown). Secret-shaped-value scan across the full tracked diff and every new (`??`) backend/frontend file: clean — no real key/token/credential pattern found anywhere. `backend/.env`'s real local keys (`GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_KEY` etc.) were never modified, confirmed byte-length-identical before and after this entire session.

**Defect 1 found and fixed — Phase 46/48 test-isolation leak:** `backend/test/integration-status-service.test.js`'s `googleAddress: not_configured...` case was failing because `config/googlePlaces.js` calls `require('dotenv').config()` at module load; the test's `withEnv` helper deletes `GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_KEY` from `process.env` to simulate "unset," but dotenv only skips vars that already exist, so the delete made dotenv reload the key straight back from the real local `backend/.env` (which genuinely has one set) on the isolated re-require. This exact root cause and file were already predicted and explicitly left unfixed by two separate prior sessions (Phase 46's 2026-09-22 emulator entry, and Phase 47's 2026-09-22 live-validation entry), both of which correctly identified it as Phase-48 scope and out of bounds for themselves. Fix: `withEnv` now stubs `dotenv.config` to a no-op only for the duration of its isolated re-require, restored immediately after — zero production code changed. Added one regression test that pre-seeds an "ambient" key before calling `withEnv` to catch this exact class of leak in the future.

**Defect 2 found and fixed — Phase 47 status-reporting staleness (release-readiness risk):** `backend/services/integrationStatusService.js`'s `getRealtyApiStatus()` unconditionally returned `status: 'contract_pending'` regardless of env, with a comment stating the RealtyAPI transport "isn't implemented yet." That was true when originally written, but the 2026-09-22 live-validation session (see Phase 47's own entries above) confirmed the contract and fully implemented the transport (`realtyApiProvider.js`'s `lookupProperty`/`normalizePropertyResult`) — the comment and the hardcoded status were never updated to match. Impact: once the client activates the real key in Render (blocker #2 below), this admin release-readiness signal would have stayed pinned on `contract_pending` forever, silently making production activation invisible to anyone checking it. Fix: `getRealtyApiStatus()` now derives `status` from `realtyApi.isConfigured()` exactly the same way every sibling integration (OpenAI/Stripe/Google) already does (`envConfigured ? 'live_validation_pending' : 'not_configured'`) — never `'verified'`, and deliberately does NOT wait on the ToS confirmation (blocker #1), since that's a legal/business gate on *using* the integration, not a statement about whether the code itself is wired up. Also corrected the now-stale explanatory comments in `config/realtyApi.js` and `realtyApiProvider.js` (comment-only) and a pre-existing test bug (`REALTY_API_ENABLED`, which is never read, replaced with the actually-read `PROPERTY_INTELLIGENCE_ENABLED`). 3 test cases added/replaced. Confirmed live end-to-end this session through a real HTTP call to `/sales/admin/integration-status` against the real (currently unconfigured, key blanked) local state: returns `{"envConfigured":false,"status":"not_configured"}`, correctly not the old hardcoded value.

**Phase 40 — live HTTP verification (closes this phase's remaining gap):** booted local Firestore + Auth + Storage emulators + the real backend against them, then exercised the real `POST /:id/export` + `GET /:id/download` endpoints (real PDF generation, real watermark overlay, real Storage round-trip, real byte-level PDF text extraction of the downloaded file — not mocks). All 4 scenarios passed: a draft report (any tier) gets the DRAFT mark and a `_DRAFT` filename; a reviewed/finalized report on a `watermark:true` tier (Starter) gets the branding mark, never the draft mark; a reviewed/finalized report on a `watermark:false` tier (Professional) is clean, neither mark present; a Starter-tier attempt at a DOCX export is correctly rejected `403 EXPORT_FORMAT_NOT_ALLOWED` (Golden Rule #4, live-confirmed). No defect found; no new production code or test needed (existing `watermark-policy.test.js` unit coverage plus this live-stack confirmation together close the gap this phase's own Status line had flagged).

**Phase 46 — safe-fallback path verified live by the client (2026-09-23), literal widget interaction remains disclosed-not-done:** with both `GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_KEY` (backend) and `VITE_GOOGLE_MAPS_BROWSER_KEY` (frontend) explicitly forced blank — confirmed via `GET /reports/property-lookup/config` returning `serverNormalizationConfigured:false` — the client manually walked the real running local stack (real backend + real frontend + real Firestore/Auth/Storage emulators, one disposable emulator-only login) and confirmed: the property-address field renders as an ordinary manual text input (no combobox, no suggestions, no spinner, no blocking error); a manually-typed address (`1425 Maple Street, Austin, TX 78701`) was preserved through the full 5-step wizard, the review screen, and the persisted report record (My Reports → Report Details showed the identical address). Session logs (`backend`/`frontend` dev-server output, captured for this entire session) were independently grepped for `googleapis`/Google-host activity: zero matches. The report's `analysis: Failed` status observed during this check was independently attributed (by both the client and a log check) to `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`WATSONX_API_KEY` being unset in this isolated stack — unrelated to the Phase 46 fallback path, not a defect. **What this does NOT close:** the literal interactive Google Places autocomplete widget (real suggestions dropdown, `PropertyProfileReview.jsx`'s edit/confirm/reject UI) was not exercised, because `AddressAutocompleteField.jsx` has no offline/stub mode (unlike Phase 47's swappable provider registry) — seeing it requires an actual working, client-supplied Google Maps browser key, which was explicitly not provided this session. This is the same disclosed gap every prior Phase 46 session already carried, now narrowed specifically to "the safe-fallback path is proven; the live-key widget path still needs a real key."
